# Engineering Guidelines

This document is the source of truth for how code in this repo is organized, named, written, and shipped. It applies to both the ongoing refactor and all future development. When this doc and existing code disagree, this doc wins — and the code is a refactor candidate.

Stack: Next.js 16 (App Router) · React 19 · TypeScript (strict) · Supabase · Stripe · Anthropic SDK.

---

## 0. Philosophy

1. **Feature-first, not layer-first.** A `billing/` folder contains everything billing — UI, server logic, types, hooks — not `components/Billing.tsx` separated from `lib/billing.ts` separated from `types/billing.ts`. A new engineer should be able to delete a feature by deleting one folder.
2. **Files end, not grow.** A file has a single clear purpose. When two purposes show up, split immediately — don't wait for 500 lines.
3. **Boring code wins.** Prefer one obvious way to do a thing. No clever patterns, no premature abstractions, no "future-proof" hooks that aren't needed yet.
4. **The type system is the API.** Strict TypeScript, no `any`, no `@ts-ignore`. Types live next to the feature that owns them.
5. **No dead code and no decorative comments.** If it's commented out, delete it (git remembers). If a comment explains what the code does, rename variables instead.

### Known debt

See [docs/REFACTOR-FINDINGS.md](REFACTOR-FINDINGS.md) for the current list of open findings (`F-NNN` ids) — pruned 2026-07-17 to open-only (resolved entries live in that file's git history). At a glance: files still over the 500-line cap (F-093, §2 table below — **the set is down to FIVE as of 2026-08-08, all five exempt-and-listed, and every file that left did so by a real split**), deliberate scale deferrals (ontology snapshot F-026, chat pagination F-027), and the CAS token design smell (F-038). Lint debt is ZERO — keep `npx eslint` at 0 errors; the invariant test suites (root `npx vitest run` + `packages/mcp-server` `npx vitest run`) are part of definition-of-done for MCP/tool and service changes. **RE-MEASURED 2026-08-08 after the split + fix wave: `npx eslint src packages apps` = 0 errors AND 0 WARNINGS, `dopl-desktop-app` `npx eslint .` = clean.** ⚠ The baseline this line carried is retired: it said "1 baseline warning, `proxy.ts:143`" (and REFACTOR-FINDINGS' gate said two, adding `use-boot-state.ts`) — there are none. Both trees are at a true zero, which means **the next warning to appear is a NEW one and there is no baseline to hide it in**. Previously: **it was NOT zero for part of the 2026-07-31 session** — `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-ops-write.ts` sat at **503** mid-wave (an over-cap `max-lines` ERROR, recorded in `channel-ops-open.ts`'s own header) and `dopl-desktop-app/main/realtime.js` was pushed past the cap by the `channel_agents` UPDATE binding (recorded in `realtime-agents.js`'s header). Both were split back under before the wave ended. Record it that way rather than only recording the green end state: the claim on this line is what a later agent trusts instead of running the command, so a session that breaks it and fixes it should say so, and a session that breaks it and stops should not be able to leave this sentence standing.

TanStack Query is now the server-state layer (§7) and every feature's client data hooks are on it — the legacy `useApiGet` / per-feature `useFetch` copies are gone. Don't reintroduce `useEffect + fetch + useState` for mount-time GETs; mutations in event handlers use `apiRequest` (plus a `queryClient.setQueryData`/`invalidateQueries` when a cached list must reflect the change).

## 1. Project Structure

```
setup-intelligence-engine/
├── apps/                          # npm workspace — shipped clients that are NOT the Next app
│   └── desktop-ui/                # Vite + React SPA bundled into the Electron app (Phase 2 of
│                                  # docs/DESKTOP-MIGRATION-PLAN.md). Builds to
│                                  # dopl-desktop-app/renderer/app/. Its OWN rules live in
│                                  # apps/desktop-ui/CONVENTIONS.md (one way to fetch, one way to
│                                  # add a route, no fetch/next-*/hardcoded colors).
├── docs/                          # This file, ADRs, runbooks
├── packages/                      # Internal workspace libs (not published to npm)
│   ├── chrome-extension/          # Browser extension (webpack build)

├── public/                        # Static assets
├── scripts/                       # One-off ops scripts (tsx-run)
├── supabase/
│   └── migrations/                # SQL migrations (source of truth for schema)
├── src/
│   ├── app/                       # Next.js App Router (routes + route handlers only)
│   │   ├── api/                   # Route handlers — thin, delegate to features/
│   │   └── ...                    # One folder per route
│   ├── features/                  # Feature modules (see §3)
│   │   ├── analytics/             # System + conversion event loggers
│   │   ├── billing/               # Stripe, subscriptions, access gates
│   │   ├── builder/               # Composite-solution builder UI
│   │   ├── channels/              # Cross-user agent-collab rooms (service-role writes)
│   │   ├── chat/                  # Chat panel + tool handlers
│   │   ├── clusters/              # ⛔ RETIRED 2026-08-07 (§7) — workflow clusters. On disk, unreachable
│   │   ├── community/             # Publishing / forking / gallery
│   │   ├── entries/               # Entry rows + search + retrieval + saved
│   │   ├── ingestion/             # Pipeline + skeleton + extractors
│   │   ├── marketing/             # Landing page components
│   │   ├── onboarding/            # First-run flow
│   │   └── workflows/             # ⛔ RETIRED 2026-08-07 (§7) — on disk, unrouted, no MCP tool
│   ├── shared/                    # Cross-feature primitives only
│   │   ├── ui/                    # shadcn primitives (Button, Dialog, etc.)
│   │   ├── design/                # Higher-level design components (MarkdownMessage, Orb, ...)
│   │   ├── layout/                # Shells + headers + sidebars
│   │   │   └── app-shell/         # NEW-DESIGN chrome: AppShell (workspace rail + sidebar + titlebar) + AppPanel (white panel with the light token scope). Mounted once by src/app/[workspaceSlug]/(app)/layout.tsx AND by [canvasSlug]/layout.tsx; every workspace page renders inside it. The canvas portals to <body> at z-[31], inset to the shell's white-panel rect via the --app-panel-* vars in globals.css (single-source geometry). Only non-workspace routes keep the legacy layout-shell chrome. Dark/light toggle removed — use-theme is a fixed-dark shim. Its NAV rows are the sidebar's source of truth (app-sidebar-core.tsx) — canvas/workflows/configuration left them 2026-08-07 (§7). ⛔ The old "clusters are workflows" model here (cluster-info + node panel types, canvas_edges connectors, dock=attach sync, dopl_cluster exposing the workflow graph, docs/WORKFLOW-BUILDER-PLAN.md) is DEAD on both counts: the panel canvas was torn down 2026-07-16 and workflows/clusters were retired 2026-08-07.
│   │   ├── lib/                   # Pure utilities (ai, github, slug, utils, http-error)
│   │   ├── prompts/               # Claude prompt templates
│   │   ├── hooks/                 # Generic hooks
│   │   ├── api/                   # parse-json, error-handler (shared route helpers)
│   │   ├── auth/                  # Route wrappers (withUserAuth, withMcpAccess, withWorkspaceAuth)
│   │   ├── supabase/              # Supabase client factories (admin/browser/server)
│   │   └── types/                 # Truly shared types (ApiError, Result)
│   ├── config/                    # Environment, flags, constants
│   ├── types/                     # Residual top-level types (api.ts, entry.ts, ...)
│   ├── middleware.ts
│   └── proxy.ts
├── CLAUDE.md                      # Pointer to this doc
├── eslint.config.mjs
├── next.config.ts
├── package.json
└── tsconfig.json
```

**Rule of thumb:** if a new thing is used by more than one feature, it goes in `shared/`. If it's used by exactly one feature, it goes inside that feature. Never create a `lib/` or `components/` tree at the top of `src/`.

---

## 2. File Size & Splitting

**Hard cap: 500 lines. No exceptions for new or edited files.**

| Threshold | Action |
|-----------|--------|
| **≤ 300 lines** | Target. No action needed. |
| **300–500 lines** | Soft cap. Review for split opportunities during the next edit. |
| **> 500 lines** | **Violation.** Any edit to a file over 500 lines must either (a) split the file in the same PR, or (b) the edit must *reduce* the line count. New files may never be created over 500 lines. |

**Exceptions (file may exceed 500 lines with justification at the top of the file):**
- Auto-generated code (Supabase types, OpenAPI clients).
- Dense type-only files where a split would fragment a cohesive domain model.
- Pure data/config tables (cluster-geometry constants, country lists).
- Single-function switch reducers where the switch is one coherent state machine — splitting by action type fragments the state transitions across files and makes the reducer harder to reason about.

**When you see a large file, split by:**
1. **Responsibility** — one file per "reason to change" (reducer vs. persistence vs. selectors).
2. **Layer** — handler vs. validator vs. service vs. data-access.
3. **Sub-feature** — if the feature has natural seams (per-platform extractors, per-tool handlers), give each its own file.

### Known files that exceed 500 lines

These are allowed under the exceptions above OR scheduled for a future split. If you touch one, either shrink it or split it in the same PR.

Tracked as **F-093** (this said F-041 until 2026-08-11; F-041 was deleted on 2026-07-31 *as superseded by F-093*, so the id this table pointed at had had no entry behind it for six weeks). 2026-07-20: the three consolidated MCP tool files (`tools/knowledge.ts`, `tools/workflow.ts`, `tools/ontology.ts`) were split under the cap and removed from this table. Pattern for these `op`-dispatched tools: the original file stays the thin **registrar** (tool schemas + `op` routing) and delegates each op to a sibling module — `<tool>-ops-read.ts` / `<tool>-ops-write.ts` (+ `<tool>-ops-admin.ts` for destructive soft-deletes), with cross-cutting resolvers/renderers/error-mappers in `<tool>-shared.ts` / `<tool>-render.ts`. Tool names, op enums, schemas, and error mapping are unchanged. Keep the registrar thin when adding ops — new op handlers go in the sibling module, not the registrar.

Split on that seam into `membership-admin.ts` (**209** — `updateMemberRole`, `removeMember`, `countActiveOwners`), which is deliberately **not** the seam F-041 scoped ("extract the accept/join sub-flows"). The original keeps the two-name **re-export** every caller already imports, so no importer moved — the same barrel shape `teams/server/repository.ts` kept through its own split. **✅ Its `eslint.config.mjs` exemption was DELETED** (removed, not moved — the `plans-billing.tsx` precedent, fourth time). **The transferable observation: a split scheduled on a line count waits indefinitely; a split scheduled by a new reason-to-change lands the same day it arrives.** |

The exemption list (`eslint.config.mjs` › the sibling block whose rule is `"max-lines": "off"`) is the backlog above and carries its own instruction: **DO NOT ADD TO IT — split the file instead.** `dopl-desktop-app/**` and `scripts/**` are outside this config's scope (`eslint.config.mjs` › `globalIgnores`); the desktop has its own `dopl-desktop-app/eslint.config.js` with the same rule at the same severity and **no exemptions at all**.

**The desktop tree still has a CLUSTER SITTING ON THE CAP WITH ZERO HEADROOM. Eight files inside eleven lines of the cap, none of them previously named here. **`test/ui-sync.test.mjs` LEFT this list: it is 367**, having been split into `test/ui-sync.test.mjs` + `test/ui-sync-tables.test.mjs` in `0a3b007`. **⚠ AND THE FOLLOW-ON CLAIM ABOUT THE SPLIT HALF WAS WRONG. A file created BY a split is not exempt from being measured, and neither is a sentence written about one. **`test/classify.test.mjs` was another of them and was SPLIT** in the F-146 pass, on the seam that was already there: the LEGACY-THREAD cases turn on a module-scope registry this machine writes (so each already built its own scope) rather than on a message's own fields, so they moved to `test/classify-legacy-threads.test.mjs` and the shared harness — source extraction, fixtures, the fresh-scope `build()` — became `test/_classify-harness.mjs`, on the `_reducer-block.mjs` precedent. Same 31 tests. `test/operator-typed-request.test.mjs` was also at 500 and fell to 476 when F-140 rewrote it.

**Any edit to one of these is a split.** Do not repeat the pattern of trimming a comment to buy back the line you need — that is how eight files converged on the same two numbers. (This document did it too: correcting `ui-sync.js`'s stale header in the F-146 pass pushed it to 508, and the correction had to be compressed three times to fit. **A file at 500 cannot absorb a COMMENT** — that is a real cost of letting a file sit on the cap, and it is why "it's only a doc fix" is not a reason to skip the lint.)

**WHAT LEAVING THE CLUSTER ACTUALLY LOOKS LIKE — AND WHAT COMING BACK LOOKS LIKE.** `main/session-reducer.js` 500 → 496 and `main/session-engine.js` 499 → 500 by real extraction (`session-state.js`, `session-shell.js`); `main/targeting.js` 493 → 395 because `legacy-threads.js` was lifted out whole; `main/realtime.js` 486 → 421; `main/trigger.js` 498 → 394; `main/session-profiles.js` 500 → 499 across two waves of extraction (`session-grant-keys.js`, `mcp-tool-names.js`). Every one of those moved a RESPONSIBILITY rather than trimming a paragraph. **And every one of them drifted back up afterwards — re-measured 2026-08-08, and the drift is worse than this note last claimed:** targeting 337 → 395 → **424**, realtime 409 → **421**, trigger 394 → **439**, reducer 423 → 496 → **500 (back at the cap it left)**, engine 471 → **500**, session-profiles → **500**. Three of the six are now at the cap and none of the six is where this note last recorded it. **An extraction buys headroom; it does not buy a habit.** That is the argument for measuring instead of remembering, and the reason none of these numbers should be copied forward without a `wc -l`. **`main/channel-agents.js` used to head this list at ~496, "the file with the least room in the tree" — it is DELETED** (channels rollback §1, F-141), and so are `main/session-greeting.js` and `main/realtime-agents.js`, both of which this block previously cited as the extractions that bought other files their headroom. A tree with no headroom does not stay under the cap by discipline; it stays under by splitting on every wave.

**This table is now a MEASUREMENT, not a pointer — as of the 2026-07-31 remeasure above.** It was a 2026-07-17 remeasure that only got corrected when a listed file was edited, which is how `server.ts` came to read 612 against 915 on disk. It will drift again the same way. **F-093 carries the re-derived backlog and the `eslint.config.mjs` exemption list that enforces it**; the exemption list and this table were reconciled on 2026-07-31 and agreed exactly through 2026-08-05. They disagreed on 2026-08-07 (F-153) — thirteen exemptions against eleven over-cap files — and the three surplus exemptions were deleted the same day. **RE-VERIFIED 2026-08-08 by measurement: TEN exemptions, TEN over-cap files, identical sets.** That was the SAFE direction to disagree in; the dangerous one showed up in the same wave when `tools/parity.test.ts` went over cap with no exemption and turned the lint red until it was split. If they disagree in any OTHER way, the **eslint list is the one with teeth** — believe it, then fix this table.

## 3. Feature Module Anatomy

Every feature in `src/features/<name>/` follows this shape. Not every feature needs every folder — create them only when there's content.

```
src/features/<name>/
├── components/                    # React components for this feature
│   ├── <feature>-<part>.tsx
│   └── index.ts                   # Barrel ONLY for external consumers
├── hooks/                         # Feature-specific hooks (use-*.ts)
├── server/                        # Server-only code (imports server deps)
│   ├── service.ts                 # Main business-logic service
│   ├── repository.ts              # DB reads/writes (Supabase calls)
│   └── dto.ts                     # snake_case ↔ camelCase mappers
├── types.ts                       # Feature-owned types (camelCase domain)
├── schema.ts                      # Zod schemas for validation
├── constants.ts                   # Feature constants
└── README.md                      # Optional: "what is this feature, what's the data flow"
```

### Rules

- **No sideways imports between features.** `features/chat` imports from `features/canvas` → NO. If both need the same thing, it goes in `shared/`.
- **`server/` folders never run in the browser.** Use `import "server-only"` at the top of `server/service.ts` and `server/repository.ts`.
- **Barrel files (`index.ts`) are the public API of a feature.** External consumers import `from "@/features/chat"` — not deep paths. Internal files import each other by relative path.
- **One service per feature.** If a feature has two services (e.g., current `clusters/service.ts` + `community/service.ts`), merge or clarify the boundary (see §8).

---

## 4. Naming Conventions

These are already 99% consistent in this repo. Codifying them so they stay that way.

| What | Convention | Example |
|------|------------|---------|
| Files | `kebab-case` | `chat-panel.tsx`, `use-panel-ingestion.ts` |
| Directories | `kebab-case` | `knowledge/`, `canvas/` |
| React components (exported name) | `PascalCase` | `ChatPanel`, `CanvasStoreProvider` |
| Functions | `camelCase` | `extractWebContent`, `normalizeTag` |
| Hooks (file + fn) | `use-kebab-case.ts` exporting `useCamelCase` | `use-chat.ts` → `useChat()` |
| Constants (module-level) | `SCREAMING_SNAKE_CASE` | `MAX_LINK_DEPTH`, `CHUNK_SIZE` |
| Local consts | `camelCase` | `const now = Date.now()` |
| Types/interfaces | `PascalCase`, **no** `I`-prefix, **no** `Type`/`Interface` suffix | `Entry`, `IngestRequest` |
| Enums / union type names | `PascalCase` | `SourceType`, `PanelKind` |
| Redux-style actions | `SCREAMING_SNAKE_CASE`, `DOMAIN_VERB` | `PANEL_MOVE`, `CLUSTER_CREATE` |
| API route segments | `kebab-case` | `/api/workspaces/[workspaceSlug]/overview-counts` |
| Dynamic route params | `camelCase` in brackets | `[chatId]`, `[panelId]` |
| DB tables | `snake_case`, plural | `canvas_panels`, `workflow_steps` |
| DB columns | `snake_case` | `entry_id`, `created_at` |
| Env vars | `SCREAMING_SNAKE_CASE`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_` prefix for client | `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` |
| Booleans | `is*`, `has*`, `should*`, `can*` | `isPending`, `hasAccess` |
| Event handlers | `on*` (prop), `handle*` (internal) | `onClick={handleSubmit}` |
| Async functions | verb-first; no `Async` suffix | `fetchEntries()`, not `fetchEntriesAsync()` |

### Actions (Redux-style reducers)

⛔ **RETIRED as a live example (2026-08-07).** The `canvas-store` this rule was written for went with the 2026-07-16 canvas teardown, and canvas/workflows/configuration are retired entirely (see §7). There is no such store in the tree today. The *convention* stands for any future reducer: action types are `SCREAMING_SNAKE_CASE` and `DOMAIN_VERB` (`PANEL_MOVE`, `CLUSTER_CREATE`) — the legacy `VERB_DOMAIN` names (`MOVE_PANEL`, `CREATE_CLUSTER`) are not a pattern to copy. When touching a reducer, normalize nearby legacy names in the same PR if the diff stays reasonable.

### Known naming inconsistency

`packages/chrome-extension/src/panel/components/*.tsx` uses `PascalCase` filenames (`EntryCard.tsx`, `ClusterBadge.tsx`). The main app uses `kebab-case` (`entry-card.tsx`). Outstanding — rename when the extension next gets touched.

## 5. Code Organization Within a File

Standard order for any `.ts` / `.tsx` file:

1. `"use client"` / `import "server-only"` directive
2. Type imports (`import type { ... }`)
3. Runtime imports (external packages, then internal `@/` aliases, then relative `./`)
4. Constants (`const MAX_FOO = 10`)
5. Types defined in this file
6. Zod schemas
7. Helper functions (unexported)
8. Main exported function / component
9. Default export if any (avoid default exports except for Next.js page/layout/route files, which require them)

### Import ordering

```ts
// 1. Server-only / use-client directive
import "server-only";

// 2. External (alphabetized)
import { NextResponse } from "next/server";
import { z } from "zod";

// 3. @/ absolute (alphabetized by path)
import { withUserAuth } from "@/shared/auth/with-auth";
import { createServerClient } from "@/shared/supabase/server";
import type { Entry } from "@/features/entries/types";

// 4. Relative
import { mapEntryRow } from "./dto";
import { MAX_CHUNKS } from "./constants";
```

Enforced via ESLint `import/order` (see Appendix A).

### Comments

Default: **no comments**. Only add one when the *why* is non-obvious:
- Hidden constraint ("Supabase RLS requires this to run as service role")
- Workaround for a specific bug ("Stripe returns `null` here instead of omitting — see issue-123")
- Non-obvious invariant

Never write comments that describe what the code does. Rename the variable.

---

## 6. React Patterns

### Component boundaries

A component should:
- Render **one thing** (a card, a form, a panel).
- Have a clear prop contract — if it takes more than ~7 props, consider splitting.
- Avoid mixing data-fetching, business logic, and presentation. Extract to hooks.

### When to extract

Extract a sub-component when:
- The JSX exceeds ~80 lines.
- There's logic that's only used for one sub-part of the UI.
- You find yourself writing `const FooInner = ...` local definitions — promote to a sibling file.

Do **not** extract just for "reusability" if there's one caller. Extract when there are two.

### Client vs. server components

- **Default to server components.** Only add `"use client"` when the component needs: state, effects, event handlers, browser APIs, or hooks that require them.
- **Push `"use client"` as low as possible.** A page can be a server component that renders a small interactive island.
- **Never** put Supabase service-role keys or Anthropic API keys behind `"use client"`.

### Hooks

- One hook per file: `src/features/<name>/hooks/use-<name>.ts`.
- Hook name matches file name (`use-chat.ts` exports `useChat`).
- A hook that mutates server state should return `{ data, isLoading, error, mutate }` or similar consistent shape.
- No "mega-hooks." If your hook returns more than ~8 things, split.

### Props

- Prefer inline type (`function Foo({ a, b }: { a: string; b: number })`) for components with few props.
- Named props type (`type FooProps = { ... }`) when more than ~4 props or when exported.
- No `React.FC<Props>`. Use function declaration.

---

## 7. State Management

This repo has three layers of state. Keep them separate.

| Layer | Tool | What lives here |
|-------|------|-----------------|
| **Server state** | TanStack Query (adopted 2026-07; provider in `src/shared/api/query-provider.tsx`) | Anything that comes from Supabase or an API |
| **Local UI state** | `useState` / `useReducer` | Form values, open/closed, hover, graph selection |

New client data code uses `useApiQuery` (`src/shared/hooks/use-api-query.ts`) over `apiRequest` (`src/shared/api/api-client.ts` — the single typed fetch wrapper: workspace header, error envelope, 204s). The legacy per-feature `useFetch`/`request<T>` copies are migration targets, feature by feature — do not add new call sites to them. Realtime refetch signals go through `useWorkspaceTablesRealtime` (`src/shared/realtime/`). List endpoints paginate with `Paginated<T>` (`src/shared/types/paginated.ts`) + `parsePageParams` (`src/shared/api/pagination.ts`).

### Writes: `useApiMutation`, and the three files under it (2026-08-07, F-159 / roadmap P0-1)

**THE READ HOOK NOW HAS ITS OTHER HALF, and new write code uses it.** Until 2026-08-07 this repo contained zero `useMutation`, zero `onMutate` and zero rollback: ~86 write sites hand-rolled `setBusy(true); await api(); await refetch()`, which is why the fastest thing a click could produce was a dimmed button for the length of two serial network hops. The pattern was written down (`apps/desktop-ui/CONVENTIONS.md`) and never implemented.

- **`src/shared/api/query-keys.ts`** — `apiQueryKey(path, {workspaceId, query})` is the EXACT tuple `use-api-query-core.ts:54` registers; `apiPathKey(path)` is the one-element PREFIX key, and `apiResource(path)` hands back both. **Writes patch by the prefix**, because TanStack matches keys per array element and a writer does not know which query-param variants a reader mounted (the channel list is cached twice — with and without `?include=archived`). Per-element matching is also why `/api/channels` can never patch `/api/channels/consent`. **Never retype a key array at a call site**: a key that differs by one element is a silent no-op — the write lands in an entry no observer is subscribed to, nothing renders, nothing fails.
- **`src/shared/hooks/use-api-mutation.ts`** — transport-injected exactly like `use-api-query-core` (`useApiMutationWith(request, config)`; `useApiMutation` is the web binding, and a FEATURE may inject its own client — channels passes `channelRequest` so every write still throws `ChannelApiError`). Config: `request(draft)` → the HTTP call; `optimistic(draft)` → cache patches applied in `onMutate` with an automatic snapshot and a verbatim `onError` restore; `reconcile(data, draft)` → patches from the server's OWN answer; `invalidate(draft)` on settle; `settleWith(gate)`; `pending`. `patchCache<TCache>(key, update)` builds a typed patch; **`coldKeys(client, keys)` filters a key list down to the entries that still hold no data** — rule 1's one exception, owned here rather than copied into features (2026-08-10, F-178). **Patches operate on the RAW response body** (`{messages: [...]}`), because `useApiQuery` stores what the endpoint returned and applies `select` on read.
- **`src/shared/ui/pending.ts`** — `PENDING_ROW` / `pendingRow(pending, className)` / `PendingRow`: the `data-pending` recipe that makes an optimistic row read as provisional (dimmed + `pointer-events-none`, token utilities only — no new CSS, nothing to mirror into the SPA's hand-copied `kit.css`).

**Four rules that are not negotiable, each of which cost a real bug to learn:**

1. **Invalidation is EXPLICIT.** Auto-invalidating every patched key re-downloads the cache the mutation just reconciled and undoes the whole point (the flagship send exists to stop re-downloading a 200-message page). List only the caches this write could NOT compute — ordering, `lastMessageAt`, a server-written lifecycle echo. **The one exception is the COLD CACHE (2026-08-08).** `optimistic` and `reconcile` both decline on an entry with no data (there is no list to patch a row into), so on a cold start or inside the IndexedDB restore window the write lands server-side and never reaches the screen — invisibly, because the surface renders from its SSR/initial-props fallback. A write whose reconcile is its only path to the screen therefore names its keys through a settle-time filter that keeps only the ones STILL holding no data: **`coldKeys(client, keys)`, exported from `src/shared/hooks/use-api-mutation.ts` (promoted there 2026-08-10, F-178)**. The check runs in `onSettled`, i.e. after `reconcile`, so "still empty" is the decline itself. Warm caches re-download nothing, which is what keeps this inside rule 1. **There is ONE implementation and it is on the layer** — chats (`use-chat-writes.ts`) and the channels SEND (`use-thread-writes.ts`) both import it; the two local copies they used to keep, `ifCold` and a private `coldKeys`, are deleted, and `ifCold` no longer exists in `src/`. It is named for what it RETURNS — the subset of `keys` that is cold — because the result is spread into an `invalidate` list rather than branched on. **The channels SEND is the one to read before adding a third caller** — it applies the filter to the transcript key only and leaves the channel list unconditional, and it is the case where the cold path and rule 1 pull hardest in opposite directions, because the read it would refetch is a 200-message page and `keepPreviousData` makes "sent into a channel whose transcript has not loaded" a one-gesture state rather than a cold-start-only one. **Rule 4 is not a substitute for this rule.** `openThread` / `threadOp` name the transcript key UNCONDITIONALLY and are right to: their opening message and lifecycle echo are written server-side under keys the client cannot derive, so for them the refetch is the only path to the screen warm or cold. Which of the two shapes a write takes is decided by whether its OWN response can reconcile the surface, never by symmetry with the write next to it. **Also: a per-item key cannot be reached by a prefix.** `apiResource(path).all` is `[path]` and TanStack matches per ARRAY ELEMENT, so `[…/members]` invalidates no `[…/members/<id>/access]` entry — a write that changes what a server-resolved per-member pane says must NAME each id, captured at submit (see `GrantDraft.memberIds` / `TeamDeleteDraft.memberIds`).
2. **Cancel BEFORE patching, and only queries that HAVE data.** TanStack's cancel reverts a query to its pre-fetch state, so cancelling second restores the very data the write just replaced. And a query on its FIRST load has nothing for the write to clobber — cancelling it strands the surface empty with nothing scheduled to fill it.
3. **`settleWith` + `useRefetchGate`, never a hand-rolled busy counter.** `createRefetchCoordinator` (`src/shared/realtime/refetch-coordinator.ts`) already stops a realtime event mid-write from refetching over an unsent local change; the gate owns the in-flight count and releases the deferred refetch in `onSettled`, so a write that THROWS cannot strand it.
4. **Key every optimistic write by the id captured AT SUBMIT.** The per-channel reads use `keepPreviousData`, so a channel switch renders the previous channel's data; an optimistic write that re-read the current selection would land in the wrong channel's cache. **AND A READ THAT FEEDS A WRITE'S PAYLOAD MUST EXPOSE `isPlaceholderData` (2026-08-08, F-178).** Capturing the id at submit protects the KEY; it does nothing for a field the draft *derives from placeholder data*. `useChannelMembers` was `keepPreviousData` with no `stale` flag, and the composer's request mode resolves a DM's addressee out of that roster — so a request sent in the first frames after a switch addressed the previous channel's peer, under the correct channel id, and came back 400 `ChannelAddresseeNotMemberError` with the optimistic row already painted. Both per-channel reads now return `stale` (`use-channel-messages`, `use-channel-members`), and the gate is in the pure decision layer (`lib/composer-mode.ts`: `rosterStale` → a blocked reason), so it disables Send and says why rather than no-opping.

**Idempotency is the client's job now.** `channel_messages.client_msg_id` (unique per channel) and the `channel_tasks` equivalent have always been honoured server-side and no client ever sent one. Every optimistic write mints one, so a retry after a rolled-back failure returns the FIRST attempt's stored row instead of double-posting — and the cache rules collapse it either way (`src/features/channels/lib/optimistic-cache.ts`).

**Four more rules, learned the same way, from the wave that finished the adoption (2026-08-08):**

5. **MERGE, NEVER REPLACE, when the response is narrower than the cache.** A reconcile that assigns the server's row over the cached one silently drops every field the write endpoint does not return. The chats conversion found this the hard way: the list read carries fields the PATCH response has no reason to echo, so the reconciled row rendered a row with holes in it. Patch field-by-field from the response and leave the rest of the cached row alone. The corollary is that a write's `reconcile` is not automatically safe just because the server answered — the question is always *what did it answer WITH*.
6. **A feature's READS must be on `useApiQuery` before its WRITES adopt this layer.** There is nothing to patch otherwise, and the failure is silent rather than loud: the write lands, the cache entry it patches has no subscriber, and the surface keeps rendering from whatever `useState` copy it was already holding. Converting writes first produces a feature that looks converted and behaves exactly as it did before. Chats had to delete a `useState` copy of query data as part of its conversion for precisely this reason.
7. **`CREATE_RESOLVE` is the pattern for a server-minted id with instant render.** When the row's identity comes from the server but the user must see it NOW, render a provisional row under a client-side id and resolve it to the server's id when the response lands. **The ontology creates use a reducer for this rather than `useApiMutation`, deliberately** — the board renders from `graphReducer`, not from a query cache, so there is no cache entry for `optimistic` to patch. The layer is for cache-backed surfaces; a reducer-backed surface implements the same three beats (provisional → resolve → rollback) in the reducer. **While a row is provisional it is PENDING for real**: its id is not addressable, and the realtime write gate is held so an echo of the create cannot arrive under an id nothing is keyed by yet.
8. **`pendingRow` on a CONTROL closes toggle races.** Applied to a row it is cosmetic; applied to a toggle, a segmented control or a role select it is the thing that stops the second click. A pure toggle that stays live through its own round trip is how two conflicting PATCHes get sent, and the operator lands on their FIRST pick with no error — the members and chats consoles both had this shape.

**The layer is now ADOPTED REPO-WIDE for the features named below; there is no remaining backlog for them.** The four families F-159 scoped are all converted (2026-08-08):

- **channels** — `use-thread-writes.ts`, `use-channel-preference-writes.ts`, `client/query-keys.ts`. The original worked example, and where the four hand-rolled `useState` override records were SUBSUMED rather than joined by a fifth idiom.
- **channels LIFECYCLE** — 5 writes, `use-channel-lifecycle-writes.ts`. **The refetch coordinator gate is REQUIRED on every one of them**, not optional: these are the writes a realtime doorbell most reliably races. (Channels audit C-27 is closed for this feature — the override maps that were silently doing the coordinator's job are gone, so the gate is now the only thing protecting them and has to be wired.)
- **chats** — 5 writes, all converted, the `useState` copy of query data deleted, reads re-keyed by path, and the double-submit paths (folder create on Enter, pin/unpin) closed.
- **members** — 13 writes converted, and F-045 closed with them: `useInvalidateBillingStatus` finally has callers (remove-member and approve-join), so the seat count no longer goes stale after a membership change.
- **ontology** — creates are optimistic via the reducer + `CREATE_RESOLVE`, per rule 7.

**What that leaves is not "more sites" but the shared-layer follow-ups the conversions surfaced — and as of 2026-08-10 there is exactly ONE left.** The duplicated cold-cache filter (rule 1) is **done**: `coldKeys` now lives in `use-api-mutation.ts` and both feature copies are gone. **Still open: the layer cannot express a PREDICATE invalidation**, which is what a write that changes every member's per-member pane actually needs (rule 1's per-item-key note is the same gap seen from the other side) — `invalidate` returns a list of KEYS, while TanStack's own `invalidateQueries` accepts a `predicate` this layer has no way to pass. See F-181, which now stands alone (its old instruction to land it alongside the `coldKeys` promotion is stale — that shipped without it). Also closed with the same wave: the pending CHAT bubble now dims (F-159's last site item). See F-159 and F-178.

### Canvas, Workflows & Configuration — RETIRED 2026-08-07, DELETED 2026-08-11

⛔ **STATEMENT OF RECORD. As of 2026-08-07 these three features were retired from EVERY user-facing AND agent-facing surface; as of 2026-08-11 all three are OFF DISK.** No user can reach them; no agent can see them; there is no code left to un-hide. Driven by [RETIREMENT-UNWIRING-PLAN.md](RETIREMENT-UNWIRING-PLAN.md) (decisions D1–D8, phases 1–7). Anything further down this section, or anywhere else in this document, that describes one of the three as a live surface is **history** — kept because the code it names is still on disk, not because a user can get to it.

**HIDE, DON'T DELETE WAS THE 2026-08-07 POSTURE. ALL THREE HAVE SINCE GRADUATED TO DELETE (2026-08-11), in three commits — configuration, then the canvas page, then workflows with clusters.** Nothing named in this section is a restore path any more. Everything below the three tombstones is the record of what the HIDE removed, kept because it is the archaeology of how the surface got here.

⛔ **CONFIGURATION IS GONE — DELETED 2026-08-11, not hidden.** `src/features/configuration/` and `apps/desktop-ui/src/pages/configuration/` are off disk entirely. It was the cheapest of the three to delete because it was 100% inert (no API, no fetch, no Supabase, no MCP, no tests, no migrations) and its page was its only importer. What remains under the name is deliberate and load-bearing: the `/{ws}/configuration` → `/get-started` 302 in `src/shared/lib/url/website-retirement.ts › APP_PAGES` (pinned by `src/proxy-retirement.test.ts`), and the deep-link fallthrough in `dopl-desktop-app/main/deep-link-target.js`, which opens workspace home for an unknown page. The two-pane guide-builder pattern it carried survives in `~/Desktop/dopl-artifacts/configuration` and in git history.

⛔ **THE CANVAS PAGE IS GONE — DELETED 2026-08-11, not hidden.** `apps/desktop-ui/src/pages/canvas/` (the `GraphView` seam and the `canvas2` alias) and the ontology graph subtree it was the sole importer of — `src/features/ontology/graph/` (`graph-view`, `graph-body`, `graph-node`, `graph-skeleton`, `derive`, `layout`, `edge-styles`, `types` and their four test files) — are off disk. **This deleted a VIEW, not data:** every row in `ontology_clusters` / `ontology_objects` / `ontology_memberships` / `ontology_relationships` is untouched and the kanban Ontology page renders them exactly as before, including the `layout` jsonb the graph used to write. `src/features/ontology/graph-state.ts` is NOT part of this — despite the name it is the ontology board's reducer, it lives at the feature root, and it stays. So do `components/cap-notice.tsx` and `components/delete-cluster-dialog.tsx` (OntologyView renders both) and all of `src/shared/graph/`. What remains under the name `canvas` is URL machinery and stays: `RETIRED_TOP_LEVEL` + `BILLING_INBOUND_PAGES` in `src/shared/lib/url/website-retirement.ts` and their proxy tests (Stripe `?billing=` returns land through them), `WEB_ONLY_ROOTS` in `dopl-desktop-app/main/deep-link-target.js`, and `RESERVED_WORKSPACE_SLUGS` in `src/config/index.ts`.

⛔ **WORKFLOWS AND CLUSTERS ARE GONE — DELETED 2026-08-11, not hidden.** The whole tree: `src/features/workflows/` (40 files), `src/features/clusters/`, `src/app/api/workflows/**` (10 routes), `src/app/api/clusters/**`, `apps/desktop-ui/src/pages/workflows/`, the five MCP tool modules, `packages/dopl-client/src/{workflows,client-workflows,clusters,client-clusters}.ts` and both of their chain links, `src/shared/lib/cluster-name.ts`, and `scripts/{smoke-workflows,port-workflows}.mts`. **Clusters rode with workflows because it was never anything else** — `ClusterRow` carried `workflow_count`/`workflow_names` and its service filtered team-visible workflows; the canvas page it was originally built for had been ontology-backed since 2026-07-16.

**The DB went in the same commit:** `supabase/migrations/20260811120000_drop_workflows_and_clusters.sql` drops all five `workflow_*` tables plus `clusters` and five assert/trigger functions. Whether it is APPLIED is deploy state — measure it (§12).

**TWO CROSS-FEATURE THINGS WENT WITH IT, and both are worth knowing about.** (1) `src/features/teams/server/invariant.ts` — the workflow↔KB invariant, whose entire subject was workflows: every team that could read a workflow had to be able to read every KB attached to it. Deleting it removed `TeamKbAccessConflictError`, the `TEAM_KB_ACCESS_CONFLICT` 409 (which had no other producer), the `autoGrant` retry flag through the schema/routes/mutation layer, and the `ConflictDialog` the 409 opened — dead the moment nothing could raise it. **A KB narrowing is now unchecked, because there is no dependent resource left to strand.** That also closes F-182 by deletion. (2) `workflow` left `TeamResourceType` and the `RESOURCE_TABLES` polymorphic map, so the map is four types over four tables.

**WHAT DELIBERATELY STAYS, each for a different reason.** `gating.ts › HIDDEN_TOOLS` — now EMPTY, kept as the hide-before-delete seam itself (INVARIANTS §10). `RETIRED_DOPL_TOOLS` in `dopl-desktop-app/main/tool-profiles.js` — all four deleted tool names, because a name dropped from a deny list becomes *unclassified*, which resolves to `gate`; `UNIVERSAL_HARD_DENY` stays 8 (§11). The `"workflow"` entry in BOTH `RETIRED_RESOURCE_TYPES` render filters (`features/teams/access-levels.ts` and `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/members-render.ts`) — the drop migration deliberately leaves `'workflow'` valid in the `team_resource_access.resource_type` CHECK, so a surviving row must still be filtered where the payload enters a surface. `"workflows"` in `website-retirement.ts › APP_PAGES` — the historical URL list, pinned by `proxy-retirement.test.ts`. And the `seed-content.test.ts` guard that no seeded skill, KB entry, ontology object or sample chat teaches workflows.

**THE TESTS WERE REWRITTEN DOWN, NEVER DELETED WHOLESALE (§14).** `retirement.test.ts` keeps its four-name `RETIRED` array — it stopped being a gate assertion and became a REGROWTH guard against those names coming back as tools or as routing prose. `delete-block.test.ts` pins `HIDDEN_TOOLS` as empty rather than dropping the check. `meta-gate.test.ts` re-pointed its suppression leg from `HIDDEN_TOOLS` (which can no longer suppress anything) to `READ_ONLY_BLOCKED_TOOLS`, because the same `isSuppressedTool` line reads both — mutation-verified, 2 reverts / 2 failures. `teams/server/repository-resources.test.ts` keeps the skill→workflows dispatch regression guard, which was never about workflows: it is about the map routing each type to its own table. `dopl-desktop-app/test/ui-sync-tables.test.mjs` replaced its un-published pin with a DROPPED pin, and had to build a bare-`DROP TABLE` discriminator to do it honestly — `PUB.dropped` would have passed vacuously, since it also matches the tail of an `ALTER PUBLICATION … DROP TABLE`.

- **Unrouted and un-navigated.** The five route rows are out of `WORKSPACE_PAGES` (`apps/desktop-ui/src/routes.tsx`), the three nav rows and their `NavSection` union members are out of `src/shared/layout/app-shell/app-sidebar-core.tsx` (the sidebar is SHARED `src/` code, not `apps/desktop-ui` — editing the route table alone leaves three rows pointing at "Not found"), and the hand copy of the page table in `dopl-desktop-app/main/deep-link-target.js` moved in the same change (`test/deep-link-target.test.mjs` re-reads `routes.tsx` at runtime and fails on drift). The page components are now referenced by nothing; they come back by re-adding a route row plus its nav row plus its deep-link entry — all three, or the feature is half-back.
- **`WORKSPACE_HOME_PATH` is `"overview"` (D4).** It was `"canvas"` with SIX funnels through it (index redirect, boot, workspace switch, workspace create, ⌘⇧H, auth change). Every "go home" resolves through that one constant — never hardcode a home path beside it, or a future repoint boots the app into a 404.
- **Copy, tour and seeds stopped teaching them.** Tour steps 2 (Canvas — it used to *navigate*) and 3 (Workflows) and the finish copy are gone, as are the workflow lines in the welcome popup, workspace-name step, create-workspace dialog, members role/invite copy, and marketing panel 05. Seeding (D5) no longer creates the "Workspace upkeep" workflow, the `walk-a-workflow` skill, the workflow-teaching Dopl Guide KB entries, or the WORKFLOW rubric in `onboarding/bootstrap-prompt.ts`; the ontology / knowledge / skills seeds are untouched. The Overview "Workflows" stat card is gone (the counts route still returns the number — a card is navigation, and there is nowhere to navigate).
- **`workflow` is no longer a grantable team resource (D7).** Out of the teams UI and out of the `dopl_members` access-matrix rows. Existing grant rows stay valid in the DB; nothing renders them.

**THE AGENT SURFACE IS 14 TOOLS, and the four that left never register (D1/D2).** ⚠ **Since 2026-08-11 they do not EXIST** — the paragraph below is how they were hidden, and the count is unchanged because 14 was already the visible surface. `dopl_workflow`, `dopl_workflow_admin`, `dopl_cluster` and `dopl_cluster_admin` were listed in `HIDDEN_TOOLS` (**`packages/mcp-server/src/gating.ts:60` since the 2026-08-08 split — it was `server.ts:391`; the constant moved, the gate's topology did not**), read by **`isSuppressedTool`** (`gating.ts:183`, called from `registrar.ts:162,299`), which returns true for a hidden tool and then for a `READ_ONLY_BLOCKED_TOOLS` name in a read-only session — same mechanism, same choke point. **(Corrected 2026-08-07: this used to say "checked at the top of the `registerTool` wrapper". The four gates were HOISTED OUT of that wrapper in the same wave, into `isSuppressedTool` + `opRefusal`, precisely because `registerMetaTool` registers straight onto the SDK server and so published two tools through none of them. That was inert — neither meta-tool is hidden, blocked, or carries an `op` — but inert was a property of today's two tools, not of the path. Two gates run at REGISTRATION, two per CALL.)** The 14-tool count and `dopl_map`'s `DOMAIN_COUNT = 3` were both re-verified against the source on 2026-08-07 and are correct. **Gate at the registrar, never at the route:** the MCP server reaches `/api/workflows/*` and `/api/clusters/*` over LOOPBACK HTTP through `DoplClient`, so gating those routes would have left the tool visible in `tools/list` and made every call a 500 (hence D3 — the routes stay open). Their registrars are still *called* in `createServer` on purpose, so the parity/scope suites keep checking the dormant schemas against their sources. Downstream, all in lockstep: `buildInstructions` no longer mentions canvas/workflows/clusters, `dopl_map` is 3 domains (`DOMAIN_COUNT`) and `dopl_search` 3 groups, the generated `SKILL.md` (`features/mcp-connect/skill-template.ts`) lost its "Following a workflow" section, and `dopl-desktop-app/main/tool-profiles.js` moved the four names off `DOPL_SAFE_TOOLS`/`DOPL_ADMIN_TOOLS` into `RETIRED_DOPL_TOOLS` — **still hard-denied**, because a name dropped from a deny list becomes *unclassified*, which resolves to `gate`, i.e. one operator click away from the tool the table says can never be opened. **Rebuild `packages/mcp-server/dist` after any of this.**

**DELETION IS APP-ONLY — agents cannot delete anything over MCP.** Same choke point, second mechanism, and **since 2026-08-07 it lives in its own module: `packages/mcp-server/src/delete-policy.ts`, NOT in `server.ts`** (which imports `DELETE_REFUSAL` + `isBlockedDeleteOp` from it). `DELETE_BLOCKED_OPS` enumerates today's delete ops per `_admin` tool verbatim, and `DELETE_OP_SHAPE` — `/^(delete|destroy|purge|trash|remove)(_|$)/` — fail-closes on any *future* `*_admin` op whose name reads as a deletion, so a new destructive op is refused even if nobody remembers the table. One `DELETE_REFUSAL` string, worded once, naming the app as the place it can be done. It runs first inside `opRefusal` — **deliberately ahead of the read-only write-scope gate, because it is unconditional and must not be reachable only after some other gate happens to let the call through** — and before workspace resolution and any client call, so a refused delete costs zero round trips and can never half-happen. This is what makes in-app hard delete safe — see **"DELETES ARE PERMANENT"** below for the product half.

**Realtime: the five `workflow_*` tables left the publication** (Phase 5 / D8, migration `20260807100000`) paired in the same release with the client-binding removal (`useWorkflowsRealtime` became an inert stub; `ui-sync.js` `SYNC_TABLES` binds 17, not 22) — see the **Realtime** bullet under "Realtime & new-workspace seeding" below for why publication changes and subscriber changes must never ship apart. That un-publish was REVERSIBLE (`ALTER PUBLICATION … ADD TABLE`); **the 2026-08-11 `DROP TABLE` is not.** `SYNC_TABLES` still binds 17 — the two changes touched different halves, which is exactly why `ui-sync-tables.test.mjs` asserts both.

- **Canvas was never its own feature after 2026-07-16.** The page rendered `GraphView` — a second view of **ontology**. Hiding it cost one view and no data, and deleting it on 2026-08-11 cost the same view again; the Ontology page is unaffected either way. **Configuration was 100% inert** — zero API/fetch/Supabase calls, fully mock-driven, zero MCP presence — which is why it is the one of the three that could be deleted outright (see the CONFIGURATION IS GONE block above).
- **Only ONE "cluster" concept exists now, and the ambiguity is what makes this worth stating.** **Workflow clusters** (`features/clusters`, table `clusters`, `dopl_cluster`) are DELETED. **Ontology clusters** (`ontology_clusters`, the `dopl_ontology` board, `OntologyCluster*` in `@dopl/client`) are live and always had zero workflow coupling. A `Cluster*` symbol in the client package or a `clusters` table reference in an old doc means the deleted one; read the prefix before assuming.
- ⚠ **"Before anyone ever deletes `features/workflows`: harvest `hooks/use-workflows.ts` first" — THAT DID NOT HAPPEN.** The tree was deleted on 2026-08-11 with the harvest outstanding (roadmap R7, now closed as unharvested). The shared mutation layer had already shipped independently, so nothing was blocked; the loss is a reference implementation, and it is recoverable from git history. **The reusable lesson: a "harvest before you delete" note in a doc is not a gate. If it matters, it is a test or it is a commit, not a sentence.**

#### History — what the surface was before retirement (2026-07-16 teardown, kept for archaeology)

The legacy free-form panel canvas (`features/canvas/`, its `/api/canvas/**` routes, the `[canvasSlug]` page, and the `canvas_panels`/`canvas_edges`/`canvas_state`/`canvases` tables) is **deleted** — that teardown is real and permanent, and is a different event from the 2026-08-07 retirement above. The "Canvas" tab then became the ontology graph view at `/[ws]/canvas` (`features/ontology/graph/`) — which was unrouted 2026-08-07 and deleted 2026-08-11, so that directory is gone too. `/canvas` survives only as the global redirect (Stripe `?billing=` return URLs depend on it — it forwards query params to `/{ws}/canvas`).

- **Shared graph substrate:** `src/shared/graph/` — generic `SceneNode<T>` + geometry types + the `EdgeLayer` SVG renderer (orthogonal edges, arrowheads, HTML label pills; edge styles injected per domain). Ontology passed `ONTOLOGY_EDGE_STYLES`; workflows passed their sequence/branch styles. **Since 2026-08-11 the RENDERING half of the substrate — `EdgeLayer`, `routeEdges`, `useNodeDrag`, `useGraphPositions` — has NO consumer at all**: ontology's went with the graph subtree, and workflows' went with the whole feature. **The SCHEMA half is live and ontology-owned** — `graphLayoutSchema` and `mergeStoredLayout` are on the ontology read/write path (`features/ontology/{schema,types}.ts`, `server/{repository,dto}.ts`, `client/api.ts`). Do not delete a piece of `src/shared/graph/` on the strength of the unused half, and do not keep the unused half on the strength of the schema half either — it is now genuinely orphaned code awaiting a decision, not shared substrate. Domain layouts were always per-feature; both graph-layout modules are gone.
- **Workflows are first-class step graphs** (dormant): `workflow_steps` + `workflow_step_edges` (edge `condition` = agent-readable branch guard; entry steps = indegree-0; no header concept). Server split: repository / graph.ts `composeWorkflow` (topo-ordered) / authoring-{graph,nodes,edges,refs,shared}. `dopl_workflow` kept its op surface plus a stateless `op='step'` walk read (paced context disclosure — one step's skills/knowledge/branches at a time; no run state in v1) right up until it stopped registering. `dopl_canvas` was retired earlier still.
- Workflow steps are **not** ontology objects — the free-plan object cap does not count them (still true of the dormant rows).

### MCP surface hardening (2026-07-18 audit-fix batch)

A swarm audit of the whole MCP surface drove a batch of fixes (tracked as F-042). Load-bearing outcomes future sessions must know:

- **Packs feature fully removed.** The `dopl_packs` tool, `/api/knowledge/packs/**`, `features/knowledge-packs/`, the `knowledge_packs`/`knowledge_pack_files` tables (drop migration `20260718000010`), the `@dopl/client` pack methods/types, and the `proxy.ts` pack-sync auth bypass are all gone. Don't reintroduce a `dopl_packs` reference.
- **Soft-delete parity.** ⛔ **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-07 — see "Deletes are permanent" below.** (Historical: `dopl_workflow` and `dopl_chats` deletes were made soft to mirror `dopl_kb`, each gaining a `deleted_at` column — migrations `20260718000001`, `20260718000002` — plus a `list_trash` read op and a `restore` write op.) What SURVIVES: **every** read still filters `deleted_at IS NULL`, because rows tombstoned before the switch are still on disk until the cleanup migration runs. `getResourceAccessMeta` still includes them. (The workflow reads this sentence used to enumerate — in `features/clusters/server/service.ts` and `features/teams/server/**` — went with those features on 2026-08-11.)
- **Ontology `delete_cluster` cascade-deletes.** ⛔ **PARTIALLY SUPERSEDED 2026-08-07 — the cascade is now a HARD delete; see below.** What survives verbatim and is the reason the RPC exists: the cluster + every object it owns (columns + nested cards) are removed **inside ONE atomic PL/pgSQL RPC**, because the earlier two-write service-layer version could commit one write and lose the other and permanently orphan a board. Today's function is `cascade_hard_delete_cluster` (migration `20260807120000`); `cascade_soft_delete_cluster` / `cascade_restore_cluster` (`20260718000040`) and `cascade_purge_cluster` (`20260718000060`) are left in the DB, unreferenced. **Never compose the old soft-delete + purge RPCs to emulate a hard delete** — that re-opens the exact desync the atomic function closes, and worse, since a failure between them leaves the objects hard-gone under a surviving tombstone. `restore_cluster` (op + `POST /api/ontology/clusters/[clusterId]/restore`) is GONE.
- **30-day trash retention.** ⛔ **REMOVED 2026-08-07.** `/api/cron/purge-trash`, its `vercel.json` entry and `features/trash/retention.ts` are deleted. The three surviving crons are `oauth-cleanup`, `reconcile-seats`, `stale-threads`. One-time migration `20260807110000_purge_soft_deleted_rows.sql` sweeps the tombstones the cron would have aged out (idempotent; `channels` deliberately excluded — see below).
- **Unified Workspace Trash (`features/trash/`).** ⛔ **REMOVED 2026-08-07.** The whole feature module, `GET|POST /api/workspaces/[workspaceSlug]/trash{,/restore,/purge}`, and every per-feature `listTrashed*` / `restore*` / `purge*` service fn are deleted. Don't reintroduce a cross-feature trash aggregator.

#### DELETES ARE PERMANENT (2026-08-07 — Samuel, RETIREMENT-UNWIRING-PLAN §2b / D6)

**Soft-delete is gone as PRODUCT BEHAVIOUR. Deleting anything in the app removes the row, immediately, with no recovery.** This is the statement of record — anywhere else in this document or in a comment still describes a trash, a restore or a retention window, it is history.

- **What each feature does now.** `deleteBase` / `deleteFolder` / `deleteEntry` / `deleteByPath` (knowledge), `deleteSkill` (skills), `deleteChat` (chats), `deleteCluster` / `deleteObject` (ontology) all call a workspace-scoped `hardDelete*` repository fn (or the atomic cascade RPC). **Every authorization gate is unchanged** — KB visibility + F-10 agent-read-only + team `edit`, skills visibility + agent-read-only, chat owner-only (and still refused for a workspace-scoped API key). Nothing became more permissive; only the write at the end changed.
- **The `deleted_at` COLUMNS STAY, and so do the read-path `deleted_at IS NULL` filters.** This was a behaviour change, not a schema teardown. The filters are permanent no-ops once the cleanup migration runs, but they cost nothing and they are what keeps pre-switch tombstones hidden in the meantime. A later migration may drop the columns, their partial indexes, and the unreferenced soft-delete RPCs. **Until then, a NEW read of a `deleted_at` table should still carry the guard** — cheaper than reasoning about whether the sweep has run in every environment.
- **`knowledge_entries.folder_id` is `ON DELETE SET NULL` — this is the trap.** Deleting a folder row alone does not delete its entries, it ORPHANS them to the base root, where they REAPPEAR. `hardDeleteFolder` therefore walks the `parent_id` subtree and deletes its entries FIRST, then the root folder (descendant *folders* cascade). Any future code that deletes a folder must do the same. `scripts/smoke-knowledge-audit-probes.ts` PROBE 7 is the live-DB guard.
- **No history event on delete.** `skill_events.skill_id` FKs to `skills` `ON DELETE CASCADE`, so a `skill.trashed`-style event written after a hard delete violates the FK. The audit trail dies with the row, by design.
- **`ontology_objects.parent_object_id` is `ON DELETE CASCADE` — and that cascade is a LIE about the tree.** It removes the MEMBERSHIP rows, not the child objects, so deleting an object one row at a time leaves its children alive, in no column and no cluster, reachable from nothing — and still counting against the workspace object cap (`billing/server/workspace-billing.ts` counts every `deleted_at IS NULL` row). Soft-delete hid this because `cascade_restore_cluster` re-linked the tree; hard delete makes it permanent and unreconstructible. `cascade_hard_delete_object` (migration `20260807140000`) is therefore the ONLY correct way to delete an object. It is deliberately **not** a blind subtree delete: it removes the target, then deletes only subtree members left with NO membership row at all, so an object hanging under two parents survives on the board that still holds it. Same reason the cluster RPC uses `UNION`. It returns the count of objects that became unreachable, which is what the confirm dialog shows.
- **Cluster delete has a reachable UI again.** It previously existed only in `graph-view.tsx`, whose sole consumer was the retired canvas page — so between the retirement and 2026-08-07 a cluster could not be deleted through ANY surface (the MCP op is refused: deletion is app-only). It now lives on the live `/ontology` tab strip; both surfaces render the same `delete-cluster-dialog.tsx`, so the cascade-count copy has one home.
- **Deleting is not just a write — it is a cache eviction.** `useKnowledgeQuery` and `useChatDetail` both prefer `data` over `error` on purpose (a failed background refetch must not blank rendered content), and the query cache is IndexedDB-persisted with a 24h `gcTime`. So an INVALIDATED entry for a deleted row still RENDERS it, across relaunches. Deleting a base must `removeQueries` its tree and every entry under it (`evictDeletedBase`, `knowledge/client/hooks.ts`); deleting a chat must `removeQueries` its transcript. Invalidating the parent LIST is not enough — and where a component seeds one-shot `useState` from a list query (`chats-view.tsx`), the list needs `setQueriesData` before the invalidate, because the cached list is served synchronously on remount and re-seeds the dead row before any refetch lands.
- **`channels.deleted_at` IS NOT A TRASH, AND SINCE 2026-08-08 IT IS A DM-ONLY MECHANIC (C-16, F-173 — Samuel's decision).** ⛔ **The sentence this bullet used to open with — "deliberately untouched" — is history: `deleteChannel` now BRANCHES on `is_direct`, and getting the branch backwards destroys data in one direction and strands it forever in the other.**
  - **A DM still SOFT-deletes, and that half is untouchable.** `deleted_at` on a direct channel is the CLOSE half of close/reopen: either side's next open revives the SAME row with its full history (`reviveChannel` / `reopenDirectChannel`), and since a DM's roster is immutable (`removeMember` refuses to tear the pair) it is the only exit a non-creator has. A tombstoned DM is LIVE PRODUCT STATE. Never hard-delete one, and never add `channels` to a tombstone-cleanup migration — that would destroy a shared transcript for both members on one member's unilateral click.
  - **Every OTHER channel HARD-deletes** (`repository.hardDeleteChannel`), owner / workspace-admin only. **Authorization is completely unchanged** — as everywhere else in §2b, only the write at the end moved. Before this, a soft-deleted non-DM was unreachable in every direction at once (no revive path, no restore route, no trash, excluded from the purge sweep) while still owning its slug against the non-partial `channels_workspace_slug_key`, so recreating the channel by its own name 409'd against something nobody could see — under a dialog that said "permanently deletes". The slug is now reusable: "delete #design, create #design again" returns `design`.
  - **ONE STATEMENT, NO RPC, and no migration.** All six child FKs into `channels` are `ON DELETE CASCADE` (members, messages, consent requests, tasks → participants, agents, sessions), so a single `DELETE` is already atomic and complete. `cascade_hard_delete_cluster` needed a PL/pgSQL body for the OPPOSITE reason — ontology's cascade is a lie about its tree — so **do not add an RPC here to "follow the pattern"**: the pattern is atomicity, and one statement already has it.
  - **`channels` still stays at `REPLICA IDENTITY DEFAULT`, and the reason changed.** Its own DELETE frame carries the primary key alone, so both subscribers' `workspace_id=eq.…` filter drops it — but the cascade fires real DELETEs on `channel_members`, which DOES carry `workspace_id` in its identity (`20260807150000`) and rides the SAME refetch signal in both subscribers (`CHANNEL_TABLES` / `SYNC_TABLES`). One doorbell, already paid for. Putting an identity on `channels` would widen the WAL record of `touchChannel` — one UPDATE per message posted, the hottest write in the feature — to deliver a frame that already arrives. `ui-sync-tables.test.mjs` carries this as the table's exemption reason. Accepted edge: a zero-member public channel deleted by a workspace admin emits no cascade frame, so other windows drop it on their next refetch rather than live.
  - **UI copy owed:** the non-DM branch of the delete dialog must now say the channel, its messages and its threads are permanently deleted for everyone, with `confirmLabel="Delete permanently"`. The DM branch must keep stating the revive mechanic — it is the only place a user is ever told about it, on the screen where it is the non-creator's only exit.
  - **THERE ARE NOW THREE WRITERS OF `channels.deleted_at`, AND THE THIRD IS NOT A MEMBER ACTION (2026-08-10, C-20).** Know all three before adding a fourth: (1) `deleteChannel`'s DM branch — a member closing the conversation; (2) `reviveChannel` clearing it on reopen — the other half of the same mechanic; (3) **`service-workspace-departure.removeWorkspaceDepartedMember`**, which soft-closes any live DM the leaver was half of. The third is **server-to-server, takes no `ChannelContext` on purpose, and must never be wired to a route, an MCP op, or anything a caller can reach** — its authority is the workspace removal that already happened upstream (`workspaces/server/membership-admin.removeMember`, admin+ with last-owner protection), and exposed directly it would be an unauthenticated "evict this user from every room" primitive. **Why a close rather than just dropping the row:** a DM with ONE member is a broken object here, not a smaller one — `buildDirectPeers` resolves the peer as `ids.find(id => id !== selfId) ?? ids[0]`, so the survivor would see the DM under their own name and avatar, and `resolveDirectPeer` requires exactly two members, so their next post would be auto-addressed to nobody. **ORDER IS CRASH-SAFETY, NOT STYLE: close FIRST, delete the membership row SECOND.** Interrupted after the close, the pair is hidden with its roster intact — inert, and self-healing on a rejoin. Interrupted the other way round leaves exactly the one-member DM the decision exists to prevent. It is reversible in the one case that matters (a rejoining leaver's next open finds the hidden row via `findDirectChannelAnyStatus` and re-asserts both rows, full history intact) and **cannot be resurrected while they are gone**, because the only re-asserting writer is reached through `createDirectChannel`, which throws on a non-member peer.
  - **⚠ AND THE TOMBSTONE IS NO LONGER A LIVE EVENT (2026-08-10, F-189).** `20260810100000` added `AND deleted_at IS NULL` to `channels_member_select`, and `channels` is in the publication — so `realtime.apply_rls` evaluates the NEW record of the very UPDATE that stamps `deleted_at`, finds it invisible to the subscriber, and **drops the frame.** The peer's sidebar loses the DM on their next refetch instead of live. Accepted, not fixed. **The general rule, because this will recur:** any table that (a) tombstones by UPDATE, (b) is in the publication, and (c) hides tombstones in its SELECT policy has silently traded its delete doorbell for a refetch. If you add `deleted_at IS NULL` to a published table's policy, you are making that trade — decide it, do not inherit it. The remedy shape is to touch a SIBLING published row that stays visible (for channels: the peer's `channel_members` row) **before** stamping the tombstone; after it, the sibling is dropped too, because `channel_members_member_select` now gates on the parent channel's `deleted_at` as well.
- **PER-MEMBER SETTINGS ARE PRIVATE AT THE DATABASE LAYER, BY COLUMN PRIVILEGE — not by policy and not by the DTO (2026-08-10, C-15, `20260810120000`).** Samuel's ruling: `role` and roster basics stay public to the workspace; per-member *settings* do not. **RLS could not express this** — a policy filters ROWS, and the requirement is a column. The DTO scrub (`dto.mapMemberRow`) could not either: its docblock claimed "it holds for every path", and that was false, because `channel_members` is in the publication and `authenticated` held table-wide SELECT, so the RAW row reached any channel member over CDC **and** over direct PostgREST with the anon key the browser ships. The fix is `REVOKE SELECT ON public.channel_members FROM anon, authenticated` plus a per-column `GRANT` of the public columns (`channel_id`, `user_id`, `workspace_id`, `role`, `last_read_at`, `added_by`, `joined_at`); `agent_tool_profile` is **service_role-only**, which binds PostgREST and realtime frames both. **THE STANDING RULE FOR ANY NEW PER-MEMBER SETTING: add it to the DTO scrub AND leave it out of that migration's `GRANT` list. Two edits; the second is the one that binds.** Keep the scrub — it is defence in depth and it is what shapes the API response, since the server reads as `service_role` and the column privilege redacts nothing on that path. **`anon` is granted the public columns rather than left bare on purpose** (see the migration header). **No new policy helper was added, deliberately** — `20260730052410` had to grant `anon` EXECUTE on the realtime RLS helpers, so every new helper is a fresh footgun on a surface that already has one. ⚠ **The accepted risk is F-190:** if the deployed `apply_rls` tests table-level SELECT ahead of its per-column loop, `channel_members` CDC goes dark for `authenticated` entirely — a lost roster doorbell, not a leak. One-statement rollback is in the migration header; **verification is owed at first live observation, by watching a frame, not by querying `information_schema`.**
- ⛔ **`/api/workflows/**` was untouched by the hard-delete switch (D3), trash and restore routes included** — the reasoning was "do not half-migrate a feature nobody can reach". **All ten routes were DELETED on 2026-08-11 with the feature**, so the soft-delete carve-out no longer exists anywhere. The rule it illustrates does: a retired surface is left INTERNALLY CONSISTENT until it is removed whole.
- **MCP agents cannot delete at all.** Blocked at the MCP layer (op-level deny-list in `packages/mcp-server`), which is what makes app-side hard delete safe: every surviving delete path is in-app and behind a confirm dialog.
- **The skills narrowing 409 is gone.** `updateSkill` used to 409 `SKILL_ATTACHED_TO_WORKFLOWS` when narrowing the sharing of a workflow-attached skill. With workflows retired the user had no surface on which to detach anything, so the error named an invisible feature and offered an impossible remedy. The check short-circuits; `workflow_skills` rows are untouched, and the check returns with workflows if they ever do.

- **`agent_write_enabled` is now actually enforced (F-10/F-10b).** The flag (per-base / per-skill) makes a resource read-only to AGENTS (`ctx.source === "agent"`, i.e. an MCP-bearer caller); humans are unaffected. It is checked in `assertBaseWritable` / `assertAgentWriteAllowed` (writes) AND the delete paths, BEFORE the team-access matrix, so read-only wins over team `edit`. Previously the flag was advertised but never read on the write/delete paths. Seeded starter KBs and skills ship with the flag OFF (agent-read-only onboarding content) — set it explicitly in seeds, don't rely on column defaults.
- **Optimistic concurrency everywhere.** `dopl_ontology` object writes now take an optional `expected_version` (= object `updated_at`), sent as `X-Updated-At`, checked via `.eq('updated_at', …)` → 412 `ONTOLOGY_STALE_VERSION` (matches the KB/skills CAS contract). Skill body CAS token moved off a JS millisecond timestamp to a DB microsecond trigger (`20260718000020`).
- **Workspace targeting is fail-closed + legible.** A blank/whitespace `workspace=` now errors instead of silently falling through to the session default (server.ts `wrapped`), and the `_dopl_status` footer reports the **effective** per-call workspace (with a note when it differs from the session default) — not just the session default.
- **MCP-2: the default-workspace fallback is REMOVED (fail-closed resolution).** `resolveActiveWorkspace` no longer resolves a "default" workspace when no `X-Workspace-Id` header is sent — it resolves off ACTIVE memberships (see §9 "Workspace resolution"): exactly one auto-targets; 0 or 2+ → 400 `WORKSPACE_REQUIRED`; a blank/non-UUID header → 400 `WORKSPACE_INVALID`. Consequences future sessions must know: (1) the MCP server boots off `client.listWorkspaces()` (directory), NOT the old `getActiveWorkspace` handshake — 1 membership auto-targets, 2+ leaves no default and the `registerTool` wrapper refuses a no-`workspace=` call listing the choices (M-3); `buildInstructions(directory)` bakes the workspace table + targeting rule into the server instructions (M-2); the footer is mandatory-effective with a source label (`per-call arg` | `sole membership` | `header pin`, M-4). (2) The `set_workspace` meta-tool is GONE (a stateless connection can't persist a switch); `current_workspace` now reports what a no-arg call resolves to (or the 2+ choices). (3) The 4 export GET routes opt into `withWorkspaceAuth({ workspaceIdFromQuery: true })` so header-less downloads keep working. (4) `findDefaultWorkspaceForUser` is now signup-bootstrap + billing-grandfather ONLY — forbidden in auth resolution. (5) UI callers of workspace-scoped `withWorkspaceAuth` routes MUST forward the header (`apiRequest({ workspaceId })`); the Settings trash section and the public pricing page were fixed to do so (a latent "shows the default workspace" trash bug is closed as a side effect).
- **The compiled `dist/` can no longer go stale.** `@dopl/mcp-server` and `@dopl/client` ship committed `dist/` that the app loads at runtime (`serverExternalPackages`). Root `build` now runs `build:packages` (rebuilds both `dist/` from src) before `next build`, so a src change can never ship behind a stale `dist/`. **When you edit either package's `src/`, rebuild its `dist/` (or run `npm run build:packages`) before committing.**

### Realtime & new-workspace seeding (2026-07-17)

- **Shared channel registry (2026-08-02, desktop migration Phase 0):** `useWorkspaceTablesRealtime` no longer opens a channel per component instance. All mounts sharing a `(topicPrefix, workspaceId, tables)` key ride ONE ref-counted channel via `src/shared/realtime/shared-channel-registry.ts`. The registry owns reconnect backoff, a StrictMode-proof teardown grace window, and — load-bearing — GENERATION-UNIQUE topic names: realtime-js dedupes `channel(topic)` by name and forgets removed channels only after the leave push settles, so topic reuse resurrects corpse channels. Never construct a raw `supabase.channel()` for postgres_changes in feature code; go through the hook. The per-instance design existed to dodge the v2 "no `.on()` after `.subscribe()`" crash — the registry keeps that safety (all bindings attach before subscribe; late subscribers share via the listener set).
- **Query cache persists to IndexedDB (2026-08-02, Phase 1):** `QueryProvider` wraps `PersistQueryClientProvider` + `src/shared/api/idb-persister.ts` (24h maxAge, success-only dehydration, gcTime === maxAge). Cold starts render last-known data; don't add per-query `gcTime` below 24h without knowing it kills that query's persistence.
- **AN UNSPECIFIED QUERY OPTION MUST BE OMITTED, NEVER PASSED AS `undefined` (2026-08-07, F-163).** TanStack merges options by spread (`{...defaultOptions.queries, ...options}`), so a key that is *present* with the value `undefined` DELETES the client default underneath it instead of falling back to it. `useApiQuery` forwarded `staleTime: opts.staleTime` unconditionally, which made `QUERY_DEFAULT_OPTIONS.staleTime` (30s) inert app-wide, on both clients, for every read that did not name its own — the whole "navigating back within 30s renders from cache" policy, never once true. `use-api-query-core.ts` now routes every caller-supplied option through `definedOnly()`; anything else that builds query (or mutation) options out of optional caller input owes the same treatment. **`0` is not "unset"** — test `!== undefined`, never truthiness, or an explicit `staleTime: 0` silently inherits the 30s cache it refused. A surface that genuinely must revalidate — the channel transcript and its thread overlay, the boot answer, the ontology snapshot, the consent inbox, the invite link — states that at its own call site rather than relying on the app default happening to be low.
- **Bearer Supabase JWTs are SESSION callers (2026-08-02, Phase 2 prereq):** `withUserAuth` routes bearers by exact prefix — `dopl_at_*` → OAuth/agent branch; anything else is verified locally as a Supabase access JWT and gets cookie-identical session semantics. The bundled desktop SPA authenticates this way. Never key agent-vs-user behavior off "has Authorization header" — key off `agentTokenId`.
- **Realtime:** every content surface streams agent/MCP writes live. Publication covers knowledge_*, skills, skill_versions, ontology_*, chats/chat_messages/chat_folders, channels/channel_members/channel_messages/channel_consent_requests/agent_presence — **17 tables, and the list is an invariant, not a description**: a published table with no subscriber costs WAL decode + a per-subscription RLS evaluation on every write forever (F-094 Residual 3), and a subscriber on an unpublished table is WORSE than an error — Realtime accepts the binding, reports SUBSCRIBED, and delivers nothing, so the surface just silently stops updating. `dopl-desktop-app/test/ui-sync-tables.test.mjs` pins both directions against `supabase/migrations` + `main/ui-sync.js` `SYNC_TABLES`; publication changes and subscriber changes MUST ship in the same release. **`workflow_*` LEFT the publication 2026-08-07** (`20260807100000_drop_workflow_tables_from_realtime.sql`, retirement Phase 5 / D8) along with `channel_agents` and `clusters` (`20260807000000`) — the tables and their server reads/writes are untouched; only the live feed is gone. Per-feature subscribers live in `features/<name>/client/realtime.ts` on the shared `useWorkspaceTablesRealtime` refetch-signal pattern (events trigger a filtered service refetch — never payload merging, so RLS + service filters like the chats retention window stay authoritative). `src/shared/realtime/refetch-coordinator.ts` defers refetches while local debounced edits are pending — any new live surface MUST use it or it will clobber in-flight typing.
- **REPLICA IDENTITY is the second half of the publication invariant, and it is what makes DELETEs deliverable at all (2026-08-07, `20260807150000`).** Both subscribers bind with `filter: workspace_id=eq.<id>`. On DELETE, the deployed `realtime.apply_rls` tests that filter against `old_columns`, which is *the replica identity* — so under `REPLICA IDENTITY DEFAULT` (primary key only) `workspace_id` is absent, the filter can never match, and **the frame is silently dropped**. This was invisible until 2026-08-07 because every user-facing delete was an UPDATE (`deleted_at` stamp → full new record → filter matched); the moment deletes became real DELETEs, cross-device delete propagation stopped for every content table at once. The fix is `REPLICA IDENTITY USING INDEX` on a unique `(workspace_id, id)` — **not FULL**: FULL logs the entire old row on every UPDATE and would detoast `knowledge_entries.body` on every 1500 ms autosave, which is the exact cost F-094 was fighting. Six tables stay DEFAULT on purpose (cascade-only children, never-deleted rows, still-soft-deleting `channels`, and `skill_versions`, whose retention trim is the highest-frequency delete in the schema and is user-invisible). Two consequences to keep in mind: **`old_record` is separately redacted to the primary key on any RLS-enabled table**, so a DELETE frame arrives bare even now — the doorbell pattern is not optional, there is no payload to merge; and this migration must stay ordered AFTER `20260807110000_purge_soft_deleted_rows.sql`, or that one-time tombstone sweep fires the entire backlog at every connected client. `ui-sync-tables.test.mjs` pins the invariant (identity must contain `workspace_id`) rather than the mechanism, so FULL would also satisfy it. **Do not verify this against upstream `walrus` — its master returns `coalesce(..., true)` here and says the opposite of what is deployed.**
- **Loading skeletons:** shared primitives in `src/shared/ui/skeleton.tsx` (`Skeleton`/`SkeletonBar`/`SkeletonLine`/`SkeletonText`/`SkeletonRow`/`TwoPaneListSkeleton`).
