Pinned by `src/shared/lib/safe-label.test.ts`, which asserts BOTH directions: every class rejected, and `Müller's Team` / `研究ノート` / `Café — Zürich` accepted.
- **`descSuffix` no longer hand-rolls a flatten-and-clip.** Its `\s*\n+\s*` pass missed U+0085 (NEL, absent from JavaScript's `\s`) and touched no markdown; it defers to the shared neutralizer, so the bound is `INLINE_TEXT_MAX` (160, `...` tail).
- **Pinned by** `narration.test.ts` (one-definition identity + source scan, workspace name, footer, directory tools), `tool-narration.test.ts` (chats, members), `tool-narration-graph.test.ts` (kb, cluster, skill, workflow, ontology, map, search), sharing `narration-fixtures.ts`. Every assertion is mutation-verified: **28 reverts, 28 test failures, 0 vacuous.**

#### MCP IDENTITY + LOCUS — one record for "who am I and where am I" (2026-07-31, F-104)

**One definition: `CallerIdentity` + its renderers in `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/identity.ts`. Every surface that answers "who am I" renders FROM THAT RECORD and from nothing else.** Three surfaces used to answer it from three unreconciled sources — the boot status ping's `user_id` (reaching only `dopl_channel`), `GET /api/workspaces/me`'s nullable `userId` (`dopl_members(op="whoami")`), and, in `dopl_ontology(op="anchor")`, no caller id at all — so two tools on ONE connection could disagree about who was calling, and did, in front of an operator.

- **The record is resolved ONCE, at boot, from the credential doing the work.** `/api/mcp/route.ts` already held the caller's `userId`, the `X-Dopl-Runtime` value and (after this round) the token's `client_id`/`client_name`, and discarded all three — then `bootServer` re-fetched `userId` over a loopback. It now passes `caller` into `BootOptions`; **the transport's id WINS over the ping's**, because it comes from the credential authorizing that request rather than a second call that fails independently. `factory.ts` merges, `server.ts` fans out to the footer, `current_workspace`, `dopl_members` and `dopl_ontology`.
- **The `_dopl_status` footer leads with WHO, then WHERE.** `caller: id=<uuid> · runtime=<desktop-session|unstamped>` sits above `active_workspace:`. It is terse ON PURPOSE — it rides every successful response. **Deliberately omitted:** the display name (a roster round-trip on a route that boots per request, and the peer-settable half anyway) and the credential label (a hostname; a per-response token cost that never changes, and one careless paste from reaching a peer). Both live in `whoami`.
- **The footer is skipped when there is no effective workspace**, which is exactly the 2+-membership case. `current_workspace` therefore renders the caller itself on that branch ONLY — with a session default the footer already carries it and restating it would print the caller twice. That branch also now prints workspace `id:` beside each slug; it used to withhold the one handle nobody can forge from the surface an agent reaches for when it is lost.
- **`whoami` is the authoritative answer** — identity line (name + email + id, or the bare id, or an explicit UNKNOWN; never a role with nobody attached), role, teams, the session's runtime + credential, and `LOCUS_NOTE`.
- **`op="anchor"` states the caller's id FIRST and frames the object as context.** It is the op `buildInstructions` sends every agent to for "my/me" requests, and it rendered `You are anchored to this object.` over a member-typed `ontology_objects.name` that any agent can re-point with `op="claim_anchor"`. The instructions were reworded to match.
- **`dopl_members(op="list")` marks `· you`**, the same wording `channel-render.formatMemberLine` uses — two rosters over the same column that disagree about how "you" looks is the same bug class as two tools that disagree about who you are.
- 🔒 **`dopl_channel(op="members")` RENDERS MEMBER EMAIL ONLY TO A WORKSPACE ADMIN OR ON THE CALLER'S OWN ROW (2026-08-10, F-100).** `formatMemberLine` (`channel-render.ts:409`) computes `emailAllowed = callerIsAdmin || isSelf`; everyone else renders name + id + role, and **a member with no display name renders BY ID ALONE, never by email**. The reason is enumeration, not secrecy: `listChannels` ORs `visibility.eq.public`, so an agent can walk every public channel it was never invited to and `op="members"` each one — an agent is a far better enumerator than the human this was parity with. **The enumeration is unchanged and that was the deliberate choice**; what is gone is the PII harvest.
  - **`isAdmin` is threaded from the boot status ping and DEFAULTS FALSE at every hop** — `server.ts:226` (`options.isAdmin ?? false`) → `channel.ts:109` → `opMembers` → `formatMemberLine`, all four with a `false` default. **Fail-closed by default value**, which is the only way a defaulted boolean is safe: a boot ping that could not resolve the flag scrubs rather than leaks.
  - ⚠ **DELIBERATE ASYMMETRY: the WEB roster still shows email to any member, so the MCP surface is now strictly TIGHTER than web.** Accepted, not an oversight. **Do not "restore parity" by loosening the MCP side** — closing the gap means applying the same admin-or-self scrub to the web `/members` DTO. Tracked as F-100's residual.

**WHAT THE SURFACE REFUSES TO CLAIM — do not "improve" any of these into a confident answer:**
- A credential label names the machine the credential was **MINTED** on, never where the session is **RUNNING**. A bearer is copyable by construction and the desktop writes the same one into more than one place.
- An absent runtime stamp renders **`unstamped`, never `external`**. Absence is usually an external client, but a desktop spawn on an OLDER BUILD is also unstamped (`session-dispatch.js` names that skew), so the copy reports the observation and not a conclusion. The header remains a routing hint that grants nothing (§8).
- **A peer's MACHINE is not knowable and is stated as not knowable**, with "do not assert it either way". A peer's **ACCOUNT is** decidable: different user id ⇒ different account. This is the exact question that cost a live round of cross-user work.

**PRIVACY FENCE.** A member other than the caller is **name + immutable id + membership, and nothing else** — no hostname, no credential, no runtime, ever. The session record now flows through `dopl_members` and `dopl_ontology`, so the fence is asserted by tests rather than held by the absence of data.

**Credential classification: `src/shared/auth/mcp-credential.ts`.** `validateAccessToken` now selects `client_id, client_name` (it had always dropped them) and returns a `credential: { kind, label }`. **`kind` keys on `client_id` — NEVER on `client_name`**, which is caller-supplied on both mint paths: a DCR app registering itself as "Dopl Desktop (device tokens)" must still classify `oauth-app`, or a remote app could dress itself as the operator's own machine inside the agent's identity readout. Nothing gates on any of it. `DEVICE_CLIENT_ID` / `DEVICE_CLIENT_NAME` moved here (ONE definition) because `mcp-oauth.ts` sits at the §2 cap.

**File-size fallout, per §2.** `mcp-oauth.ts` stayed under 500 by moving the device-client constants out; `members.ts` (491) shed its formatting half into **`members-render.ts`**, which the parity split-scan picks up automatically as a `members-*.ts` sibling.

**Pinned by** `identity.test.ts`, `members-identity.test.ts`, `ontology-anchor.test.ts`, plus additions to `server.test.ts`, `factory.test.ts` and `mcp-oauth-device-token.test.ts`. Mutation-verified: **12 reverts, 25 test failures, 0 vacuous.** MCP suite 206 → 262; root 1299 → 1301.

#### v2.6 additions — server-side DM addressing, so a reply is never undeliverable (2026-07-29)

A live prod DM exchange proved the addressing contract was only half-enforced: the peer's agent posted an ADDRESSED message, the operator's session agent replied over MCP `post` with metadata `{summary}` only, and the reply was silently never delivered — the peer's `classify()` saw an agent-authored UNADDRESSED message and returned `ignore` (the v1.3.1 loop brake, working as designed), and with no `taskId` the v2.5 inbound gate had no `feedInboundForTask` route either. The web composer auto-addressed a DM peer since v1.6; the MCP path did not, and "the model must remember a parameter" is not a delivery guarantee. Fix is **server-side, metadata-only** (no schema/migration change, jsonb as always).

- **`postMessage` metadata folds moved to `server/service-writes-metadata.ts`** (`resolvePostMetadata`) — one place now owns the v1.1 reserved-key anti-spoof strip, addressing, and the v1.5 task-key stamping. `service-writes.ts` was over the §2 cap, so the same pass also split the task lifecycle out to `server/service-tasks.ts` (`createTask` / `closeTask` / `setTaskMode` / `reopenTask`, re-exported from the `service.ts` barrel unchanged); `service-writes.ts` is back under 500 lines. No route, schema, DTO, or client change.
- **DM auto-address.** A post into an `is_direct` channel with no caller `to` is stamped `metadata.to_user_id` = the OTHER member of that DM, resolved LIVE off `channel_members` (the same table the explicit `to` path validates against, so the addressee-is-an-active-member rule holds by construction and `CHANNEL_ADDRESSEE_NOT_MEMBER` is untouched). The stamp flows through the SAME validated path as `to` — reserved keys are still stripped from caller metadata first and re-added only from validated values, so a spoofed `metadata.to_user_id` still cannot survive. **Fail-quiet, never fail-closed:** a roster that is not exactly two rows (torn-down DM, unexpected third row) resolves to nothing and the post proceeds unaddressed rather than 400ing. An explicit `to` ALWAYS wins. **Never** auto-addressed in a non-direct channel — with 3+ members the recipient is ambiguous and a wrong guess prompts the wrong operator.
- **Task-id inheritance for session replies.** An untagged plain `message` in a DM addressed to the peer inherits `metadata.taskId` from the channel's SINGLE open first-class task whose participants are exactly `{author, peer}` (either direction), and that inheritance runs through the normal stamping path so `taskMode` / `taskCreatedBy` / `taskTitle` / `taskTarget` are stamped from the task row — the fields the desktop's routing + suppression predicates read. Conservative by construction: 0 or 2+ candidate open tasks stamps NOTHING; a caller-supplied `taskId` (first-class OR legacy `task-<uuid>-<seq>`) suppresses inheritance entirely; a non-`message` kind never inherits (a stray `task_failed` must not land on an unrelated task's card).
- **Loop safety re-verified in code.** Addressed agent replies now classify `trigger` (or `task-reply`) instead of `fyi`, so the loop brake is no longer what stops a two-agent DM. Every hop is still bounded by a human-or-standing-decision gate: a fresh trigger raises a `channel_consent_requests` row (Allow/Deny, or an `auto_allowed` trust rule the operator created), and a reply bound for an existing session passes `session-gate.js` where it is HELD until the operator Accepts unless they armed auto-approve / "Accept for this task" — plus the v1.9 turn cap 24, 15m idle TTL, cost cap, and one-window-per-(channel,task). Nothing changed on the desktop: unaddressed stays ignorable; the point is that legitimate replies are now addressed.
- **MCP description.** `CHANNEL_DESCRIPTION`'s `post` bullet states plainly that a direct-channel post is addressed to the other member automatically and that `to` is still how you address one member of a 3+ member channel. Parity test green (op substrings + param-usage assertions unaffected); `dist/` rebuilt (`npm run build:packages`).
- **Tests.** New `server/service-writes-metadata.test.ts` (15 cases through `postMessage`: peer stamped, explicit `to` wins, non-direct untouched, unresolvable/ambiguous peer no-stamp, caller `to_user_id` still stripped then replaced by the validated peer, inheritance single-candidate both directions + reserved-key stamping fires, 2+ candidates / closed / third-party tasks no-stamp, explicit + legacy taskId suppress, non-direct + lifecycle-kind never inherit). Root suite **669 green**; MCP parity 52 green, zero drift. Residual debt: **F-079** (two extra reads per DM post; `notify_scope='none'` no longer mutes an addressed DM ask, same delta v1.6 took on the web; inheritance is DM-only).

#### v3.0 additions — THREAD / SESSION vocabulary (2026-07-30)

A naming round, not a behavior round: the product called one exchange a "task", which was wrong (a thread may be a single message) and left "session" doing double duty. THE MODEL at the top of this section is now the definition of record; state it that way in the MCP tool description, in the docs, and in the desktop's first-turn prompt framing. No schema change, no migration, no route change.

- **MCP HARD CUTOVER, no aliases.** `create_task` → `create_thread`, `close_task` → `close_thread`, `set_task_mode` → `set_thread_mode`, `list_tasks` → `list_threads`, `get_task` → `get_thread`; `post`'s `task=` param → `thread=`. The old names return the standard unknown-op error, so **a connector must be reloaded**. Enum + `WRITE_OPS.dopl_channel` (`server.ts`) + `READ_OPS.dopl_channel` (`parity.test.ts`) + handlers + `@dopl/client` methods moved together; parity 52 green with `KNOWN_*_DRIFT` still `{}`. **Both `dist/` rebuilt** (`npm run build:packages`) — the app loads `dist` at runtime, so a stale `dist` ships the old surface.
- **`CHANNEL_DESCRIPTION` rewritten.** Leads with THE MODEL verbatim, then the protocol (open or find a thread; thread every post with `thread=<id>`; post progress as it lands; close when the GOAL is done). It also now states the real cost model, correcting a standing inaccuracy: **every** channel op may gate, own-channel `post` included, and the operator's v2.9 Axis-B Messages posture is the only thing that can send without a click (the agent cannot observe it, and any grant is per-session and dropped on park).
- **Web domain types + copy.** `ChannelTask` → `ChannelThread`, `TaskStatus` → `ThreadStatus`, `TaskMode` → `ThreadMode`, `TaskOutcome` → `ThreadOutcome`; `useChannelTasks` → `useChannelThreads` (`hooks/use-channel-threads.ts`, returns `threads`); `task-panel.tsx` → `thread-panel.tsx` (`TaskPanel` → `ThreadPanel`); client fns → `listChannelThreads` / `createChannelThread` / `closeChannelThread` / `reopenChannelThread` / `setChannelThreadMode`. Visible copy: `Task active|complete|failed` → `Thread active|complete|failed`, `Close task` → `Close thread`, `Reopen task` → `Reopen thread`, `Open window` → `Open session`, the panel heading → `Threads`, and the two session notes now say thread ("This thread has no session on this machine.", "The session was ended on the desktop. The thread stays open."). Route paths and request/response field names are UNCHANGED (storage names) and mapped at the client boundary.
- **Name clash resolved (F-081).** The channel detail pane is `ChannelPane` (`components/channel-pane.tsx`), so `ChannelThread` now names only the TYPE (one exchange). The transcript component followed one level down: `MessageThread` → **`ChannelTranscript`** (`components/channel-transcript.tsx` + test), imported by `channel-pane.tsx` — it renders the WHOLE channel transcript (every message, session card, and activity row), not one thread. In `lib/group-thread.ts` the pure domain type `TaskOverlay` → `ThreadOverlay`; `SessionGroup.taskId` deliberately KEEPS the `task` spelling (it is the `metadata.taskId` wire value verbatim) and carries a BOUNDARY comment saying so.
- **Not renamed on purpose.** Everything on the storage side: `channel_tasks`, `metadata.task*` keys, `task_*` message kinds, `TASK_*` error codes, `/tasks` routes, and the server lane's own identifiers. See the boundary note at the top of this section.

#### v3.1 additions — server-side audit fixes: thread atomicity, thread-scoped writes, slug allocation (2026-07-30)

Four server bugs from the six-agent audit. **No schema change and no migration** — every fix is in the service/repository lane, so nothing on the wire moved and the installed desktop (1.7.11) keeps working unchanged.

- **`create_thread` is now atomic-by-convergence (was: the idempotency key could swallow the request forever).** The thread row and its initiating message are two writes with no transaction around them. If the insert landed and the post then threw, the retry hit the `client_msg_id` short-circuit, returned the stored thread and NEVER posted — leaving a thread that exists in the panel with no message for the responder's desktop to route, so no session ever spawned (there is a live example in prod). Fix: the initiating message now carries a DERIVED idempotency key, `task-open-<threadId>`, and the post is re-driven on EVERY path that returns that thread (fresh insert, `client_msg_id` short-circuit, lost-23505-race). `postMessage`'s own idempotency (`findMessageByClientId` → the unique index) collapses the repeats, so the request lands exactly once and a failed post is repaired by the next retry. **Deliberately NOT moved into a PL/pgSQL RPC:** `postMessage` owns the addressee check, the DM auto-address, the reserved-key strip and the task-key stamping, and forking that into SQL would give the metadata contract two homes. The short-circuit only re-posts when the caller IS the thread's `created_by`, so a colliding key from another member still dedups but cannot write into their thread. Residual: with NO `client_msg_id` there is still no dedup at all (unchanged), so a retry there creates a second thread rather than repairing the first. **WAKE-V1 additive response field:** `POST /api/channels/[channelId]/tasks` now returns `{ task, openingSeq }` — the seq of that opening message — and `@dopl/client.createChannelThread` returns `{ thread, openingSeq }` (null when the route omits it, i.e. an older deployment or the short-circuit that returns ANOTHER member's thread). The `create_thread` MCP result quotes it as `since=<seq>` directly; before, it told the agent to call `read limit=1`, which cost a round-trip AND raced the peer — a reply landing in between becomes "the newest message", so the await armed one past the request and never returned the reply it was waiting for.
- **A thread may not be addressed to its own creator (added 2026-07-31, same lane).** `createTask` refuses `toUserId === ctx.userId` — `TaskSelfTargetError` (`server/errors.ts`) → **400 `CHANNEL_TASK_SELF_TARGET`** (`http-mapping.ts`); distinct from `DIRECT_SELF_TARGET`, which is the self-DM. It was accepted silently and observed live: the panel rendered a live request "addressed to <caller>", the peer's listener logged `verdict ignore`, and because only `created_by` and `target_user_id` may post into a thread (bullet below), the ONE member allowed to answer was the member who asked — unanswerable, permanently. **The guard sits AFTER the addressee-membership check and BEFORE the `client_msg_id` short-circuit, on purpose:** behind it, a retry would find the stored dead thread and return it as a success, so the caller is told the thread is fine exactly once it is unfixable; in front, every attempt errors identically. No UI path regresses — the web address picker already filtered the caller client-side — so this closes the AGENT path, where `to` is whatever user id the model resolved (a session holding two dopl connections self-addressed one live). **MCP:** a `self_target` kind in `classifyBadRequest` (`channel-errors.ts`) with a friendly `opCreateThread` arm that points at `op="members"` (the failure is not knowing who ELSE is in the channel); `opPost`'s case is marked UNREACHABLE and exists only to keep the switch exhaustive, because **`post to=self` stays deliberately unguarded** — the receiving desktop classifies it as noise, and a post is not a thread. Tests: `service-tasks-idempotency.test.ts` (NEW — `service-tasks.test.ts` was over the §2 cap, 492 lines, and its idempotency §2 moved out).
- **Channel membership is not THREAD membership (was: any member could write into any thread).** `metadata.taskId` is caller-settable by design (a responder replies within a thread), and the server validated only that the id resolved in THIS channel. In a 3+ member channel every member can read every thread id, so any of them could stamp another pair's id — and the server then stamped `taskMode`/`taskCreatedBy`/`taskTitle`/`taskTarget` from the real row, satisfying the desktop's routing predicate and landing the message inside someone else's thread card AND session window. A caller-supplied first-class id must now belong to the poster (`created_by` or `target_user_id`) or the post is **refused 403 `TASK_FORBIDDEN`**. Refused, not silently un-threaded: a message the author believes landed in a thread and the recipient never sees is exactly the invisible-delivery failure this feature exists to prevent. Closing/reopening were already gated this way. INHERITED ids need no check (inheritance only resolves a thread whose participants are `{author, peer}` by construction).
- **The calm-terminal flags are reserved keys now.** `declined` / `dropped` / `interrupted` / `capped` / `ended` ride on a `task_failed` and decide whether the other side's card reads as a calm operator-chosen ending or a red failure (and drive the message receipt). They were never stripped, so a member could POST `{kind:"task_failed", metadata:{taskId:"<their thread>", declined:true}}` and fabricate that thread's outcome. The strict `=== true` read in `lib/group-thread.ts` defends against type coercion, not against a literal `true`. They are now stripped from caller metadata like any reserved key and re-stamped ONLY for a thread participant — first-class threads via the gate above, and a LEGACY `task-{channelId}-{seq}` id (no `channel_tasks` row, but the shape the installed desktop posts most of its lifecycle events with) via its opening request's `{author, to_user_id}` pair, the same pair `groupThread` joins on. Fails closed: unknown id shape, missing opener, or an unaddressed opener → not a participant → the flag is dropped and the message still posts. Only a literal `true` is re-stamped, so `capped:"true"` no longer reaches storage at all.
- **Slug allocation now agrees with the index (was: a soft-deleted channel was invisible to allocation but still owned its slug).** `channels_workspace_slug_key` is a plain (NOT partial) unique index on `(workspace_id, lower(slug))`, but `existingSlugs` filtered `deleted_at is null` — so "create #Design, delete it, create #Design again" allocated `design` twice and 409'd naming a channel the user can no longer see; on the DM path the 23505 fell into a catch whose live-rows-only lookup returned null and rethrew raw, i.e. a generic 500 on "open direct message". `existingSlugs` now includes soft-deleted rows (case-folded to match `lower(slug)`). **The index is deliberately left non-partial:** a soft-deleted DM is REVIVED by pair (`findDirectChannelAnyStatus` → `reviveChannel`), which would break if another channel could take its slug while it was hidden. The DM catch also converges on the soft-deleted pair (reviving it) and maps an unresolvable 23505 to a clean 409 instead of a raw 500.
- **`repository.ts` split (§2).** It was 534 lines (a pre-existing violation, not in the §2 table). The transcript lane moved to `server/repository-messages.ts` (`listMessages`, `findMessageByClientId`, `findMessageBySeq`, `insertMessage`, `lastMessages`), joining the existing `repository-tasks.ts` / `repository-collab.ts` siblings; `repository.ts` keeps channels + members + profiles and is 409 lines. Services and tests import `repository-messages` directly (no barrel re-export). The dead `findDirectChannel` (live-rows-only) was deleted — every caller that converges on a DM must see the hidden row.
- **Tests (+15, root suite 695 green).** `service-tasks.test.ts` — the B1 regression drives insert-ok → post-throws → retry and asserts the request exists exactly once (plus a third send that inserts nothing), and a colliding foreign key posts nothing. `service-writes.test.ts` — a non-participant is refused 403 and nothing is inserted; the target may post. `service-writes-metadata.test.ts` — calm flags stamped for a legacy-pair participant and for a first-class participant, stripped for a third party, stripped when the opener is unresolvable, stripped when truthy-but-not-true, stripped with no thread id. `service-direct.test.ts` (NEW, split out of `service-tasks.test.ts`) — soft-deleted slug is taken, a genuine race is a 409, the DM 23505 revives the pair. `service-tasks-lifecycle.test.ts` (NEW, split out of `service-writes.test.ts` for the §2 cap) — the close echo + reopen silence.

#### MULTIPLAYER — named agents, breakout rooms, and THE LAW (2026-07-31) — ⛔ SUPERSEDED IN FULL

> **⛔ SUPERSEDED IN FULL by the channels rollback (2026-08-05, F-141 — see "PHASE 2 OF THE ROLLBACK" below). READ THIS AS HISTORY, NOT AS LAW.**
>
> **Nothing in this subsection or the next (`MULTIPLAYER v2`) describes a surface that exists.** Named agents, `@handle` addressing, `to_agent` / `to_agents` / `as_agent`, summoning, breakout-room participant sets, engagement and THE LAW are all deleted — server-side, in `@dopl/client`, in the MCP tool and in both clients. The whole addressing surface is `toUserId` plus `intent` (chat vs. request). The two migrations named below were NEVER reverted, so `channel_agents` and `channel_task_participants` still hold every row and nothing writes them.
>
> Both subsections are kept whole and unedited because the ROLLBACK bullets state what was removed by naming it, and a reader needs the original to know what those names meant. **If a statement here conflicts with the Phase 2 bullets, the Phase 2 bullets win with no exceptions.**

The wave that took Channels from "two people, two agents, one pair-shaped thread" to N. **THE MODEL above still holds and is not replaced — this extends it.** State the extension this way everywhere:

> *(All four lines below are DELETED MODEL — see the banner.)*
> A CHANNEL (or DM) is a **MEETING ROOM** where humans talk.
> Either human summons their own **AGENTS** — a first-class named entity, owned by the member who summoned it, running on **that owner's machine**, addressed by handle (`@quartz`).
> Work moves into **BREAKOUT ROOMS**, which are **THREADS with a participant set** — not sub-channels. One channel, one listener, one cursor, one transcript.
> **THE LAW: nothing acts unless addressed.** `to_agent=<handle>` makes an agent act. Addressing a HUMAN does one of two things and **`as_agent` decides which** — see the addressing rule below. There is no third case.

**Two migrations, both ADDITIVE-ONLY and both APPLIED LIVE** (house precedent: channels v1.2/v1.5, the 2026-07-20 audit batch). Local files `20260731120000_channel_agents.sql` / `20260731130000_channel_task_participants.sql`; the prod ledger stamped them `20260731224658` / `20260731224726` (the usual divergence when a migration is applied through the Supabase MCP, which assigns its own version — cf. local `20260725120000_channels.sql` = remote `20260726003630`). New tables and one new guard function; **no existing table altered.**

- **`channel_agents`.** `id`, `channel_id`, denormalized `workspace_id`, `owner_user_id`, `name`, `status`, `created_at`, `updated_at`. `name` carries the addressing charset as a column CHECK — `^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{1,30}$`, duplicated in TS as `AGENT_HANDLE_RE` (`schema-agents.ts`) — and is unique **per channel, case-folded** (`UNIQUE INDEX (channel_id, lower(name))`). Deliberately channel-wide, not per-owner: an `@`-mention resolves against the ROOM, so two owners cannot both hold `quartz` in it. `status` is TEXT+CHECK (not a PG enum) over `summoned | active | parked | dismissed`, default `summoned`; a dismissed row is **kept**, because the messages it authored still need attribution. House write model exactly: RLS on, base INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE REVOKEd from `authenticated`+`anon`, **no write policy at all**, service-role writes only; one member-SELECT policy mirroring `channel_messages_member_select` (an agent is visible to the whole room — peers have to be able to address it). Reuses `channel_child_workspace_guard`, scoped `UPDATE OF workspace_id, channel_id` so a rename or a status flip never re-fires it. **IN the realtime publication** — the web's agent chips render live status.
- **`channel_task_participants`.** `task_id` → `channel_tasks`, `workspace_id`, `kind` (`user|agent`), nullable `user_id` / `agent_id`, `added_by` (`ON DELETE SET NULL`). The discriminator is **exclusive in both directions**, written as two equivalences so neither column can ride along on the wrong kind: `((kind='user') = (user_id IS NOT NULL)) AND ((kind='agent') = (agent_id IS NOT NULL))`. Identity uniqueness is an **expression** index — `(task_id, kind, coalesce(user_id, agent_id))` — safe because the shape CHECK guarantees exactly one is non-null, and `kind` is in the key anyway. New guard `channel_task_child_workspace_guard`, because the v1 guard resolves the parent through `NEW.channel_id` and this table's parent is a THREAD; PUBLIC/anon/authenticated EXECUTE revoked on it (an inert grant trips the Supabase advisor). **NOT in the realtime publication** — a participant set changes rarely and the web refetches it on thread events, so publishing it would re-create the F-072 read-amplification shape for no live benefit (the same call `20260728010000` made when it pulled `channel_tasks` back out). Storage keeps the `task` spelling on purpose (§8's storage-boundary rule); the domain type is `ThreadParticipant`.

**Server lane.** `server/service-agents.ts` (211) + `agent-names.ts` (the curated mineral/star pool, `NAME_PICK_ATTEMPTS = 3` retry on unique violation) + `repository-agents.ts` / `agents-dto.ts`; `server/service-participants.ts` (355) + `repository-participants.ts`; `server/service-writes-agents.ts` (133) for addressing. `service.ts` (87) is a pure re-export facade — **nothing is enforced there**; do not read it as the gate.

- **Ownership is enforced in ONE helper, and it does not cover create.** `loadOwnedAgent` (`service-agents.ts`) throws `ChannelAgentNotFoundError` when the agent is missing or belongs to another channel, then `ChannelAgentForbiddenError` when `owner_user_id !== ctx.userId`. `renameAgent` and `setAgentStatus` both route through it. **`createAgent` does NOT and must not be described as owner-only** — it is gated on channel membership and the caller BECOMES the owner.
- **THE CURATION RULE — the fix for the wave's worst hole.** Only a thread's `created_by`, its `target_user_id`, or an existing **`kind:"user"`** participant may ADD a row (`isThreadCurator`); everyone else is 403 `TASK_FORBIDDEN`, **including a caller adding only themselves — there is no self-join exemption and drop-in join is a product decision nobody made.** The curator check is raised BEFORE `assertIdentityBelongs`, so the 400/201 split cannot be used to probe membership. **Agent ownership does NOT confer curation** (product call): owning an agent in the set does not make you a curator, for the same reason `mayWriteThread` refuses to infer a human's write access from a process they started. **Ejecting is narrower than admitting** (`mayRemoveIdentity`): removing yourself, or an agent you own, always works — nobody is held in a room — but removing anyone else is the two original parties' call, not any participant's. Leaving is idempotent. Channel membership is a **precondition, never the permission**: a participant must already be able to read the room (else you create a writer who cannot see what they write into), and an agent participant must belong to THIS channel (an agent from another room has no listener here).
- **Addressing (`service-writes-agents.ts`).** `toAgent` accepts an id **or** a handle, must resolve inside this channel, and sets `toAgentId` + `toAgentOwnerUserId`. It is **membership-checked** — the owner is run through the same `findMembership` predicate `postMessage` applies to an explicit `toUserId`, throwing `ChannelAddresseeNotMemberError` otherwise. **THE OWNER BRIDGE, stated exactly:** `service-writes-metadata.ts` computes `toUserId = agents.toAgentOwnerUserId ?? input.toUserId ?? peerUserId`, i.e. addressing an agent **also stamps its owner as `to_user_id`, and that stamp WINS over an explicit caller `to`.** This is what lets a machine that only understands `to_user_id` route an agent-addressed message, and it is why the desktop's escalation rule needs the `to_agent_id` conjunct (§18) — a message FOR my agent also names ME. `asAgent` sets `metadata.author_agent_id` and is ownership-verified: `ChannelAgentForbiddenError` → **403**. Naming differs by surface on purpose: MCP params `to_agent`/`as_agent`, HTTP schema fields `toAgent`/`authorAgentId`, metadata keys `to_agent_id`/`author_agent_id`.
- **The participant-aware thread-write gate — `mayWriteThread` (`service-writes-metadata-thread.ts` since the 2026-08-01 §2 split; it lived in `service-writes-metadata.ts` when this was written).** Two regimes, split on "does this thread have rows in `channel_task_participants`". **Zero rows → the pair gate, verdict-for-verdict unchanged** (`isThreadParticipant`: creator or target, nothing else) — it is the same predicate, though the path now pays one unconditional `listParticipantsByTask` read to learn the set is empty. **Rows present → the set is ADDITIVE, not governing:** creator and target keep access regardless of the set (a room that admitted collaborators must not evict the person who opened the exchange), and on top of them a `kind:"user"` row admits the human, a `kind:"agent"` row admits a post **only when the caller SUPPLIED `as_agent`** naming that agent. **The agent half requires the caller to CLAIM the identity, never infers it from ownership** — inferring would mean "any member who owns any agent in the set may post as themselves", which quietly widens a human's write access. Scope: `mayWriteThread` is reached **only** on the caller-supplied UUID branch; the legacy branch and the DM inheritance branch never consult participants. **What keeps an ordinary thread's authorization fixed is the CURATION RULE, not "participants are only seeded at create"** — that was true of `createTask` and said nothing about the join route, which is exactly how a bystander could once write themselves a set and be admitted here.
- **The legacy-id gate is CLOSED, and this is what unblocked the history fence.** `isLegacyThreadParticipant` (same 2026-08-01 §2 split — now `service-writes-metadata-thread.ts`) is the ONE resolver: it pins the prefix to this channel, requires a positive safe-integer seq, and compares the opening message's `{author_user_id, metadata.to_user_id}` against the caller. A non-participant's tag is **`delete metadata.taskId` and the post proceeds untagged — it never throws**, because installed desktop 1.7.16 posts legacy ids for lifecycle events. **The calm-terminal flags now follow the SAME tag decision** rather than re-running the check (`calmFlags.length > 0 && typeof metadata.taskId === "string"`), so a fabricated outcome has nothing to attach to and there is exactly one lookup per post.
- **Five new error codes + mappings** (`server/errors.ts`, `http-mapping.ts`, 1:1): `CHANNEL_AGENT_NOT_FOUND` **404** · `CHANNEL_AGENT_NOT_IN_CHANNEL` **400** · `CHANNEL_AGENT_NAME_CONFLICT` **409** · `CHANNEL_AGENT_FORBIDDEN` **403** · `CHANNEL_PARTICIPANT_NOT_MEMBER` **400**.
- **Routes.** `GET|POST /api/channels/[channelId]/agents` (list; summon, `minRole:"member"`), `PATCH /api/channels/[channelId]/agents/[agentId]` (one handler, discriminated on `op: "rename" | "set_status"`), and `POST|DELETE /api/channels/[channelId]/tasks/[taskId]/participants` (both `minRole:"member"`; **no `GET` by design** — the set rides the thread read). The `/tasks` path segment is still the storage name.

**MCP lane** (`packages/mcp-server/src/tools/`). The registrar stayed thin per the §2 pattern: new siblings `channel-ops-agents.ts` (handlers), `channel-agent-refs.ts` (resolution), `channel-schema.ts` + `channel-description.ts` (split out of `channel.ts`), `tool-group-files.ts`.

- **Six new `dopl_channel` ops**, appended after the existing twelve: `agents` · `summon_agent` · `rename_agent` · `set_agent_status` · `join_thread` · `leave_thread`.
- **`post` gained `to_agent` / `as_agent`; `create_thread` gained `participants`** — an array (max 20) of prefixed strings `"agent:<handle>"` / `"user:<email-or-id>"`, resolved **against the CHANNEL roster** (`listChannelAgents` / `listChannelMembers`), explicitly NOT `resolveMemberOr`, which reads the WORKSPACE roster. That distinction is the whole of blocker 4 — see below.
- **`as_agent` is REFUSED on `create_thread`, not dropped** (`channel.ts`, before `opCreateThread` runs). Justified and worth keeping: a thread's opening message is the OPERATOR's request, and an agent-attributed opener would classify as a notify-only escalation and **never wake the responder**. The error tells the caller to open the thread plainly and then post inside it with `as_agent`.
- **Per-call agent locus in `_dopl_status`.** The `caller:` line gained ` · as \`<handle>\` (\`<agent-id>\`)` when a call carried `as_agent`. It is PER CALL, not per session — the tag is attached to the response and stripped in `server.ts` before render. Pinned by `channel-locus.test.ts`, including a non-leak case.
- **THE LAW block in `CHANNEL_DESCRIPTION`** (`channel-description.ts`) — seven bullets an agent cannot derive from the op list. The load-bearing one: *"ADDRESSING A HUMAN (`to=`) DOES ONE OF TWO THINGS AND `as_agent` DECIDES WHICH. Post AS YOURSELF — `as_agent` together with `to=` — and that person is NOTIFIED and no agent of theirs starts. Post WITHOUT `as_agent` and the same `to=` is a REQUEST from your operator: it triggers that member's listener, which is what starts their agent. There is no third case."* The `post` op bullet states the identical conditional, and so does `to_agent`'s param prose. **If you edit one, edit all three** — they were out of step once and it shipped.
- **`channel-law.test.ts` scans BY SENTENCE, and has a meta-test.** The negative guard used to grep whole LINES for three exact phrases, which is sentence-blind: a paragraph could carry a true clause and a false one and pass. It now splits on `(?<=[.!?])\s+` and requires any sentence that pairs "the other side" with "starts/triggers" to also be KEYED on `as_agent`. The meta-test feeds it the exact false sentence that actually shipped and asserts the guard catches it — **do not delete that test when refactoring the scanner; it is the only thing that proves the scanner works.**

**Web lane.** `agent-chips-bar.tsx` (live status off the realtime publication), `mention-popup.tsx` + `lib/mention.ts` (@-autocomplete), `lib/composer-commands.ts` (`/new-agent`), `rooms-sidebar.tsx` + `lib/rooms.ts`, `threads-button.tsx`, `notify-scope-button.tsx` (**deleted 2026-08-08, F-170**), `hooks/use-channel-agents.ts`, `lib/agent-display.ts` (agent-authored attribution chips).

**The four review blockers this wave shipped WITH, and their fixes, are F-110 in `REFACTOR-FINDINGS.md`** — read it before touching the curation rule, the durable team-agent count, THE LAW's wording, or `create_thread`'s participant seeding. Its residuals list is the honest inventory of what is still open.

#### MULTIPLAYER v2 — CHAT vs REQUEST, engagement, multi-address (2026-07-31, F-111) — ⛔ SUPERSEDED IN PART

> **⛔ MOSTLY SUPERSEDED by the channels rollback (2026-08-05, F-141 — "PHASE 2 OF THE ROLLBACK" below). Exactly ONE of the four lines below survived.**
>
> **SURVIVED:** `intent` chat-vs-request, and the LOOP BRAKE. Those two are live law and are stated again in the rollback bullets.
> **DELETED:** engagement in every form (`channel_agents.engaged_at` / `engaged_by` have writers nowhere, `main/channel-engagement.js` is gone, `removeMember` sweeps nothing), multi-address (`to_agent_ids`), the OWNER BRIDGE that filled `to_user_id` from the first addressed agent's owner, and the thread PARTICIPANT SET that this section calls "the standing consent". A thread is its creator and its target, full stop.
>
> Kept whole for the same reason as the subsection above: the rollback bullets name what they removed.

**THE LAW ABOVE IS AMENDED, NOT REPLACED, AND THE AMENDMENT IS THE WHOLE POINT.** "Nothing acts unless addressed" shipped and did not survive its first real use. It could not express a human talking to a human, it made the operator re-`@` on every turn, and its own sentence about threads ("inside a thread everyone in it hears everything") was false on the desktop. State the model this way now:

> *(Lines 2 and 3 are DELETED MODEL; lines 1 and 4 are still law — see the banner.)*
> A post is **CHAT** or a **REQUEST**. Chat is people talking: it addresses nobody and starts nobody.
> Addressing an agent **ENGAGES** it. While engaged it also acts on that human's UNTAGGED, non-chat messages in that channel — a human tags to START an exchange, not on every turn.
> Inside a **THREAD**, the participant set is the standing consent and nobody tags anybody.
> **THE LOOP BRAKE IS ABSOLUTE AND UNCHANGED: an agent-authored message engages nobody and starts nobody, at any N.**

- **`intent: "chat" | "request"` (`schema.ts:172-175`, `.optional()` at `:250`).** **There is NO default and nothing ever writes or compares the string `"request"`** — the whole feature is negative branching on `input.intent === "chat"`, so absence is UNTREATED rather than defaulted, and every pre-existing caller is byte-identical. `metadata.intent` is stamped ONLY when supplied (`service-writes-metadata.ts:361`), and `intent` joined the reserved-key strip list at `:355`. **`chat` does not resolve the DM peer and then discard it — it never resolves one** (`:382-386`: the `resolveDirectPeer` call is inside the ternary). `chat` + a HUMAN `to` is a self-contradiction and is refused **400 `CHANNEL_CHAT_ADDRESSED`** (`assertChatIsUnaddressed`, `service-writes-agents.ts`), raised BEFORE the `clientMsgId` idempotency short-circuit so a retry cannot be handed the stored contradiction as a success. **`chat` + agent mentions is ALLOWED by design** — the guard tests only `input.toUserId`. Tagging an agent is not requesting a person, and chat+`toAgents` is the composer's PRIMARY shape for giving an agent work. (`chat` + `summary` is likewise not a contradiction and passes.)
- **MULTI-ADDRESS — `toAgents[]` → `metadata.to_agent_ids[]`, and the scalar is a COMPAT MIRROR.** `to_agent_id` is still stamped as element 0 (`service-writes-agents.ts`) because **installed desktop 1.7.17 reads only the scalar** and has no concept of the array — drop the mirror and every addressed message goes invisible on the build in the field. The cap is `MAX_ADDRESSED_AGENTS` (**8**, `schema.ts:200`) applied to the **DEDUPED MERGE** of `toAgent` + `toAgents`, before resolution ⇒ 400 `CHANNEL_TOO_MANY_AGENTS`. The MCP lane declares its own `MAX_ADDRESSED_AGENTS = 8` (`channel-addressing.ts:85`) — a deliberate duplicate for the same reason `GROUP_CHANNEL_MIN_MEMBERS` is one (no shared source tree), pinned to the web constant by `channel-addressing-rule.test.ts`. **THE OWNER BRIDGE OUTRANKS AN EXPLICIT `to`:** `toUserId = agents.toAgentOwnerUserId ?? input.toUserId ?? peerUserId` (`service-writes-metadata.ts:398`), so setting both `to` and `to_agent` addresses the AGENT'S OWNER, not the member in `to`. `metadata.to_user_id` remains a single scalar; multi-address on it is still not built.
  - **`MAX_DERIVED_AGENTS` now reads `MAX_ADDRESSED_AGENTS` rather than restating `8` (`service-thread-handshake.ts`), and the bug it closes is exactly why a "they happen to agree" duplicate is not a duplicate.** The post path capped `toAgents` ALONE, so `toAgent` + eight made NINE addressed agents; the ninth was engaged and woken by the address, then truncated out of the handshake derivation and 403'd out of the thread it had been woken for. The merged cap holds at the post and the handshake reads the same constant.
- **ENGAGEMENT — `channel_agents.engaged_at` / `engaged_by`.** Migration `20260731140000_channel_agent_engagement` (additive: two nullable columns, two `COMMENT ON COLUMN`, one partial index) and `20260731150000_drop_channel_agents_engaged_idx`, which **drops the index the migration one before it created** — it was added on the reasoning that "the engaged set of a room is read per channel" and nothing reads it that way; the only engagement-column filter is `clearEngagementByEngager` on `(channel_id, engaged_by)`, a write. Both applied live. Read the drop migration's header before adding an index here.
  - **`engaged_at` IS A FACT; EXPIRY IS A POLICY, AND THE SERVER DOES NOT ENFORCE IT.** No sweep, no cron, no derived `isEngaged` on the wire, no server read that filters on it. `ENGAGEMENT_TTL_MS` = **60 min**, defined once at `src/features/channels/constants.ts:105`. **It is applied by BOTH CLIENTS, not "on the desktop"** — anywhere a comment says only the desktop applies it (including migration `20260731140000`'s own header) it is half right. The web's `lib/agent-engagement.ts` is what decides the chip's engaged/idle split; the desktop **re-declares its own copy** (`main/channel-engagement.js`) because it is a separate package that cannot import from the Next app. The two disagree by ONE MILLISECOND on the future-stamp/clock-skew arm (web exclusive, desktop inclusive); cosmetic, recorded so nobody re-derives it as a bug.
  - **ADDRESSING BY A HUMAN ENGAGES. `{op:"disengage"}` is OWNER **or** ENGAGER** (`service-agents.ts`; MCP op name is `disengage_agent`). **Park and dismiss CLEAR it** server-side (`DISENGAGING_STATUSES`, folded into the same UPDATE at `repository-agents.ts:117-127`), and leaving a channel clears what you engaged (`clearDepartedEngagement`, `service-writes-members.ts:152`, fail-soft, after the delete).
  - **THE ENGAGEMENT BRAKE KEYS ON THE CREDENTIAL, AND THIS WAS A REAL HOLE.** `recordAgentEngagement` (`service-writes-agents.ts`) returns on `ctx.source === "agent"` FIRST. `ctx.source` is derived from the credential (`buildChannelContext`: `auth.agentTokenId ? "agent" : "user"`) and there is no field a caller can set to change it. **`authorKind` is a CALLER-ASSERTABLE display/routing claim** (§8 v1.1 says so and it is load-bearing elsewhere), so keying engagement on it made the loop brake defeatable by one word: an agent-token caller posted `{toAgent:"quartz", authorKind:"user"}`, quartz was engaged for 60 minutes, and the agent then drove it with untagged lines carrying the same assertable label — two agents sustaining each other with no human at any hop. **A test pinned the old behaviour as intended before it was recognised as a hole** (`service-agents-engagement.test.ts` says so in its own header) — which is the thing to notice: the suite was green throughout. **The `authorKind` and `author_agent_id` checks STAY as defence in depth**; they answer a different question and catch a case the credential cannot — an operator's COOKIE session (`source: "user"`) posting on an agent's behalf, and installed desktop 1.7.17, which posts agent replies with `authorKind:"agent"` and NO `author_agent_id` (agents are newer than it).
  - **KNOWN KNOCK-ON, and it is intended: MCP posts are agent-credentialed, so `to_agent` over MCP addresses, wakes and routes exactly as before but no longer ENGAGES.** "Addressing engages" is true of HUMANS only. Say it that way in agent-facing copy.
  - **WHO IS RECORDED AS THE ENGAGER IS THE MESSAGE'S AUTHOR, NOT THE CALLER** — identical on a first attempt, different on an idempotent replay, and the engagement belongs to whoever actually addressed the agents.
- **@MENTIONS ARE REAL ADDRESSES NOW.** Before this they were decorative text: the composer had autocomplete and nothing resolved the tokens at send time, so the operator's core flow ("@quartz do X") did nothing at all. `extractMentionedAgents` (`lib/mention.ts`) resolves handles out of the COMPOSED BODY at send time against the CHANNEL roster — backtick-blanked (`CODE_SPAN_RE`), **whitespace-anchored** (`/(?:^|\s)@([A-Za-z0-9-]+)/g`, so an email address can never be a mention), case-folded, trailing-hyphen-trimmed, deduped, capped at 8 — and sends `toAgents`. **It resolves only ADDRESSABLE agents (`summoned | active`); the SERVER deliberately does not check status at all** (`service-writes-agents.ts`), so `@parked-agent` is inert from the web and accepted over the API. That divergence is an unmade product call, not a bug on either side.
- **THE HANDSHAKE KEY IS REWRITTEN ONTO THE RESOLVED CHANNEL ID, and refusing would have been worse than rewriting.** `opCreateThread` resolves its `channel` param from a slug OR a uuid; `normalizeHandshakeKey` (`channel-handshake-key.ts`) then re-mints a `thread-open-…` `client_msg_id` as `thread-open-${ch.id}-${seq}`, splitting the LAST digit run as the seq (greedy `^(.+)-(\d+)$` — a UUID contains hyphens, the same reason `parseLegacyTaskSeq` is written that way) and discarding whatever the caller put in the middle. **Without it, two agents given the same instruction — one holding the slug, one holding the uuid — mint DIFFERENT keys, land on different entries of the partial unique index, and open TWO THREADS for one instruction**, which is the exact outcome the handshake exists to prevent. A `thread-open-` key with no digit tail is REFUSED, not repaired, and a rewrite is always reported back in the result (`channel-ops-threads.ts:236-242`), never silent.
- **THE PARTICIPANT SET OF A HANDSHAKE THREAD IS DERIVED SERVER-SIDE (`service-thread-handshake.ts`), and the module assumes the protocol is ignored.** The MCP lane teaches agents that exactly one of them opens (the addressed agent whose AGENT ID sorts lexicographically first), but **a rule an agent has to remember is a rule the third model that reads it will break**, so the server holds instead: the create's handshake key names a `seq` in this channel, the message at that seq is the human's instruction, and its **server-stamped** `metadata.to_agent_ids` is the address list. The derived set is every addressed agent + every addressed agent's OWNER + the instruction's author, each re-validated through `identityBelongs`. **It cannot be forged** (`to_agent_ids` is reserved and re-stamped only from validated fields) and **it is not a curation bypass** — it seeds at CREATE time on the thread the call created or converged on, and a caller who is not the instruction's author and owns none of the addressed agents is not in the derived set at all. **EVERY MISS IS SILENT AND SEEDS NOTHING**: a non-handshake key, a seq with no message, a message that addressed nobody, a deleted agent, an owner who has left. Seeding fewer identities is always the SAFE direction, and a `client_msg_id` is caller-supplied and unverifiable, so it must never make a create fail.
  - Why an addressed agent brings its OWNER in as a user participant: the agent runs on the owner's machine and every message it posts carries the owner as `author_user_id`, so the owner is the identity the write gate actually sees. Seeding the agent alone would admit it in name and refuse it on any post that did not also claim `authorAgentId`.
- **`read` AND `await` NOW RENDER AGENT ADDRESSING**, which is the precondition for the whole handshake rule — an agent could not previously see that it had been co-addressed. `agentAddressTag` (`channel-render-agents.ts`) appends `` · to agents `quartz` (`agent-1`) `` to the message head; when the roster read fails or a handle is unknown it degrades to the **bare id with no parens**, not to silence. An agent-only address also suppresses the old `· unaddressed` marker (`channel-render.ts:283-287`).
  - **`AWAIT_UNNAMED_NOTICE`'s predicate was WRONG for exactly the case multi-address created.** It tested `to_user_id` alone, and the owner bridge stamps that from the FIRST addressed agent's owner — so on `"@quartz @onyx work on X"`, onyx's machine woke to a message naming its own agent by handle and was told *"NONE of the messages above NAMES you as its addressee"*, i.e. instructed not to act on the job it had just been handed. It now also asks whether any addressed agent id is MINE (`channel-ops-await.ts:307-313`). **Fail-soft is preserved:** a failed roster read leaves `agents.mine` empty, which makes the notice fire exactly as before rather than suppressing on a guess.
- **THE LAW's NEGATIVE GUARD GENERALIZED — and it was ADDED BESIDE the old one, not rewritten in place.** The original `to=`/spawning guard is intact (`channel-law.test.ts:319-332`). A SECOND guard sits next to it whose stated form is the general rule: **"a sentence must not promise the READER an effect the reader cannot cause"** (`:356-358`) — the rule that would have caught four of this session's six agent-facing lies. Its teeth-test pins the exact sentence that shipped (*"To pick the exchange back up, address it by handle again — that re-engages it."* — false for an agent reader, since engagement requires a human credential). **What is ENFORCED is still a narrow regex pair** (`ENGAGES` × `HUMAN_KEYED`); the general form lives in the docblock. Do not read the docblock as coverage.
  - **Trap in that file: the test named `keeps the law to at most 12 BULLETS` does not measure bullets.** It counts non-blank LINES including the `THE LAW OF THIS ROOM` header (`:277`), so the real bullet ceiling is **11**, and the law is at 11 today. New length guards are real and do work: `LAW.length <= 4000` (actual 3650) and `<= 900` per bullet (longest 722).
- **THE `channel-` FILENAME PREFIX IS LOAD-BEARING IN `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/`, in BOTH directions.** `toolGroupFiles` (`tool-group-files.ts:39-47`) is a runtime `readdirSync` scan — not eslint, not a size test — that treats every non-`.test.ts` file starting `channel-` as CHANNEL TOOL SOURCE, and two invariant suites read it: `parity.test.ts` (every declared schema param appears in the source; every `args.X` the handler reads is a declared param) and `channel-deadlines.test.ts` (no stray `240_000`). **A real op module MUST carry the prefix** — `channel-render-agents.ts`, `channel-post-notes.ts` and `channel-ops-open.ts` each say so in their headers. **A TEST HARNESS MUST NOT**, which is why the two new fixture files are `agent-addressing-fixtures.ts` / `agent-ops-fixtures.ts` and not `channel-*`. Stated precisely, because the tempting version is wrong: leaking today's fixtures would break NO test — the hazard is **fail-open masking** of a described-but-dead schema param at `parity.test.ts`, plus a false failure the first time a fixture writes `args.something`. The prefix decision is deliberate; the breakage is latent.
- **New server modules.** `service-writes-members.ts` (193) — channel membership writes split out of `service-writes.ts` (`addMember` / `removeMember` / `updateMyMemberSettings` + the private `clearDepartedEngagement`). `service-thread-handshake.ts` (229) — the handshake seq parse + participant derivation above. **New MCP modules:** `channel-handshake-key.ts` (137), `channel-render-agents.ts` (128, split off `channel-render.ts` at the cap), `channel-post-notes.ts` (167, the result lines that narrate what a post's addressing actually did) and `channel-ops-open.ts` (126, `open` + `invite`, split off `channel-ops-write.ts` when the notes extraction alone left it at **503**). Test splits: `channel-thread-participants.test.ts` (284), `channel-ops-participants.test.ts` (219).

**Everything this wave shipped WITH is F-111 in `REFACTOR-FINDINGS.md`.** Read its residuals before touching `to_user_notify`, the web/server disengage asymmetry, or the auto-posture bound on two agents in one thread.

#### SESSION IDENTITY, CLOSED THREADS, AD-HOC IDS (2026-08-01, F-113/F-114/F-115)

- **`session_id` IS A RESERVED METADATA KEY, ON EXACTLY THE `runtime` / `appVersion` TERMS (F-113).** The reserved-key strip list is now `to_user_id`, `summary`, `runtime`, `appVersion`, **`session_id`**, `to_user_notify`, `to_agent_id`, `to_agent_ids`, `author_agent_id`, `intent`, `handoff`, the **six** calm flags (the five plus `session_ended`), the two close-proposal keys (`closeProposed`, `closeOutcome`), **`threadReopened`**, and the four `task*` keys. *(Corrected 2026-08-08 — this list had drifted five keys behind the code, which the channels audit flagged as docs drift. The MARKER subset — the calm flags, the close-proposal keys and the reopen marker, i.e. every reserved key whose job is to change how a CARD READS — now lives in `service-writes-metadata-markers.ts`, which states the rule they share once; `service-writes-metadata.ts` applies it.)* Header-only input (`X-Dopl-Session-Id` → `src/shared/auth/session-header.ts` → `WorkspaceAuthContext.sessionId` → `buildChannelContext`'s re-narrow → `ChannelContext.sessionId` → `resolvePostMetadata`), shape-checked at BOTH boundaries (id characters only, no whitespace, ≤128 — it is rendered into a message line on another member's screen), and **absent header ⇒ no key at all.** `/api/mcp` forwards the caller's header onto the loopback the same way it forwards `runtime`; `DoplTransport` carries it as `sessionId` and lists it reserved so `customHeaders` cannot clobber it. **WHY IT EXISTS:** one `channel_agents` row can be claimed by any number of processes holding the owner's credential — `as_agent` is per-call and ownership-checked ONLY — and on the desktop that is by design: `slotKey` gives a ROOM slot `(channel, agent)` and a PAIR slot `(channel, thread)` disjoint key spaces, so several live sessions of one handle is documented behaviour, not a race. Two of them gave a peer contradictory instructions 79s apart and nothing could attribute either. **IT IS A LABEL, NOT A LOCK, and that is a locked decision:** nothing enforces one live session per agent id (it would break the three-slot design and would not touch an external CLI session passing `as_agent`), and nothing may GATE on the value — it is a routing/forensic hint on the same footing as `runtime`. Desktop half: `withSessionStamp(options.mcpServers, store.slotKey(s))` in `main/session-query.js`, kept OUT of `buildMcpServers` because that builder is shared with the one headless spawn-config file, which has no session to name. **The trap for §5's "agent identity is NOT 1:1 with session":** it is still true — the stamp names a SLOT, so two SEQUENTIAL sessions in one slot stamp the same value.
- **A CLOSED THREAD ACCEPTS THE POST AND SAYS SO — WARN, NEVER REFUSE (F-114).** `resolvePostMetadata` returns `{ metadata, threadClosed }` (`isThreadClosed`, `service-writes-metadata-thread.ts`), `postMessage` attaches `threadClosed: true` to its return ONLY when true, and `POST /messages` answers `{ message, threadClosed }` — an ADDITIVE ENVELOPE KEY beside the message, the shape `echoSeq` already uses, so the stored row is untouched and a READ can never carry it. `@dopl/client` normalizes an absent key to `false`. The MCP `post` result then carries `closedThreadNote` and `close_thread`'s result (plus `CHANNEL_DESCRIPTION`) says closing stops the thread's **PASSIVE routing**, not that it seals it — true since the same-day F-116 desktop gate; an explicitly ADDRESSED agent still hears you inside a closed thread. **A 403 IS THE WRONG FIX AND WAS REJECTED:** it breaks the legitimate "one last word after the close echo" pattern, and its only remedy — `reopen` — has NO MCP counterpart (web-only), so the refusal would point an agent at an action it cannot take. An idempotent REPLAY does not carry the flag (it re-resolves nothing). **A LEGACY tag can never warn**: no row, no status.
- **A `task-<channelId>-<seq>` ID IS RENDERED AS AN AD-HOC EXCHANGE, NEVER AS A THREAD (F-115).** The MECHANISM is unchanged in every respect — deterministic ids from `(channel, seq)`, the server-side strip of a tag that is not the poster's own exchange, the desktop's UUID gate on the thread lane. Only the LABEL moved, and it moved because the two were indistinguishable in `read`/`await` lines, in the legend, and in the post's linkage note — which told an agent to "continue this thread with `thread=<id>`", an instruction the server would then silently strip. `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-render-threads.ts` is the ONE place that decides: `isFirstClassThreadId` (a UUID, the same test `resolvePostMetadata` and `targeting.firstClassTaskId` gate on) picks `· thread <tag>` vs `· ad-hoc <tag>`, the legend splits into a threads line (which keeps "continue one with `thread=`") and an ad-hoc line (which points at `create_thread` and says outright there is no row behind the id), and `channel-post-linkage.ts` reads the same predicate so the write lane cannot disagree with the read lane. **`shortRef` prints the trailing SEQ for a synthetic id**: every one in a channel begins `task-` + the same channel uuid, so a blind `slice(0,8)` collapses distinct exchanges onto one tag. It is `seq 345`, not `#345`, because `neutralizeInline` strips `#`. **Nothing on the desktop renders one to a user** (`renderer/**` only passes ids as arguments), so there is no desktop half.
- **MCP schema minimums mirror the route (F-115/F5).** `body` / `client_msg_id` / `title` gained `.min(1)`, `to_agent` and each `to_agents` item gained `.max(64)` — all four used to pass the tool and die at the route as an opaque 400 the write ops mis-narrated. **`summary` is NOT split and its declared 2000 stays**: one param serves two routes with two caps and the schema declares the LOOSER so a legitimate `close_thread` summary is never refused client-side; the tighter 200 is in the `.describe()`. `.trim()` is added only where the route trims before measuring — adding it to an agent ref would change the bytes SENT.

**Everything these three shipped WITH is F-113 / F-114 / F-115 in `REFACTOR-FINDINGS.md`, and the same-day adversarial review round that corrected their copy and enforced the closed-thread routing claim is F-116.** Read the residuals before adding a `reopen` op, before treating `session_id` as a run id, or before auditing the web UI for the same ad-hoc label defect.

### MCP credits — WHY THE COUNTER IS A CAS ROW, NOT A `COUNT` (2026-08-11)

The obvious build was "count this period's rows in `mcp_tool_calls`". It was rejected on three measurements, and the reasons are worth keeping because each one also disqualifies the next analytics table somebody reaches for.

- **`mcp_tool_calls` DOES NOT COUNT TOOL CALLS.** It counts LOOPBACK REQUESTS: one MCP tool call makes 0..N of them (`dopl_map` fans out, a `workspace=` arg adds a `listWorkspaces`, `dopl_channel(op="await")` polls ~4x). The 2148 rows live on that day were not 2148 tool calls. A meter built on it would have overbilled the tools that fan out and undercharged the ones that do not — invisibly, because both look like usage.
- **ITS WRITER IS ALLOWED TO LOSE WRITES.** `analytics/server/mcp-tool-calls.ts › logMcpToolCall` is `void`-ed, fire-and-forget, and swallows every error BY DESIGN — correct for analytics, disqualifying for billing. A counter you cannot bill from is not a counter.
- **A `COUNT(*)` OVER A GROWING TABLE IS ON THE HOTTEST PATH IN THE PRODUCT.** §12's "do not tax the hot write path to answer a question a cold reader asks once a day" cuts exactly here.

So: one row per `(workspace_id, period_start)` in `workspace_credit_usage`, spent through `consume_workspace_credits` — a SINGLE `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE … WHERE used + amount <= limit RETURNING` statement (`20260811130000_mcp_credits.sql`). Three details are load-bearing:

- **Modelled on `claim_workspace_checkout` (20260720210814), NOT on `check_and_record_rate_limit_subject` (20260608010000).** The rate limiter takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock`; the checkout claim's header records why that shape was abandoned here — **an earlier advisory-lock attempt leaked locks through PgBouncer transaction pooling.** A single-statement CAS is cross-instance correct with no lock to leak.
- **BOTH limit paths need a guard.** The `ON CONFLICT … WHERE` clause guards only the UPDATE; the fresh INSERT is guarded by `IF p_amount <= p_limit` in the function body. Without it, a workspace on a zero-credit plan gets its first call free — the classic version of this bug.
- **UPSERT, NOT UPDATE, for the same reason the checkout claim needed it:** most workspaces have no `workspace_billing` row at all until their first paid event, and none has a credit row until its first tool call.

**The period SELF-ROLLS; there is no reset cron.** The resolved `period_start` is stamped on the row, so the first consume of a new window simply inserts a new row and the old one is never touched again. `features/billing/credits.ts › resolveCreditPeriod` picks the subscription anchor when both `workspace_billing` period columns are stamped and the end is still in the future, else the UTC calendar month — the fallback is what keeps a lapsed subscription from pinning a workspace to a window that never advances. `current_period_start` had to be ADDED (only the end was ever stored); `cancel_at_period_end` rode the same migration and the same webhook write site.

**FAIL OPEN, decided rather than inherited.** The two existing precedents point opposite ways — `checkAndRecordRateLimitSubject` fails CLOSED, `logMcpToolCall` fails OPEN — so the direction had to be chosen. A credits gate that fails closed on a DB blip refuses every tool call in the product and tells the operator they are out of credits when they are not. Exhaustion is the ONLY hard block; the decision is stated at both halves of the seam (`/api/mcp/credits/consume` and `registrar.ts › createCreditedRunner`).

**Meta-tools (`current_workspace`, `list_workspaces`) are exempt, deliberately.** They are how a lost agent finds out where it is and what it may pass as `workspace=`, they are user-scoped rather than workspace-scoped, and on a 0/2+-membership session there is no workspace to charge. F-146 already notes that `registerMetaTool` bypasses the wrapper by construction; that is the mechanism, not the reason.

**Unrelated to the DELETED `credit_ledger` / `user_credits` feature** removed by hand outside migration history (see §7's baseline notes). Nothing was revived; this is a new, per-workspace, per-period counter.

### The billing page split — USAGE vs BILLING, and what came in-app (2026-08-11)

`/billing/[segment]` grew a card, an invoice table and a cancel flow the same week it grew a credit meter. Four decisions are worth keeping.

- **TWO TABS ON ONE ROUTE, VIA `?tab=` — NOT A SECOND PATH LEVEL.** `[segment]` is the WORKSPACE segment (`{slug}-{publicId}`), so `/billing/[segment]/usage` would have meant re-deriving every helper in `features/billing/url.ts`, every `upgrade_url` envelope already in the wild, every Stripe `return_url`, and `dopl-desktop-app/main/deep-link-target.js`'s hand-copied page table (pinned by its own test). A query param touches none of them and is still shareable. The resolver is a pure module of its own — `features/billing/billing-tabs.ts › resolveBillingTab` — **deliberately not in `url.ts`**, which is the money-URL BUILDER and must not gain a param no `return_url` carries. The RSC page calls it so the link decides the FIRST paint; the client shell holds it as state and syncs the address bar with `history.replaceState` (a router push would re-run a `force-dynamic` RSC and remount an open checkout form to change one word).
- **THE DEFAULT FLIPS ON INTENT, NOT ON ROLE.** A bare visit opens Usage; any recognised `?billing=` (upgrade / success / return) opens Billing, because all three mean the visitor was sent here mid-transaction by a 402 envelope, a checkout return or the portal.
- **READ THE CARD IN-APP, EDIT IT IN STRIPE.** Brand/last4/expiry answers the question people open billing for and needs no PCI surface of ours; CHANGING a card does, so "Update" stays the hosted-portal handoff every other card affordance uses. Same split for invoices: the table is ours, the PDF is Stripe's. The portal POST + redirect is now one hook (`components/use-billing-portal.ts`) because a second surface needed it.
- **CANCEL SETS `cancel_at_period_end` AND WRITES THE LOCAL ROW IN THE SAME REQUEST.** Never `subscriptions.cancel` — an immediate cancel revokes access already paid for. The local write is not optimism: Stripe's `customer.subscription.updated` echo arrives seconds later through a different process, and the person who just clicked has to be told NOW when access ends. `lastStripeEventCreated` is deliberately NOT stamped, or the real event would look stale and be dropped (same rule as `upgrade-to-team`). Resume is the identical call with `false`, which is why it is one route and not two. `sessionOnly: true` on it, like every other billing write — and unlike the portal handoff, this one ends the subscription on our own route with no Stripe-hosted page a human must click through.

---

## 9. API Routes

### Shape

Every route handler is ≤ 80 lines. If longer, you're doing business logic inline — extract to `service.ts`.

```ts
// src/app/api/<feature>/<action>/route.ts
import { NextResponse } from "next/server";
import { withUserAuth } from "@/shared/auth/with-auth";
import { parseJson } from "@/shared/api/parse-json";
import { <Action>Schema } from "@/features/<name>/schema";
import { <action> } from "@/features/<name>/server/service";

export const POST = withUserAuth(async (req, { userId }) => {
  const input = await parseJson(req, <Action>Schema);
  const result = await <action>(input, userId);
  return NextResponse.json(result);
});
```

### Auth wrappers (reuse — do not reinvent)

Auth wrappers live in `src/shared/auth/`:

- `withUserAuth(handler)` (`with-auth.ts`) — same, plus injects `{ userId, agentTokenId?, apiKeyWorkspaceId?, params }`.
- `withMcpAccess(action, handler)` (`with-auth.ts`) — composes `withUserAuth`; paywall-gates MCP (bearer) callers, logs analytics.
- `withWorkspaceAuth(handler, { minRole, workspaceIdFromQuery, writeScopeExempt, sessionOnly })` (`with-workspace-auth.ts`) — composes `withUserAuth`; resolves the active workspace **fail-closed** (MCP-2, see §Workspace resolution below), verifies membership + `meetsMinRole`. The default for workspace-scoped content routes. `workspaceIdFromQuery: true` lets `?workspaceId=` participate in resolution for header-less `<a download>` export routes. Both wrappers also take `writeScopeExempt` / `sessionOnly` — the OAuth caller-type gates (see "OAuth write-scope & session-only gating" below).
- `isAdmin(userId)` (`with-auth.ts`) — site-admin check vs `ADMIN_USER_ID` env.
- `requireCronSecret(request)` (`require-cron-secret.ts`) — bearer gate for `/api/cron/*`, fail-closed 503 when unset.

Service-layer gates: `requireWorkspaceRole(workspaceId, userId, minRole)` in `src/features/workspaces/server/authz.ts` (the one membership-fetch + role-check helper — don't re-roll it), and the pure member-management hierarchy in `src/features/workspaces/member-policy.ts` (`memberManageDenial` / `canGrantRole` / `canShowMemberControls`, shared with the members UI).

They handle: Bearer OAuth access tokens (`dopl_at_`, validated via `mcp-oauth.ts`), Supabase session cookies, OAuth-subject rate limiting, and automatic 5xx system-event logging.

### OAuth rate limiting — ONE budget, both doors (2026-08-08)

**An OAuth bearer is rate-limited wherever it lands, not only at the MCP transport.** `withUserAuth`'s bearer branch calls `checkAndRecordRateLimitSubject` on the SAME subject the transport uses — `mcp:<tokenId>` — so a token has **one unified budget across both doors** rather than an unbounded second one. Until 2026-08-08 the limiter lived only at `with-mcp-transport-auth.ts`, whose comment assumed tokens reached `/api/*` only over loopback; `with-auth.ts` accepts `dopl_at_*` bearers **directly on every REST route**, so the same token against ~128 routes was uncapped. The check runs **before** the sessionOnly/write-scope gates, so requests that would 403 still spend budget — a flood does not get a free pass by being unauthorized. Fail-closed: any DB error → 429. Cookie/JWT sessions never enter this branch and are never limited. Note the transport's own loopback `/api/*` calls now also count, so one MCP tool call spends ~2–3 of the per-minute budget; that is deliberate, and `MCP_OAUTH_RATE_LIMIT_RPM` tunes both.

**`/api/oauth/register` and `/api/oauth/token` carry per-IP limits** (`oauth-rate-limit.ts`), because both are unauthenticated by RFC and `register` INSERTs a row per call. Register is the one that mattered: it was an unauthenticated unbounded table-growth primitive whose `client_name` renders on the consent screen, so "Dopl Official Desktop" was registerable. Three layers now: the IP limit, the oauth-cleanup cron reaping token-less `oauth_clients` after 7 days (a client that never exchanged its code in a week never will), and the consent screen marking a client **unverified** unless the user already holds a token for it — with the name framed as the app's own claim rather than a badge. **No schema column was needed**: "verified" derives from token history. RFC 7591 dynamic registration stays open; the limits constrain abuse without removing the capability.

### OAuth write-scope & session-only gating (2026-07-20)

`withUserAuth` / `withWorkspaceAuth` take two options that gate by **caller type** (OAuth bearer `dopl_at_` vs. session/cookie), orthogonal to `minRole`. Cookie/session callers authenticate AS the human and are never gated by either — these options only ever constrain OAuth tokens.

- **`writeScopeExempt` (default: gate ON).** An OAuth bearer calling a write method (any non-`GET`) without the `dopl.write` scope → 403 `WRITE_SCOPE_REQUIRED` with `WWW-Authenticate: insufficient_scope`. Set `writeScopeExempt: true` to opt a route out: `/api/mcp` (op-level `WRITE_OPS` gating already covers it) and the `user/mcp-status` ping are the only exemptions.
- **`sessionOnly: true`.** Rejects ALL OAuth tokens (403 `SESSION_REQUIRED`) regardless of scope — for destructive, credential-minting, or permission-mutating routes an agent must never reach. **Applied to 20 route files (COUNTED 2026-08-10, `grep -rl "sessionOnly: true" src/app/api`; this line said 18 and was stale by two).** The list: user delete; workspace delete; member role-change + remove; invitation create + revoke; join-request approve; billing checkout / portal / upgrade-to-team; access-matrix PUT; teams members add + remove; teams access PUT; **team DELETE**; join-link POST; oauth-grant revoke; **`auth/mcp-device-token` POST + DELETE** (Channels v1.1 — an agent must not mint itself a 90-day credential; F-085 — nor revoke one); **`channels/consent/[id]` PATCH + `channels/trust` POST/DELETE** (Channels v1.2 — a spawned agent must not self-approve its own consent gate or self-grant standing trust; see §8 "v1.2 additions"); **`channels/[channelId]/members` PATCH** (C-12, 2026-08-10 — below). ⚠ **Both stale entries are instructive: `teams/[teamId]/route.ts` was gated on 2026-08-07 and this prose was never updated, so the count under-reported the protected surface for three days.** The authoritative list is the array in `write-gate-coverage.test.ts`, which is executable; this paragraph is a summary and **must be re-counted, not amended by memory**. Note the granularity is per-METHOD: the `GET`s on the channels consent/trust/members routes are deliberately ungated.
- **`PATCH /channels/[channelId]/members` IS `sessionOnly` BECAUSE THE AGENT TOOL PROFILE IS A CONTAINMENT CONTROL, NOT A PREFERENCE (C-12, Samuel 2026-08-10).** The attack it closes: the desktop hands every spawned agent a 90-day `dopl.read`+`dopl.write` device token via `--mcp-config`, that agent's whole job is to process an untrusted teammate's message, and a `full` profile has live Bash while `sdk-loader.js` fences only `Read`/`Grep`/`Glob` from secret paths. Ungated, the agent reads its own bearer off disk and PATCHes its profile back to `full` after the operator tightens it — **the setting relied on for containment, writable by the thing it contains, DURABLY** (the column outlives the session). **The METHOD is the right granularity here for a specific reason: since F-170 removed notify scope, `agentToolProfile` is the ONLY field this PATCH carries**, so per-method and per-field coincide. Do not generalize that to the next route — check what else the method writes. Not an outage for the operator: desktop main authenticates with Supabase cookies and the bundled SPA with a Supabase access JWT, and only a `dopl_at_*` bearer takes the OAuth branch, so neither reaches the gate; nothing in `dopl-desktop-app/main` PATCHes this route at all. **`POST`/`DELETE` on the same file (add / remove a member) stay UNGATED by ruling** — invites are unchanged (C-13's invite half). ⚠ **Open residual, Samuel's to rule on: `minRole` stays `viewer`** — a viewer setting their OWN agent's profile is defensible and the service always targets `ctx.userId`'s row, but it has never been decided out loud.
- **THE SECOND GRANULARITY: A FIELD-LEVEL SESSION GATE, WHEN THE METHOD CARRIES WRITES AN AGENT MAY LEGITIMATELY MAKE (C-13, 2026-08-10 — first of its kind).** `sessionOnly` is a wrapper option and therefore per-METHOD, which is **wrong** for `PATCH /channels/[channelId]`: it is four writes behind one verb (`name`, `topic`, `visibility`, `archived`) and only one changes who can see the room. Gating the whole method would spend an agent capability the ruling never asked for. So the handler carries `const SESSION_ONLY_FIELDS = ["visibility"] as const` and throws 403 `SESSION_REQUIRED` when an `agentTokenId` caller names a gated field. **THE RULE: gate the METHOD by default; gate the FIELD when the method carries writes an agent may legitimately make.** The gate is **direction-free** (both private→public and public→private) because it is simpler, costs nothing — `@dopl/client` has no channel-update method at all, so no narrowing caller exists to break — and needs no read of the current row; the ruling itself is about widening, since private→public exposes the entire history to every workspace member. ⚠ **A FIELD GATE IS INVISIBLE TO THE METHOD-LEVEL TRIPWIRE** (there is no `sessionOnly: true` in the file), so `write-gate-coverage.test.ts` grew a **second pin** that greps for `SESSION_ONLY_FIELDS` and asserts the set is exactly `channels/[channelId]/route.ts` — plus that the constant names `visibility`, that the handler actually reads `auth.agentTokenId`, and that it throws `SESSION_REQUIRED`, because a constant nothing reads would satisfy a file list while gating nothing. **Adding a second field-gated route is a conscious edit to that test.** The human half is `components/go-public-dialog.tsx`, whose copy says plainly that the whole history goes workspace-visible and that re-privatizing does not un-read it; **the server does not trust the dialog** — the dialog is the human's confirmation, the field gate is the enforcement.
- **Regression tripwire:** `src/shared/auth/write-gate-coverage.test.ts` pins **three** sets — the `writeScopeExempt` set, the method-level `sessionOnly` set, and (since 2026-08-10) the field-level `SESSION_ONLY_FIELDS` set. Adding a destructive route without `sessionOnly`, or narrowing any of the three, fails the suite. Update the test in the same PR when the surface legitimately changes.

### Workspace resolution (MCP-2 — fail-closed, no default fallback)

`resolveActiveWorkspace(userId, headerWorkspaceId)` (`features/workspaces/server/service.ts`) is THE resolver behind `withWorkspaceAuth` and `GET /api/workspaces/me`. It is fail-closed — there is **no** silent default-workspace fallback (the old oldest-owned / auto-create path is gone):
