It is a PULL, not a replayed event — a handle is fixed for the life of the session KEY, so one read at mount is correct and a reload re-reads; riding `init` instead would have put it in the replay ring and made three emit sites responsible for remembering it. **Never call `nameForSession` with anything but a live session:** `list()` releases every key it does not see, so naming something outside the registry mints a handle and then loses it.
- **A HELD `peer` PICK COLLAPSES TO THE STEER, and that is a safety rule, not a nicety.** The peer name rides `init`, so a park's recreate or an auth hold's synthesized init can land a picked-on window in a session with NO counterparty; a stale `peer` would route the draft to `sendToPeer`, which posts with no addressee at all. `resolveTarget` is therefore never a passthrough — every read of the target resolves against the CURRENT rows, and the fallback direction is the one that keeps the words on this machine.
- **THE PEER BUBBLE CAME BACK WITH THE PEER HALF**, because a post that paints nothing is a post the operator cannot tell happened: `peer_message` is its own stream kind (never a `turn` — my agent never saw it; never an `outbound` — my agent did not draft it), right-laned by the surviving `chrome.ME_KINDS` entry, with a Sending/Sent/Not sent line that **fails closed on anything but `ok === true`**. FIX F8 survives the rewrite and moved into the module that owns the send: a preload with no `sendToPeer` says so in the stream and leaves the draft in the field, rather than clearing it silently.
- **`session.js` STAYED UNDER THE §2 CAP BY GIVING THE MODULE MORE, NOT BY TRIMMING.** `createComposer` owns the control, its copy, the target state, the async name read AND the refusal path — the arrangement `session-modes-ui.js` and `session-close-ui.js` already have, where the controller keeps one mount and one paint. The first cut kept the refusal and the name read in the controller and pushed it to 505.
- **STILL OPEN AFTER THIS PHASE:** ~~`test/live/` remains deleted (F-141 item 4, unbuilt through phases 3 and 4).~~ **NO LONGER TRUE — corrected 2026-08-06 (F-151): the tier is REBUILT and on disk** (nine files, `npm run test:live` restored). The three "test/live is deleted" lines in the phase records above are accurate to their own phase and are left as history; this one was the live claim and it had gone stale. §3.4's shell half landed in Phase 1 and is done; **§3.5's MCP ops (spawn-with-handoff, message-a-session, read-session-state) are unstarted**, and §3.3's `thinking` state is still unbuilt — **not for the reason recorded at the time**: see the corrected note above and in plan §3.3 (F-146). `agent_presence`'s measured retirement (item 7) still waits.

### Channels — PHASE 5 OF THE ROLLBACK: the three session capabilities that replace summon/to_agent (2026-08-05, F-144)

Phase 5 (final) of `docs/CHANNELS-ROLLBACK-PLAN.md` (§3.5 + sequencing item 5). The full story is **F-144 in REFACTOR-FINDINGS.md**. `summon_agent` / `to_agent` are gone (F-141); their *capabilities* come back as three things an EXTERNAL agent (Claude Desktop / Claude Code over MCP) can do in the session-centric model. The rules a future session must not re-derive:

- **SPAWN-WITH-HANDOFF IS A DECLARED FLAG ON `create_thread`, AND IT INVERTS THE CASE-3 PIN — ONLY WHEN DECLARED.** `handoff: true` (`TaskCreateSchema`) rides the OPENING MESSAGE as the reserved `metadata.handoff` stamp: `service-writes-metadata.resolvePostMetadata` strips any caller copy and re-stamps `true` only from `PostMessageOptions.handoff`, which only `service-tasks.postOpeningMessage` passes (from the validated field). The desktop honors it in `targeting.requesterTaskOpen` — the runtime-stamp conjunct became `if (!desktopRuntime(m) && !declaresHandoff(m)) return false;`, so an EXTERNAL unstamped create that declares handoff opens a full requester session on the operator's machine, where without the flag it still opens NOTHING (F-140's CASE 3, now inverted-under-the-flag by `operator-typed-request.test.mjs` / `thread-followup-predicate.test.mjs`). **Handoff substitutes for the STAMP, NEVER for the identity pair** (`authorUserId === me` AND `taskCreatedBy === me`, still required around it), which is what makes it unable to open a window on anyone else's machine — a peer's create fails those conjuncts whatever it stamps. `declaresHandoff` reads `metadata.handoff === true` STRICTLY (a server-written literal boolean, never a `metaStr` string). A handoff session arms NO request strip (`requesterTypedByOperator` keys on `desktop-ui`, which a handoff create does not carry) and the MCP `create_thread` result under handoff tells the external agent NOT to arm `await` — the operator's window carries the reply, and a second watcher would race it.
- **MESSAGE-A-SESSION: THE PEER DIRECTION ALREADY EXISTS; STEER-MY-OWN IS A FLAGGED DESKTOP GAP.** Messaging the peer's running session is a plain `op="post"` REQUEST addressed to the peer member (or a post threaded into the exchange), which triggers their listener — the Phase 1 / §3.1 semantics, pinned in the post/create suites and unchanged. It was NOT rebuilt; the description points at it. An EXTERNAL agent steering its OWN desktop session window (posting into a specific open session on the operator's machine) needs desktop-side IPC that does not exist and is **flagged, not half-built** — an external MCP post reaches the server, not a specific renderer window, so wiring it is a later item.
- **READ-SESSION-STATE SHIPS AS A SERVER READ CONTRACT + MCP OP; THE DESKTOP-PUSH DELIVERY IS A FLAGGED GAP.** New `dopl_channel` op `read_sessions` → `client.listChannelSessions` (GET `/api/channels/sessions`) → `session-state-service.listSessionStates` → `repository-collab.listSessionStates`, reading the new `channel_sessions` table (migration `20260805120000`), OWN-SCOPED to the caller (`ctx.userId` + RLS). It returns the `ChannelSessionState` shape — the SAME projection the pills show (F-142: "phase 5 lifts `list()` to MCP and adds no second derivation"), with the `working`/`idle`/`ended` vocabulary and NO `thinking`. **The chosen delivery is push-on-state-change (plan §5 option a), NOT a heartbeat** — the desktop would write a row only when a session's derived digest changes (`session-summary.js` already coalesces to exactly that), a handful of writes per session lifetime versus `agent_presence`'s 120/hr, so this is the CHEAP side of §5 and is what lets item 7 proceed. The desktop WRITE is **not wired in this phase** (it needs `session-summary.js` — deliberately network-free above its sentinel — to gain an authenticated writer + the workspace/session-key context the summary shape does not carry today), so the op returns `[]` live and says so honestly ("no live sessions being reported"), never fabricating state. When the push lands, the same op renders it with no server change. **— THE PUSH LANDED: see "PHASE 5's DELIVERY GAP CLOSED" below (F-147). `main/session-state-push.js` is the writer, the op did not change to receive it, and `repository-collab.listSessionStates` is now `repository-sessions.listSessionStates` (§2 split).**
- **CONSENT POSTURE FOR SPAWN-WITH-HANDOFF (documented for Samuel, no card added).** An external agent causing a window+agent to open on the operator's machine is a real capability, but the caller is the operator's OWN credential, the window opens on the operator's OWN machine, and the opened session's tool use still gates through the untouched inbound consent card (§3.6). The unforgeable bound is the identity pair, and handoff is an explicit opt-in the operator's own agent declares (the operator prompted it: "spin up an agent on Dopl to talk to X"). So the call made here is **"I asked Claude to do this" is sufficient — no extra card for the window-open itself** — with the diag as the observable signal. Flagged for Samuel to confirm before 1.9.0.

### Channels — THE ROLLBACK REVIEW FIXES: unknown MCP args, the read that 500'd, and four guards that lost their tests (2026-08-05, F-145)

A three-lens review of Phases 1–5 (F-140…F-144). The full story is **F-145 in REFACTOR-FINDINGS.md**. The rules a future session must not re-derive:

- **AN UNKNOWN TOOL ARGUMENT IS REFUSED BY NAME, NOT STRIPPED — AND THAT IS A SCHEMA RULE, NOT A COPY RULE.** Every `dopl_*` tool was registered with a RAW SHAPE, which the MCP SDK turns into a plain `z.object` and parses with `safeParseAsync`; a plain `z.object` DROPS unknown keys, so `dopl_channel {op:"post", body:"hi", to_agent:"quartz"}` was accepted, `to_agent` never reached the handler, the post landed UNADDRESSED and the result narrated success. That is the invisible delivery `src/features/channels/schema.ts#removedParam` already refuses at the ROUTE (the deleted named-agent params are `z.never()` for exactly this reason), and the MCP layer in front of it had the hole the route had closed. **`server.ts`'s `registerTool` / `registerMetaTool` now register through `server.registerTool(name, {description, inputSchema: strictInput(shape)}, cb)`, where `strictInput` is `z.strictObject`.** The positional `server.tool()` overload **cannot** carry a built schema — `isZodRawShapeCompat` returns false for a schema instance and the SDK then reads it as annotations and throws — so **`tool()` is the regression to watch for**, and all four `McpServer` test mocks expose `registerTool` and deliberately NOT `tool` so a revert is a TypeError. The published JSON Schema is byte-identical apart from a gained `additionalProperties: false`; an unknown key becomes `-32602 … Unrecognized key: "to_agent"`, which NAMES the field. Pinned end-to-end through the real SDK over `InMemoryTransport` in `packages/mcp-server/src/strict-args.test.ts` (not mocked — the defect lives inside the SDK's parse step).
- **NO SHIPPED STRING MAY TEACH REMOVED VOCABULARY, AND THE SCAN IS THE WHOLE SURFACE.** `channel-law.test.ts` banned the removed named-agent words in `CHANNEL_DESCRIPTION` and `channel-discovery.test.ts` banned them in `buildInstructions`; both stayed green while `closedThreadNote` — emitted on EVERY post into a closed thread — taught `to_agent="<handle>" starts that agent`. A tool RESULT is read by the same model at the moment it is deciding what to do next, so it teaches harder than the description. The guard now parses every non-test `channel-*.ts` in `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/` with the TypeScript compiler API and tests every STRING / TEMPLATE literal against `REMOVED_VOCABULARY`. Comments are not literals, so a comment may still discuss `to_agent` while a shipped sentence may not. **There is no allowlist, deliberately** — if legitimate English collides, rephrase it (the ad-hoc legend's "no participants" became "no recorded parties").
- **`read_sessions` DEGRADES TO EMPTY ON A MISSING RELATION, AND ON NOTHING ELSE.** F-144 shipped `channel_sessions` UNAPPLIED and documented the op as returning `[]` live; it actually returned **500**, because PostgREST answered `PGRST205` and `mapChannelError` has no arm for a raw driver error. `repository-collab.listSessionStates` now maps that ONE code to `[]` — matched on `error.code`, never on the message prose — and rethrows everything else, because a permission error / a moved column / a dead connection mean the answer is UNKNOWN, and an empty list is a CLAIM. **Delete the degrade once the table is applied everywhere and the desktop push has landed**; past that point a missing relation is a real deployment fault and should be loud. **UPDATED BY F-147: the push HAS landed and the function now lives in `repository-sessions.ts`, so only the migration is outstanding — the degrade stays until it is applied, and the matching WRITE deliberately does NOT degrade.**
- **`?channelId=` ON `/api/channels/sessions` IS VALIDATED** (`SessionStateQuerySchema`, the same `z.string().uuid().optional()` the consent inbox's identical param uses). It went straight into `.eq()`, so a non-uuid became a Postgres cast error and a 500 for a malformed request.
- **THE THREE AGENT-ATTRIBUTION KEYS STAY IN THE STRIP LIST, AND NOW HAVE THE SECURITY TEST THAT SAYS WHY.** `resolvePostMetadata` deletes `to_agent_id` / `to_agent_ids` / `author_agent_id` and never re-stamps them. The WRITE path is gone; **the READ path is deliberately alive** — stored rows still render "quartz · Ada's agent" (`lib/agent-display.ts`, `channel-transcript.tsx`) — which makes these display credentials with a live reader and NO legitimate writer, and makes the strip the entire boundary. Their test died with the deleted `service-writes-metadata-agents.test.ts`; deleting any one of the three lines left all 2109 root tests green. `service-writes-metadata-attribution.test.ts` is the guard, and each line is individually mutation-proven.
- **A MIXED TEST FILE WHOSE FEATURE IS DELETED IS REWRITTEN DOWN TO WHAT SURVIVES, NOT REMOVED** — the rule that saved `service-writes-members.test.ts`, restated because `session-addressee-truth.test.mjs` was deleted whole and took the **H2 DM-recipient naming** guard with it (nothing in the desktop tree so much as mentioned `directChannel`). Restored as `test/session-dm-addressee-truth.test.mjs`, minus the genuinely-deleted mention section.
- **A PIN ON A SYMBOL IS NOT A PIN.** `operator-typed-request.test.mjs` asserted `/\[RUNTIME_HEADER\]: DESKTOP_UI_RUNTIME,/`, so rewriting the constant's VALUE stayed green while the entire Phase-1 stamp stopped matching the server. There is **no shared module** across that join (main is CommonJS Electron; the reader is `src/shared/auth/runtime-header.ts`), so both literals are pinned on both sides and compared against the web tree's own source.
- **THE HANDOFF RESULT STATES A REQUEST, NOT AN OUTCOME.** The `create_thread handoff=true` result said "A full session is opening on your operator's Dopl app … You are done", off nothing but the caller's own flag. `session-dispatch.maybeOpenRequesterSession` returns false SILENTLY on four ordinary paths (window mode off, `requesterTaskOpen` refusal, window budget spent, desktop not running), and "you are done" on a handoff nobody picked up leaves the exchange with NO watcher. The copy keeps the do-not-race default (a real handoff is the common case) and adds the fallback: how to notice with `op="get_thread"`, and the real `since=` cursor if nothing picks it up.
- **`channel_sessions` MIGRATION: an index exists only if a named statement uses it, AND AN FK CASCADE COUNTS AS ONE.** This reconciles the two precedents that look opposed — `20260731150000` dropped an index with no query behind it, `20260802180000` added a covering index for every unindexed FK. The engaged index was a PARTIAL index on a non-FK column; an FK-covering index is named by every cascading delete. Result: four indexes, one per reason, **all four FK columns covered as a LEADING column** (which is what the `unindexed_foreign_keys` advisor checks). `(user_id, channel_id)` was replaced by `(channel_id)` — it served the narrowed read but covered no FK, and `user_id` is already led by the unique upsert key. `task_id` gained the cover it never had. **`channel_name` / `thread_title` are CHECK-bounded** on `20260731100000_channels_name_topic_bounds.sql`'s class (length / trimmed / no control / no zero-width), because the comment that justified leaving them open described a writer that does not exist yet. **`updated_at` is stamped by a trigger**, not by the writer: the read is `ORDER BY updated_at DESC`, and an upsert that forgets the column freezes the ordering with no error and no empty result.
- **`state` IS RENDERED THROUGH A CLOSED-SET TEST.** It was the one field on `read_sessions`' line spliced raw into SERVER NARRATION, guarded only by a CHECK in an unapplied migration and an unchecked `as SessionPillState` in `collab-dto.ts`. `formatSessionLine` now says `(unrecognized state)` for anything outside `working|idle|ended` rather than emitting it. Unreachable today; the writer lands later and this is the layer that has to hold.
- **THE `desktop-ui` BOUND IS ON THE VALUE, NOT ON THE WINDOW — SAY IT THAT WAY.** Four places claimed or implied that an external MCP post can never open a requester window. `narrowRuntime` bounds ONLY `desktop-ui`; `desktop-session` is credential-agnostic **by design** (a desktop-spawned session authenticates with exactly that device token), and `targeting.requesterTaskOpen` accepts EITHER stamp — so a `dopl_at_*` holder sending `X-Dopl-Runtime: desktop-session` on a create of its own opens a window on that account's Mac. **Pre-existing, not a regression** (there was no bound at all before Phase 1) and **not a peer's path** (the identity pair holds). It is the account's own credential acting as the account, so **the real boundary is TOKEN CUSTODY**, and F-144's consent posture is restated as "the identity pair PLUS token custody" — gating handoff behind a card would buy nothing while the other stamp is reachable. **Do not "fix" this by bounding `desktop-session`**: that refuses the exact caller the value exists for.

### Channels — THE RESIDUE PASS: nine dead symbols, twenty stale comments, and the CI gap under all of it (2026-08-05, F-146)

The layer under the rollback's ship-blockers. The full story is **F-146 in REFACTOR-FINDINGS.md**. **No behaviour changed** except two lint rules and the CI workflow. The rules a future session must not re-derive:

- **THE RESIDUE PATTERN IS THE PARTIAL-FILE UPDATE, and it is what a future rollback should design against.** Almost every stale comment sat two paragraphs from a CORRECT rollback annotation in the same file — `targeting.js:160`/`:176` say the module is gone while `:68`/`:168` describe it acting; `channel-render.ts:253` says the agent-address clause went while `:230` documented its output format. **A rollback sweeps by SYMBOL, and the residue is wherever a symbol was named in PROSE but not in CODE** — which is exactly the set a grep for the symbol finds. That grep is now a test.
- **`dopl-desktop-app/test/removed-vocabulary.test.mjs` IS THAT TEST, IN THREE TIERS.** (1) no deleted module exists on disk; (2) nothing `require`s one — the failure that breaks the app at LOAD, since `module.exports` is evaluated and the rollback already shipped one `ReferenceError: sessionTeam is not defined`; (3) **every surviving mention is annotated as history**, checked against the contiguous comment BLOCK, not the line, because the honest annotations in this tree run several lines. **Tier 3 cannot be an eslint rule** — no lint rule knows what a paragraph MEANS, and meaning is the whole point. To fix a failure, decide whether the paragraph is history (annotate it) or a claim about live behaviour (it is wrong); do NOT add a marker word to silence it.
- **`no-redeclare` IS ON IN THE DESKTOP CONFIG, at `error`, on both blocks.** Its absence is why F-143's duplicate `nameForSession` shipped: the later declaration wins silently, and `max-lines` cannot catch it because a duplicate makes a file BIGGER. `builtinGlobals: false` deliberately — these files run under the `node`+`browser` globals union, which legitimately contains `name` / `status` / `origin` / `close`, and flagging those is a rename wave with no defect behind it.
- **CI RUNS EVERY SUITE NOW, AND THE WORKFLOW IS `ci.yml`, NOT `packages.yml`.** It ran `npm test -w @dopl/client` alone — **48 of 5 120 tests** — because the root `package.json` had no `test` script and `packages/mcp-server`'s had none either, so there was nothing else to call. Both exist now (plus a root `test:all`). The workflow runs the root suite, mcp-server and the SPA in the matrix job; a **separate `desktop` job** (own npm project, own lockfile, so its own `npm ci --ignore-scripts` — the suite launches no Electron binary); and a `typecheck-lint` job. **The path filters are DELETED on purpose** — a filter over a repo-wide job would let a `src/` PR skip the `src/` suite, which is the gap this closes.
- **A FILE AT 500 CANNOT ABSORB A COMMENT, and that is a real cost of the cluster in §2.** Correcting `ui-sync.js`'s stale header pushed it to 508 and the correction had to be compressed three times; the same happened to `session-engine.js`. "It's only a doc fix" is not a reason to skip the lint.
- **`server/agent-names.ts` IS THE CANONICAL SPEC OF A SHIPPED GENERATOR, not a reservation for a future one.** It has no production caller and is not residue: `main/agent-names.js` is a byte-for-byte PORT, the pool ORDER is behaviour, and `agent-names-desktop-parity.test.ts` — the only thing holding the copies together — compares the live desktop copy against THIS module. Deleting it deletes the guard and leaves the names a user sees unpinned. Change the TS module FIRST, then the port.

### Channels — PHASE 5's DELIVERY GAP CLOSED: the desktop reports its session state, ON CHANGE (2026-08-05, F-147)

F-144 shipped read-session-state as a server READ + MCP op and flagged the desktop WRITE as a delivery gap, so `dopl_channel(op="read_sessions")` answered an honest empty. The write is wired now. The full story is **F-147 in REFACTOR-FINDINGS.md**. The rules a future session must not re-derive:

- **THE TRIGGER IS `session-summary.js`'s DIGEST, AND THERE IS EXACTLY ONE.** `flush()` already coalesces a burst of engine dispatches (200 ms) and fires only when the projection really moved; it now emits a CHANGE EVENT (`subscribe()`) beside the renderer frame, and `main/session-state-push.js` is its one subscriber. **The two gates are separate on purpose**: `start()` resets the WINDOW's `lastDigest` so a rebuilt renderer is repainted, and a rebuilt renderer must not cost a server write, so `lastChangeDigest` is its own and is never reset. **`session-summary.js` stays network-free** — it is the one place engine state becomes a pill state (F-142), it is import-free below its sentinel, and every one of its tests reads it as SOURCE; an `apiFetch` in it ends all three. The seam is a subscription, and the writer re-derives NOTHING (it maps the entry to the wire and posts it).
- **THE SUMMARY GAINED A SECOND PROJECTION, NOT A SECOND DERIVATION.** `reportList()` builds entries carrying `key` and `workspaceId` — the stable `(channel, thread)` upsert key (`sessionId` is ephemeral: a park or a recreate mints a new one) and the workspace the row is fenced on, both facts the module already held. `list()` is `reportList().map(wireSummary)`, so the IPC payload and `DesktopSessionSummary` are byte-unchanged. `noteEnded` freezes `workspaceId` with the rest of the identity, because a retained ended entry has left the registry and there is nowhere else to read it from.
- **THE ROW SET MIRRORS THE PILL SET, AND AN `ended` ROW IS DELETED RATHER THAN SWEPT.** F-142's "a pill lives exactly as long as its window" is inherited by the server row, because the row IS that projection: a session's row exists while its pill does and goes when the pill leaves, ended included. A kept-`ended`-plus-sweep design was rejected twice over — a sweep needs a scheduler this product does not have (so "later" means "never" and the table grows, which is the accumulation plan §5 exists to shed), and an `ended` row with no window answers "what is flint doing?" with a session the operator cannot open, when the channel transcript is already the record. **The delete is IMPLICIT, which is why the push carries the WHOLE SET for a workspace**: a delta protocol needs an explicit removal message that a crashed or quit desktop never sends.
- **THE PERSISTED RECORD IS KEYED BY OPERATOR, BECAUSE A ROW IS.** `sessionReportWorkspaces` in electron-store maps `userId -> [workspaceId]` — the workspaces this machine has written rows into. Its one job is the run that starts with NO sessions in a workspace a previous run left rows in (a crash or a quit): those get one empty push and are then forgotten. A machine-wide list would make the next operator to sign in clear a workspace they may not be in, and would forget that the previous one still has rows there. **What is NOT covered is signing out** — the credential that could delete the rows is gone before anything can react — so those rows stand until that account signs in on this Mac again. Stated, not papered over.
- **THE IDENTITY GUARD IS AN ORIGIN STAMP, AND IT FAILS CLOSED.** Signing out does not end sessions (the engine owns their lifetime), so operator A's are still projecting when B signs in on the same Mac, and a push under B's credential would file A's handles, channel names and thread titles as B's — readable by B through `read_sessions`. So every session key is stamped with the identity current WHEN THE WRITER FIRST SAW IT, and only keys stamped with the identity being posted as are reported. A key first seen with no resolvable identity is never reported at all. A sign-out and a sign-in as the SAME operator resumes reporting on its own — there is nothing to un-quarantine by hand. **Mutation-proven**: dropping the filter turns three cases red.
- **THE TRANSPORT IS `api.js`, AND A THIRD FETCH COPY IS THE THING TO REFUSE.** `listener-io.js` keeps its own SEND because its long-poll wires a caller abort signal into the controller and its timeouts are load-bearing; `api.js` is the shared helper for everything else and carries the F-132 401 repair both share. This is a short POST with no abort wiring, so it takes `api.js` and inherits the repair, the app-version stamp and the undici pool reset. A test pins it as a source fact.
- **FAILURE IS BOUNDED BY THE SESSION'S OWN LIFE, NOT BY A TIMER.** Two attempts (one retry, 2 s, only for a throw / 5xx / 429 — a 4xx will not answer differently), then STOP; a failed push records NO digest, so the session's next real state change is the retry. There is no timer anywhere, which is the shape `ui-sync`'s ~39 000-attempt storm is the cautionary tale for. Failures log ONCE per `(workspace, shape)` and the line says what it COSTS ("read_sessions will not see this machine until…"); a success clears the latch so a later outage is reported again.
- **THE WRITER ARMS IN SPA MODE, beside the pill push** (`shell-mode.wireSpaServices`), because that is where the projection and the operator's identity already meet — it subscribes to session-summary and reads `authTokens.getAuthState().userId`. The retired remote shell (§9.3) does not grow capabilities and does not even start `authTokens`. The sign-in transition calls `kick()` because a fresh credential is not a state change and would otherwise never fire.
- **THE SERVER WRITES ONLY WHAT CHANGED, and that is about `updated_at`.** `POST /api/channels/sessions` → `reportSessionStates` → `repository-sessions.replaceSessionStates`, which SELECTs the caller's current rows, upserts only those that differ (field-by-field, never `JSON.stringify` — key order differs between a PostgREST row and a service-built object) and deletes by explicit key. The read is `ORDER BY updated_at DESC` and the trigger stamps it on every UPDATE (F-145), so an unconditional whole-set upsert would make five sessions all claim to have changed when one did — plausible, wrong, and exactly the failure the migration's own `updated_at` note describes.
- **THE WRITE IS OWN-SCOPED IN THE SERVICE AND HAS NO CREDENTIAL BOUND.** `ctx.userId` / `ctx.workspaceId` are the only identity the repository sees and the body has no field for either, so a caller can only ever replace its own set; the table REVOKEs writes from `authenticated`, so the statement runs on the admin client and the service IS the fence. Narrowing it to a runtime stamp or a cookie session would refuse a future desktop lane and buy no boundary — the mistake F-145 warns against for `desktop-session`. The honest bound is F-144's restated posture: the identity pair plus token custody.
- **THE WRITE DOES NOT DEGRADE `PGRST205`; THE READ STILL DOES.** An empty read is honest over a missing table because the answer is the same either way. A write that swallowed it would report a store that did not happen, so until the migration is applied the POST throws, the route answers 500, and the desktop says so once per workspace per run. `repository-sessions.ts` is the split-out home of both (§2 — the write pushed `repository-collab.ts` to 532).
- **VALIDATION MIRRORS THE MIGRATION'S CHECKs, CHARACTER FOR CHARACTER** (`schema-sessions.ts`): `name ~ '^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{1,30}$'`, `state IN ('working','idle','ended')` with no `thinking`, `channel_name` / `thread_title` on `safeLabel`'s class at 120 / 200 — the lengths `channels.name` and `channel_tasks.title` themselves carry, so a legitimate name can never be refused into this projection. `sessionKey` is charset-bounded to a uuid pair because the reconcile DELETES BY KEY. Duplicate keys are REFUSED rather than deduped (two entries for one key hit `ON CONFLICT` twice — Postgres 21000, read as an opaque 500 — and there is no honest way to pick which of two contradictory states is true).

### Channels — THE CONTAINMENT UI: three named controls, and what a UI may claim about a profile (2026-08-08, F-174)

`channel-settings-popover.tsx` had one heading, "Agent tools & trust", over two unrelated settings. It now exposes **three**, and the split is by BACKING STORE AND LIFETIME, which is the only split that can be honest:

- **Permissions** / **Sends** — the two axes of the desktop permission ARM (`main/channel-prefs.js`, H2 below). Desktop-only; the section is absent in a browser.
- **Tools** — `channel_members.agent_tool_profile`, the cloud, durable, per-channel value. **This is the containment control** — it decides whether a teammate's request can reach the connectors on this machine — and it is the one that had no explanation of any kind.

**RULES A LATER SESSION MUST NOT RE-DERIVE:**

- **The arm may never be presented as a stored preference.** Single use, 30-minute TTL, one consumer (a launch a human is actively approving). Any surface showing it says so; the section heading is *"For the next request you allow"*, never *"this channel"*. Presenting it as durable is the H2 defect's own mental model.
- **One channel's arm has ONE value across every mounted reader.** `useChannelPermissionPreset` merges a patch onto what is STORED (re-read immediately before the set) and broadcasts the settled pair to every mounted reader of that channel. Per-component snapshots are what let two surfaces revert each other (C-25). A new surface showing the arm inherits this for free; one that keeps its own copy re-opens the bug.
- **The web's fallback for an unresolved tool profile is the DESKTOP's fallback.** `UNRESOLVED_TOOL_PROFILE` (`channels/constants.ts`) is `read_only`, matching `tool-profiles.normalizeProfile` after C-11. Do not "fix" it back to `full` because the column defaults to `full`: null means *this DTO does not know*, and a label reading "Full access" over a session the machine will run `read_only` is a lie in the direction that makes an operator relax.
- **Per-profile copy says what a profile GRANTS, never what it withholds.** `full` includes the operator's own connected apps and MCP servers — that is the product (a teammate asking your agent to pull something out of your Slack is the feature), so the copy names it plainly and does not hedge. Equally, the restricted profiles are not written as the "safe" or recommended answer: `tool-profiles.js` states that `dopl_only` is deliberately not more dangerous than `full`. **And no UI sentence generalizes the deny floor**: since F-177 both lanes deny exactly `UNIVERSAL_HARD_DENY` under `full` (`SESSION_HARD_DENY` IS that constant), which is the Dopl admin + retired tools — nothing more. A claim like "destructive tools are always denied" is wrong on BOTH lanes now: what a `full` session cannot do WITHOUT A CLICK is the honest statement, and the thing that supervises it is the operator's permission preset.
- **Trust is WORKSPACE-WIDE and the copy carries the scope.** `UNIQUE (operator_user_id, trusted_user_id, workspace_id)`, no channel column, decided to stay that way. The section states the scope, the effect (no approval card, a session starts), and which tool scope that session gets — and renders an EMPTY STATE rather than vanishing when the channel has no other members.

Both suites pin the copy: the root suite static-renders the pure panel and cross-checks every claim against the desktop source it describes; the SPA suite drives the bridge, the drill-down and the two-surface arm case in jsdom.

### Desktop app — THE MINIMUM-VERSION GATE (2026-08-03, F-125)

Phase 4 retires usedopl.com, after which the bundled SPA is the only client and a Mac on an old build is on an old product forever — no Vercel deploy reaches it. So a build asks the server for the floor it must meet and blocks ITSELF when it is below. `main/version-skew.js` looks like this gate and is not (it compares a PEER's build for one diagnostic line and documents itself as never gating), and `src/shared/auth/app-version-header.ts` forbids gating on the `x-dopl-app-version` stamp — **that contract is unchanged**; the gate does not ride the header, and the rejected `426 Upgrade Required` design is recorded in that file so the question is not rediscovered.

- **The floor rides CODE, with an env OVERRIDE read per request** (`src/shared/version/desktop-floor.ts`). Stage C (2026-08-05): `DEFAULT_MIN_VERSION` in code is the floor when `DOPL_DESKTOP_MIN_VERSION` is unset — bump it in the release that becomes mandatory, beside `DEFAULT_DECLARED_LATEST` (a same-commit test pins floor ≤ latest so the clamp can never refuse its own release). The env var repositions the floor without a deploy; an explicit OFF spelling (`""`, `none`, `0`, `off`) runs floorless; **malformed = no floor**, still the fail-open direction for mistakes. A refused floor is invisible on the wire (it is the same `null` a correct unset config produces), so the route logs it once per distinct misconfiguration, naming which source it clamped against.
- **The anti-brick clamp's `latest` is DERIVED, not declared** (`src/shared/version/latest-release.ts`). A floor above the newest published build is REFUSED at the server, so a fat-fingered floor cannot take the fleet offline — and the number that clamp compares against is read from **`https://github.com/SamuelrWang/Dopl/releases/latest/download/latest-mac.yml`**, the electron-updater channel file the desktop already consumes. Not the REST API: unauthenticated `api.github.com` is 60/hr **per IP** and a lambda egresses from a shared pool (a design-time probe hit `403 rate limit exceeded` from an ordinary IP), while the channel file is a release ASSET served off the asset CDN, outside that quota. It is also the same fact the client's `GUARD 2` reads, and a `tag_name` can exist with no mac artifact behind it while a `version:` in the channel file cannot. **Nothing is bumped by hand at release time; `npm run release` is unchanged.**
- **`DOPL_DESKTOP_LATEST_VERSION` still exists, and should normally be UNSET.** It is now only the fallback for the seconds before a cold lambda has reached GitHub, and the manual override for a feed that has gone away. **Do not set it from `dopl-desktop-app/package.json`** — that file is a claim about what should ship and runs AHEAD of what is published (`1.8.2` there vs `1.7.24` released, as F-125's residual was closed), and a latest ahead of reality is what permits a floor nothing can install. A derived value always beats a declared one.
- **The derivation never touches the request path.** `GET /api/version` reads a per-instance cache and schedules the refresh with `after()` (10-min TTL on success, 60s backoff on failure, single-flight); `GET` is a synchronous function, so it cannot await a socket even by accident. Every failure degrades toward a LOWER `latest`, which refuses MORE floors and blocks FEWER people: a throw, a timeout, a 403, a 5xx or a 200 full of HTML all leave the previous value in place — **expiry schedules a refresh, it never blanks the value**, because a stale-low latest blocks nobody while a `null` latest disables the clamp entirely.
- **`GET /api/version` is unauthenticated, and that takes TWO places.** Having no auth wrapper is not enough: `src/proxy.ts` 401s every unmatched `/api/**` path before a route runs, so `/api/version` is listed in its `PUBLIC_ROUTES` **and** `SELF_AUTH_ROUTES` (the one entry there that is not machine-*authenticated* — it qualifies on the property that list turns on: nothing below the auth call can change its answer). It shipped missing from both, the desktop read the 401 as "no answer", and the whole gate was silently inert. Pinned by `proxy.test.ts`.
- **Policy is pure, the shell is not** — the `update-policy.js`/`updater.js` split. `main/min-version.js` holds every decision as a truth table (no electron, no timers, no I/O); `main/version-gate.js` holds the fetch, the timer and the notification. **FAIL OPEN is the whole design:** offline, a timeout, a 5xx, a captive portal's HTML, a malformed floor, an unparseable own version — every one is "no floor" and the app opens. Only a well-formed floor strictly above a well-formed running version blocks. **No floor is ever cached to disk**, so a cold boot can never start inside a block with no network to re-check against.
- **TWO anti-brick guards, from two different sources of truth.** Server: a floor above the declared latest is refused. Client: `GUARD 1` — no updater at all (dev, unpackaged, electron-updater failed to load) degrades to a warning, because no button could end that block; `GUARD 2` — the updater has genuinely COMPLETED a check and found nothing newer, so the floor is above the newest build that exists; degrade rather than hold the app hostage to a config mistake. A FAILED check never sets that evidence — it learned nothing.
- **It composes with the updater, it does not replace it.** `updater.js` keeps owning the feed, the download, the 4h timer and the restart prompt; it grew `updateState()` (`supported/checked/available/ready/checking/failed/version/percent`) and an `onState` fan-out. The gate rides the SAME 4h cadence — the floor and the release feed answer the same question — plus `wake.js` (a Mac can sleep across a release) and a 10-minute retry for the machine that booted offline and would otherwise not learn there is a floor for 4h. The screen's buttons are `updater.checkNow` / `updater.requestRestart`, never a second implementation.
- **The block is ONE branch, in the ONE shell factory.** `shell-mode.createShellWindow` is where the dock icon, the tray's "Open Dopl", a clicked notification and a `dopl://` deep link all already resolve, so a single `isBlocked()` check makes the block total with no new interception point, and both shells are covered. `update-required-window.js` is a local page with its own preload (contextIsolation + sandbox, `loadFile` only, three sender-bound IPC handlers). **Quit always works and says so on screen.** Live SESSION windows are deliberately untouched — the gate blocks the product surface, not an agent mid turn, for the same reason the updater never force-restarts.
- **The page cannot import the design kit** (local file, `default-src 'none'`), so it carries `.auth-btn-3d` / `.auth-btn-3d-light` and the auth-surface type scale as literal CSS, with a test that diffs the copy against `apps/desktop-ui/src/styles/kit.css` declaration by declaration. Same bargain, and same rule, as F-074's other kit copies: the kit and this file change in one commit.
- **Dev knob: `DOPL_VERSION_GATE`**, the `DOPL_UI` shape. `off` disables the gate; `force` synthesizes a floor above this build so the blocking screen can be dogfooded without deploying one (it blocks through the REAL branch, and rewrites the updater view for the screen as well as the verdict, or a developer sees a screen no user gets); anything else — **including a typo** — is the shipping behavior.
- **SCOPE, stated so it is not re-litigated:** builds ≤1.8.0 do not have this code and never will. The gate protects the FUTURE population, which is why it ships now rather than at cutover. It is not authorization and cannot be — the floor is PULLED by a client that chooses to obey it.

### Desktop app — THE `dopl://open` VERB (2026-08-03, retirement Stage A)

The `dopl://` handler adopted auth fragments and nothing else, and a REJECTED fragment returned *before* `showMainWindow()` — so a plain `dopl://` link launched the app on macOS (the OS does that on protocol activation) and then sat there. The website-retirement plan (§2.5) wants that to be deliberate, and wants an old web URL to be able to arrive somewhere. Ships in **1.8.3**, beside the gate.

- **THE GRAMMAR.** `dopl://auth#<tokens>` — unchanged, and still what **any** unknown verb falls back to (builds in the field send it; `open` opts IN). `dopl://open` — show the app. `dopl://open/{segment}/{page}` — show it AT that page. `dopl://open?target=<url-encoded web path>` — the same, for a caller holding a `location.pathname`; a present, non-blank `target` wins over the path.
- **POLICY IS PURE, THE SHELL IS NOT** — the `min-version.js`/`version-gate.js` split again. `main/deep-link-target.js` (no electron, no window, no I/O) holds the grammar and the web→SPA map as a truth table; `main/deep-link.js` holds the protocol registration, the pre-ready park and the two verbs. Both were carved out of `index.js`, which was at 499/500 lines — the auth handoff moved **verbatim**, comments included.
- **A ROUTE COMES BACK, NEVER A URL.** Every value is `/` or a path assembled from segments decoded and character-checked one at a time (`[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,127}`), so nothing — absolute URL, protocol-relative `//`, `/\`, `..`, `%2e%2e`, `%2F`, a control byte, 512+ chars — can steer the router off the app. **Dot segments are checked on the RAW string, before `new URL`**, because `new URL` NORMALIZES `..` away rather than refusing it: `/../secrets` would otherwise arrive as `/secrets`, a link that says one thing and resolves to another. **That includes the path the parser hands the mapper** — it is cut out of the URL string (`rawPathOf`), never read off `parsed.pathname`, which is already normalized. The unit cases could not see the difference, because each one was handed a raw string directly; a runtime pass over the dispatcher was what found it.
- **MALFORMED OPENS HOME; UNKNOWN RESOLVES AS DEEP AS IT CAN.** There is no failure mode where a Dopl link does nothing. An unknown page inside a real workspace opens `/{segment}/` + `WORKSPACE_HOME_PAGE` (the workspace half is still good information) — **that value is `overview` since 2026-08-07, not `canvas`** (retirement Phase 1 / D4), which is also what makes an old `/{ws}/canvas`, `/{ws}/workflows` or `/{ws}/configuration` bookmark land somewhere real instead of being refused; `/canvas`, `/login`, `/pricing`, `/terms`, `/admin/**` — the workspace-less web surface — open `/`, which is the SPA's boot route and where the default-workspace resolve lives. Query and fragment are DROPPED, not rejected: `?billing=upgrade` is dead weight, but a bookmark carrying one is still a bookmark.
- **THE PAGE TABLE IS A HAND COPY, AND A TEST SAYS SO.** Main cannot import the SPA's TypeScript, so `WORKSPACE_PAGES` is duplicated from `apps/desktop-ui/src/routes.tsx`; a page ported into the SPA and not added here would be unreachable by any link, silently. `test/deep-link-target.test.mjs` reads `routes.tsx` and fails on drift — including `WORKSPACE_HOME_PATH`, which decides where an unknown page lands.
- **SIGNED OUT IS A SHOW, NOT A NAVIGATE**, and remote mode (`DOPL_UI=remote`) gets the window and nothing else. Steering a signed-out SPA at a workspace route replaces the sign-in screen with a page waiting on a session. **There is deliberately no deferred navigation** — landing home after sign-in is the accepted outcome (plan §2.5), and a queue that outlives an OAuth round trip is a feature, not a hardening detail.
- **THE COLD-START SEND.** A deep link is the one navigation that routinely reaches a window still loading (the link is what launched the app; the park is flushed a few lines after the shell window is created), and `navigateTo` is a `webContents.send` that a renderer which has not yet subscribed drops with no error. So: send on `did-finish-load` when loading, immediately when not, then **once more after 300ms** — the subscription is a React effect a tick behind the document's load event, and re-sending the same route is free.

### The landing page is DOWNLOAD-ONLY, and the download URL is RESOLVED (2026-08-03)

- **`/login` links left the landing page; the `/login` ROUTE did not.** The nav's dark button and the hero's dual CTA are now one Download each (`src/features/marketing/`). The route is on the retirement plan's KEEP list — the desktop OAuth handoff and password reset both land there — so nothing about it changed. `/pricing`'s subscribe flows still push `/login`: they need a session, and they are not landing CTAs. Below 900px `.lp-nav-center` is `display:none`, so the nav button is desktop-only, exactly as the Login button was; the hero CTA is what a phone sees.
- **THE DOWNLOAD ASSET NAME IS READ, NOT GUESSED.** `DOWNLOAD_URL` was `…/releases/latest/download/Dopl-arm64.dmg` with a comment claiming a version-less asset name. electron-builder stamps the version in (`Dopl-1.7.24-arm64.dmg`), so **that link 404'd for every release there has ever been.** `/releases/latest/download/:asset` is evergreen in the RELEASE, not in the asset NAME. `GET /download` (`src/app/download/route.ts` → `src/shared/version/mac-download.ts`) reads the dmg's name out of `latest-mac.yml` — the same channel file the anti-brick clamp reads, for the same reasons — and 307s to the evergreen URL. Public in `proxy.ts` `PUBLIC_ROUTES`, or the middleware bounces the download to `/login`.
- **Rejected: pinning `artifactName` to a version-less name.** One line, and it would make the original constant true. It renames the artifacts in **1.8.3** — the gate's first release, the one whose auto-update path must not acquire a new variable — leaves the public button 404ing until that release ships, and drops the version out of the file name a support conversation asks for. The resolver survives that rename if it is ever made, because it never assumed a name.
- **Every failure is still a github.com URL.** Timeout, 5xx, HTML from a captive portal, a feed with no dmg in it → `…/releases/latest`, the human page. A Download button may not show anybody a stack trace.

### The download goes THROUGH the account now: "Get Started" → `/login` → `/get-started` (2026-08-04)

Supersedes the CTA half of the section above; the resolver half is unchanged and
still the source of the dmg.

- **ONE PLACE DECIDES WHERE A SIGN-IN ENDS: `src/shared/lib/url/post-auth-landing.ts`.** `explicitPostAuthTarget(raw)` answers "did this URL ask for somewhere?" — a validated path, or `null` for missing AND for hostile (so a crafted `redirectTo` cannot change which BRANCH the callback takes as a side effect of being rejected). `webPostAuthDestination(raw)` is that or `WEB_POST_AUTH_LANDING` = `/get-started`. Its three consumers are `/auth/callback`, `use-login.ts` and `google-one-tap.tsx`.
- **`safeRedirect`'s `/canvas` default was NOT changed at the time, deliberately.** It is also read by `/onboarding` and `POST /api/onboarding/complete`, which ask "where does the app live", not "where does a sign-in end". Those two answers were about to diverge — `/canvas` was on the retirement plan's RETIRE list and `/get-started` is its replacement — so they got separate functions. ⛔ **The default itself is `/get-started` now** (`POST_AUTH_LANDING_FALLBACK` in `safe-redirect.ts`, pinned equal to `WEB_POST_AUTH_LANDING` by test rather than imported, to avoid a cycle): every current caller passes its own fallback so the old value was unreachable, but a NEW caller would have silently inherited a dead route. The separate-functions split above still stands.
- **PRESENCE OF `redirectTo` IS THE SIGNAL, so `use-login.ts` no longer threads the default into the callback URL.** It used to set `redirectTo=/canvas` on every `/auth/callback?…`, which made a plain signup indistinguishable from a deep link. Now the param is set only when the visitor's own URL carried one. Pinned end to end: a plain `/login` → Google builds `redirect_to=…/auth/callback` (bare), `/login?redirectTo=/invite/tok` builds `…/auth/callback?redirectTo=%2Finvite%2Ftok`.
- **NO WEB SIGN-IN DETOURS THROUGH `/onboarding` ANY MORE — the detour is deleted, not narrowed (F-136, supersedes the plain-signup half of F-130).** It was narrowed here to "only a deep-linked first-run user detours, carrying the link", and Stage B then retired `/onboarding`: the retirement hop replaces the query (`applyTarget`), so the detour deleted the deep link it existed to carry — an invite, a join link, an MCP OAuth consent screen whose client never got its code. **A valid `explicitTarget` now wins regardless of onboarding state, and `/auth/callback` does not read onboarding state at all.** Safe because every target these arrivals name is on the retirement KEEP list (`/oauth/authorize`, `/invite/*`, `/join/*`, `/auth/reset-password`, `/billing/*`) and none of them gates on onboarding; onboarding happens inside the app, which is what the `!isDesktop` guard always meant for app sign-ins, and the SPA carries its own `/onboarding`. A plain signup still lands on `/get-started`, unchanged.
- **THE `/onboarding` RETIREMENT CARRIES A VALIDATED `redirectTo` (F-136).** `retirementRedirect` drops the query on every generic redirect except this one: `/onboarding?redirectTo=<same-origin path>` lands on that path, anything else on `/get-started`. Validation is `explicitPostAuthTarget`, so a hostile value is not a destination; it cannot loop because the value handed back was encoded inside the URL it came from. This is INSURANCE for a URL shape that now has no producer — a 302 in flight across the deploy, a bookmark — not a route. `/canvas` deliberately does not get it: nothing ever put a `redirectTo` on it.
- **THE DESKTOP HANDOFF IS UNTOUCHED AND PINNED.** `?desktop=1` branches before the landing is read, so `/auth/desktop-start` → provider → `/auth/callback?desktop=1&state=…` → `/auth/desktop-handoff?state=…` → `dopl://auth` is byte-identical. `src/app/auth/callback/route.test.ts` is the matrix (plain · first-run · deep link · desktop · hostile · failure).
- **`/get-started` IS AUTH-REQUIRED, AND ITS NAME IS LOAD-BEARING.** It is deliberately NOT in `PUBLIC_ROUTES`, so a signed-out visitor bounces to `/login?redirectTo=/get-started` and returns. It must also not begin with `/download`: that entry is a `startsWith` prefix, so any `/download*` path would be silently public. New top-level routes also go in `RESERVED_WORKSPACE_SLUGS` (`src/config/index.ts`) and `NON_WORKSPACE_ROOTS` (`layout-shell.tsx`) — `download` was missing from the former and was added with it.
- **THE PAGE IS THE RETIREMENT PLAN'S `/retired`.** Same audience (signed in), same message ("Dopl lives on your desktop"), same download. Stage B's `WEBSITE_RETIRED` redirect map should point at `/get-started` rather than mint a second page that would drift from it.
- **THE AUTO-START IS A ZERO-SIZED SAME-ORIGIN IFRAME, NOT `location.assign`.** Both download identically when the asset resolves. They differ when it does not, and it did not during this build (see F-131): a top-level navigation to a GitHub 404 COMMITS, throwing the visitor off the page and taking the install instructions with them; the same response in an iframe is `X-Frame-Options: deny` and the page survives. The frame is not `sandbox`ed (Chrome blocks downloads from sandboxed frames) and not `display:none` (not guaranteed to load). Visible "Not working? Download again" is a real `<a href="/download">` — a user-chosen click is the one navigation allowed to leave.
- **THE PAGE IS TWO FULL-HEIGHT FIELDS, cloned from the reference Samuel supplied** (Wispr Flow's post-login download screen), rebuilt 2026-08-04. Left 43%: brand lockup, a SERIF display heading (`--font-playfair` = Newsreader, the same token the auth wordmarks use — the marketing lockup's own wordmark stays grotesk by `.lp-brand-word`'s explicit rule), three numbered steps, "Not working? Try again."; the reference's bottom-left ornament slot carries the live `role="status"` download line instead, because Dopl's only circular brand object is the app icon already sitting at the top of the same column. Right 57%: the illustration, cropped by the field rather than centred in a box. **BOTH FIELD COLOURS ARE EXISTING TOKENS** — left `--ink` (marketing.css's own black), right `--rail-tile` → `--rail` (globals.css, the app-shell frame, also `--body-bg`): the drawing of the install sits on the colour of the thing being installed. Below 900px they stack, copy first.
- **THE INSTALL ANIMATION IS CSS OVER DIVS, ON ONE CLOCK.** `src/features/get-started/` — ten keyframe tracks sharing `--gs-dur`, in a fixed **640×660** coordinate space scaled as a whole by `--gs-k` (cursor targets are literal px; a percentage layout puts the cursor beside the folder at one width and inside it at another). **`--gs-k` reads the FIELD, not the viewport** — `min(calc(100cqw / 640px), calc(100cqh / 636px))` against `.gs-field`'s `container-type: size`, so the width fit keeps the browser fragment bleeding past the left edge and the height fit keeps the volume window off the fold on a short, wide field; the same one factor is therefore correct in the stacked layout too. The composition has TWO anchors — a browser fragment cropped by the field's top and left edges (its recede beat is **opacity-only**, because a scale would walk its hidden corners back into view) and the volume window in the lower right. `prefers-reduced-motion` gets the LAST FRAME, not a blank box. Scoped under `.lp` so type, ink, hairlines and gutter all come from `marketing.css`; the only values added are the mock macOS chrome greys plus alphas of white for on-dark ink, declared once as `--gs-*`. **Three stylesheets, one per purpose** (page/copy · composition · timeline): `get-started.css`, `install-animation.css`, `install-animation-motion.css`, all imported by `page.tsx`.

## Appendix A — ESLint rules to add

- `import/order` with groups: builtin, external, internal (`@/` alias), parent, sibling, index.
- `no-restricted-imports` forbidding deep feature imports (`@/features/*/server/*` from client components).
- `max-lines` at 500 (warn), 700 (error), with the exceptions from §2.
- `@typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any` error.
- `@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars` error.
- `no-console` warn (allow `console.warn`, `console.error`).

## Appendix B — `CLAUDE.md` pointer

This doc is the source of truth. `CLAUDE.md` should be short and end with:

```
For all code organization, naming, and architectural decisions,
follow docs/ENGINEERING.md. When it conflicts with existing code,
the doc wins and the code is a refactor candidate.
```

### 2026-07-31 — the three HIGH release blockers (H1 auth hold, H2 permission arm, H3 sender binding)

Three defects that composed into one another. All desktop-side unless marked.

**H1 — an auth hold must be a state the whole engine understands.** `session-auth.js` recorded a held session ONLY as `s.authHold`, a field no other module could see, while stamping `s.state.phase/parked/activity` by hand. Two failures followed, and the second was only reachable because of the first:
- `session-reducer.wakeEffects` saw nothing but `parked` and RESUMED held sessions. A peer follow-up on a channel seeded `auto_both` was enough: inbound → auto-accepted → wake → `session-park.resumeParked` spawned the SDK on a Mac with **no Claude Code credential**, and a later sign-in started a SECOND query beside it — two `claude` children for one request, the orphan still holding the session's pre-approved `dopl_channel` access.
- `holdIfAuthFailure`'s "already held" branch returned "handled" having done nothing, so a session the wake had dragged back to `phase:'running'` stayed there forever: no query, no idle timer (`scheduleIdle` only arms on `launched`), nothing that could park or settle it, and a peer awaiting a reply that never came.

The hold is now the reducer event **`auth_hold`** (and `auth_release`), setting `state.authHeld`. **Rules, all tested:** a hold IS a park (same `parkEffects` — `denyPending` fail-closed, then `abortQuery`, `clearIdle`, persist `parked`) and resets both axes and every standing grant, for idle_timeout's reason; `wakeEffects` refuses to resume while `authHeld`; `inboundAutoAccepted` returns false while `authHeld`, so a peer message is HELD beside the sign-in button instead of being pushed onto a dead iterator; `session-gate.decideInbound` refuses an ACCEPT **before** shifting the head, so the message stays queued and the card stays answerable (a DECLINE still works — dropping needs no agent); both events are idempotent. `resumeAfterSignIn` and `runSignIn` are single-flight, and **`session-query.startQuery` now calls `abortInFlight(s)` BEFORE assembling anything** — that is the real backstop for the two-children bug and holds whatever the caller does.

**H2 — a stored permission posture may only apply to a launch a human is approving in that moment.** `startSession` is the single construction site for EVERY spawn shape, and it folded the channel's stored preset in unconditionally via `channel-context.startingModes`. Storage was per-channel, permanent, no TTL, no delete. So one consent card's `bypass`/`auto_both` silently re-armed the channel forever: days later a peer reply → `recreateParkedShell` → wide-open start → auto-accepted inbound → wake → the agent ran with Bash/WebFetch pre-approved and auto-outbound posting, **no card, no click**. Before that change every recreated shell started manual/ask.

The fix is an **arm**, not a setting (`main/channel-prefs.js`), enforced three ways because each closes a different hole: **(1) single use** — `consumePermissionPreset` returns the pair and deletes it in one call; **(2) expiring** — `ARM_TTL_MS` 30 min, and a record with no `at` (a pre-H2 durable preset) is treated as EXPIRED so an upgrade can only ever narrow; **(3) one consumer** — only `trigger.inboundApproved` consumes it, and only when `meta.humanAllowed` is true. `consent-watcher.isHumanAllow` distinguishes `allowed` (a person clicked) from `auto_allowed` (standing server-side trust) and passes the verdict as a **call argument, never stamped on the persisted record** — an authority verdict must not survive a restart and re-authorize a launch from disk. `channel-context.startingModes` is **deleted**: the posture now travels only as an explicit `spec.startModes`, so there is no ambient read for a future spawn shape to inherit by accident. A parked shell refuses a posture even if one is handed in. Deny/expiry clear the arm (`trigger-outcomes.js`). Nothing in the session path ever writes an arm back.

`test/session-preset-start.test.mjs` previously regex-matched reducer SOURCE TEXT and never drove `recreateParkedShell` or `session-park.startResume` — the two paths that actually re-applied the preset — so it could not have failed while the bug was live. It now drives the real construction site and asserts the resulting axes per shape.

**H3 — `main/channel-dir-ipc.js` was renderer-reachable with no sender binding.** All SIX handlers validated their payload and never their caller, while `renderer/preload.js` exposes them on the window that loads REMOTE `usedopl.com` content. `setPermissionPreset` was the HIGH (chained with H2 it was zero-click local execution), but `chooseFolder` (native dialog on demand), `clearFolder`, `getFolderLabel` (leaks a local path fragment), `getPermissionPreset` and `sessions:reopen` were all equally unbound. Every handler now goes through `mainOnly()`, which checks **two** things: the sender is the main window's `webContents`, **and** it is that window's TOP frame — a cross-origin iframe SHARES its host's `webContents`, so identity alone would let embedded content drive all six. Fails closed on a missing/destroyed/unbuilt window, on a `senderFrame` getter that throws (a detached frame), and when `register()` is given no `getMainWindow` accessor at all. The refusal shape deliberately matches an invalid-payload rejection so a hostile page cannot use the difference to probe. `index.js` passes a lazy accessor (the window is built after `register()` and replaced on reopen). A structural test asserts every `ipcMain.handle` in the file is wrapped, so a new op cannot be added unbound.

**§2 splits this required** (all three parents were at or within one line of the 500-line cap): `main/session-query.js` (SDK option assembly + `startQuery`/`consume`/`abortInFlight`, out of `session-engine.js`); `main/session-effects.js` (the reducer's pure effect builders — `gatePhase`, `endedEmit`, `endLifecycle`, `endEffects`, `modesEmit`, `parkEffects` — out of `session-reducer.js`); `main/trigger-outcomes.js` (the no-reply terminal echoes, out of `trigger.js`). `session-reducer.js` requires the effect builders at module scope ABOVE its sentinel, so inside the block they are free vars and the two sentinel blocks CONCATENATE back into the standalone program the extraction tests always evaluated. **`test/_reducer-block.mjs` is now the one place that slices them** — eight test files share it, so the next split costs one edit instead of eight.

### Desktop app — THE RELIABILITY ROUND: cursor ordering, silent terminals, and two fail-open defaults (2026-08-08, CHANNELS-AUDIT C-2…C-11)

Nine audit findings, all on the desktop side. Act on the rules below rather than re-deriving them.

**C-3 — THE PERSISTED CURSOR MOVES ONLY PAST A MESSAGE WHOSE DISPATCH LANDED.** `channel-listener.js` wrote `io.setCursor` (electron-store, i.e. state that survives a restart) BEFORE `messages.dispatchMessage`, so every downstream early return was a permanent silent drop: a 15s network blip during the consent POST meant no consent row, nothing in Pending Requests, and a restart re-awaiting from the ADVANCED cursor while the peer's agent long-polled forever. The per-page loop now lives in **`listener-messages.drainPage`**, beside the dispatch it gates; the transport loop only asks "was the page finished?". `dispatchMessage` returns a **deferral REASON, falsy = handled** — every route is terminal for the message it claims, and only `trigger.handleTrigger` can answer `'no-claude-runtime'` / `'no-consent-row'`. Both of those returns are UPSTREAM of `watcher.register`, and the consent create is de-duped server-side on (operator, channel, kind, seq), so a retry is side-effect-free. **The old ordering was NOT load-bearing** — the one comment that leans on it (`session-park`'s FIX F4) is stale, because `session-history`'s FIX F5 dropped `since` from that read entirely. A held cursor is bounded: `LISTENER.DISPATCH_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (8) over the reconnect ladder ≈ 4 minutes, then a LOUD escape that says the message was dropped. Retrying forever would trade a bounded announced loss for an unbounded silent stall of every later message in that channel.

**C-5 — THE THREE TERMINALS THAT POSTED NOTHING NOW POST A STATUS NOTE.** The 12h abandonment (`endLifecycle('abandoned')` returned null), the Q6 auth-preflight hold (`launch()` answers with a sessionId, so `trigger` takes its success branch and NOTHING reaches the wire), and the window-budget eviction (`settle()` bypasses the reducer). All three now emit **`task_progress` + the reserved `session_ended` marker**, body `session-effects.INACTIVE_NOTE` — the operator-End precedent (P1-7). It is deliberately NOT a terminal kind: `group-thread` treats `task_progress` as an ENTRY and never as an `endEvent`, so it reaches `calmEndStatus` and can never become the shared thread's outcome. **One wording for all three**, because which one it was is a fact about the other operator's machine. The IDLE park still posts nothing (it is a pause; its own abandonment bound speaks if nobody returns). `session-window.onEnded` drops a repeat of the same (thread, cycle) marker — keyed on the echo id, so the local guard and the server's `client_msg_id` dedupe agree by construction.

**C-4 — A LAUNCH THAT NEVER EMITS `system/init` ENDS ITSELF.** The idle timer was armed only by effects requiring `launched`, so a hung child sat at `phase:'launching'` with no watchdog: `hasLiveSession` true, every retry `{skipped:'busy'}`, its slot spent against `MAX_WINDOWS` for the life of the process. `session-state.idleTimeout` now answers `{ms: LAUNCHING_MS, type:'inactive'}` for a launching state — checked FIRST, because `parked` is false and `activity` is `working` there — and **`session-query.startQuery`** arms it (that is the ONE deferred launch, so it covers the post-sign-in relaunch too). `LAUNCHING_MS` is 5 minutes and it is DERIVED: above `mcp-config.MCP_CLIENT_TIMEOUT_MS` (290s), equal to `session-spawner.MAX_RUNTIME_MS`, 5× the `AWAIT_HOLD_MARGIN_MS` this repo already prices "auth + MCP boot + the workspace handshake" at, and a third of `DEFAULT_IDLE_MS`.

**`inactive` is the ONE calm terminal**, shared by the launch watchdog and the eviction. Eviction dispatches it instead of calling `settle()` directly — do not reintroduce a second teardown beside the reducer's.

**C-7 — LOCAL CONSENT EXPIRY ROUTES THROUGH THE SAME RESOLVER AS SERVER EXPIRY.** `MAX_WATCH_MS` equals the server's `CONSENT_TTL_MS` and `createdAt` is stamped after the insert returns, so the local clock ALWAYS wins and `inboundExpired` never ran — leaving a pre-consent window open forever with a live Accept over an expired row, and the operator's armed permission preset armed for the next launch. Routing (not raising the constant) is the fix: one definition of what an expiry does. A belt settles the record if a resolver is missing or throws.

**C-9 — AN ACCEPTED CONSENT ENTRY IS RELEASED ON EVERY TERMINAL THAT IS NOT AN ADOPT.** `decide()` leaves the entry in the registry ON PURPOSE (`takeForAdopt` is the remover), but adoption happens on one branch only; `busy` / `auth-hold` / `cap` / `no-sdk` / `disabled` / `gone` all leaked, `count()` feeds `atWindowCap`, and `evictIdleShell` walks only the SESSION registry — so six leaks capped the desktop permanently. `sessionConsent.release(watcherKey, reason)` (shares `drop()` with `close()`, sends NO `consent_resolved` — the card already said Accepted) is called from `trigger.js` at each terminal. A sweeper was rejected: the caller KNOWS, it is holding the skip reason. `fetched.retry` deliberately does not release.

**C-8 — QUIT NO LONGER ORPHANS `claude` CHILDREN.** `before-quit` set a flag and stopped the listener; every live `sdk.query()` outlived the app still holding pre-approved `dopl_channel` access. **`main/quit-guard.js`** owns the decision: it NAMES the threads a quit would interrupt (title + channel, never a count), offers *Quit anyway* / *Wait for them to finish*, ends each session through `inactive` (C-5's terminal, so the peer is told), and RACES one final `session-state-push.flush()` against `FLUSH_DEADLINE_MS` (1500ms) rather than awaiting a 15s HTTP budget. The wait path quits BY ITSELF and is capped at `DEFAULT_IDLE_MS + 60s` — derived, because a stalled agent is parked by its own TTL and a parked session holds no child. **The kill predicate is "holds a live child" (`!settled && !parked`), NOT the pill state**: a session between turns reads `idle` and still owns a process. Every path fails OPEN — a quit the operator asked for always happens.

**C-11 — AN UNKNOWN TOOL PROFILE FAILS CLOSED, AND SAYS SO.** `normalizeProfile` answered `'full'` for anything unrecognized, `myAgentToolProfile` is `null` for a non-member read / an unrefreshed DTO / any out-of-enum value, and nothing logged it — while `session-park.knownProfile` answered `read_only` one file over. Unknown now resolves to `read_only` and reports through an injected sink (wired to `diag` OUTSIDE the extracted block, lazily, so `tool-profiles.js` stays dependency-free for targeting's harnesses). **An explicit `'full'` is untouched** — the DB column default is `full` and that is correct. `targeting-window.resolveToolProfile` delegates here instead of carrying its own copy.

**C-10 — THE DENY FLOOR IS UNIVERSAL; `full` IS STILL `full`.** `full` granting the operator's REAL tools, global MCP servers included, is the PRODUCT. Nothing narrows it: no `--tools`, no `--allowedTools`, no `--settings`, and **no `--strict-mcp-config` on the `full` path — do not add one.** What changed is that `buildDeniedTools('full')` returns `UNIVERSAL_HARD_DENY` (`DOPL_ADMIN_TOOLS` + `RETIRED_DOPL_TOOLS`) instead of `[]`, matching what the SDK lane has always applied. **The residual C-10 left — `session-profiles.SESSION_HARD_DENY` being broader — was CLOSED on 2026-08-08 by F-177, from the other side: the SDK lane came down to the floor rather than the headless lane going up. See "`full` MEANS FULL" below.**

**C-2 — AD-HOC SESSIONS ARE FILTERED CLIENT-SIDE, and the server contract is unchanged.** An unthreaded inbound keys as `task-<channel>-<seq>`, which `SESSION_KEY_RE` and `threadId: z.string().uuid()` both refuse; zod validates the ARRAY, so one such row 400s the whole payload, `retryable(400)` is false, the digest is never recorded, and `read_sessions` answers `[]` for the machine INCLUDING its valid sessions. `session-state-push.reportable()` drops them before the grouping (so the digest, the empty-set delete and the retry all operate on what really goes on the wire) and logs once per session key. An ad-hoc session has no thread for `read_sessions` to be about.

**§2 splits this required:** `main/consent-store.js` (the watcher's two persisted maps + the durable projection + the settled TTL, out of `consent-watcher.js`); `main/quit-guard.js` (out of `index.js`); `test/tool-profile-defaults.test.mjs` and `test/ui-sync-replica-identity.test.mjs`. `session-reducer.js`, `session-engine.js`, `ui-sync.js` and `session-profiles.js` all sit at EXACTLY 500 — the next line added to any of them is a split.

**F-108 paid off once:** `thread-followup-predicate.test.mjs`'s "route 6 runs after classify" seam was a regex over shipped source text and went red on C-3's refactor without the invariant changing. It is asserted behaviourally now (the harness already ran the real `dispatchMessage`), which also catches a route that returns true and raises consent anyway — a shape the regex could not see.

### Desktop app — `full` MEANS FULL: the two spawn lanes now answer the profile identically (2026-08-08, F-177)

**THE DECISION (Samuel's, reversing FIX H2/H3 for this profile).** A `full` channel session SHOULD be able to use the delegation / outbound / persistence / escalation built-ins. Act on this rule, do not re-derive the old split.

**THE RULE.** `session-profiles.SESSION_HARD_DENY` **is** `tool-profiles.UNIVERSAL_HARD_DENY` — literally `UNIVERSAL_HARD_DENY.slice()`, not a copy and not a superset. So under `full`, BOTH lanes hard-deny exactly eight names: the four `dopl_*_admin` tools and the four `RETIRED_DOPL_TOOLS`. Everything else exists and is governed by the operator's permission preset.

**WHAT CAME OFF the SDK lane's `full` (25 built-ins, all now `gate`):** `Task`, `Agent`, `TaskCreate`, `TaskUpdate`, `TaskStop`, `TaskGet`, `TaskList`, `TaskOutput`, `Artifact`, `SendMessage`, `SendUserMessage`, `PushNotification`, `RemoteTrigger`, `ReportFindings`, `DesignSync`, `CronCreate`, `CronDelete`, `CronList`, `ScheduleWakeup`, `Monitor`, `EnterWorktree`, `ExitWorktree`, `Workflow`, `Skill`, `ToolSearch`.

**WHY THE OLD SPLIT WAS NOT A BOUNDARY.** It rested on "these tools outlive the watched window". But `Bash` was live-gated under `full` the whole time, and anyone with Bash has `curl`, `launchd` and a child that outlives the window. A table that hands over the shell and then refuses `SendMessage` is keeping a list, not drawing a line. **`full` gets its supervision from the OPERATOR'S PERMISSION PRESET (Axis A), not from the tool table.**

**WHAT REPLACES IT, and why this is a widening rather than a loss of control.** `AUTO_TOOLS` and `BYPASS_TOOLS` are POSITIVE allow-lists (v2.9 FIX F3: an unclassified name gates in every mode, `bypass` included). No released name is on either, and none is in `preApproved`, so **every one of them stops on an operator button in every posture** — they are allowed to EXIST, not allowed to run. **Nothing was moved into `preApproved`**; do not "finish the job" by pre-approving any of them, because a shadowed tool never reaches `canUseTool` at all.

**`read_only` and `dopl_only` DID NOT CHANGE.** They still hard-deny the whole of `DENIED_BUILTINS` — `Task`/`Agent` included, because there a subagent really does escape the bound — plus the admins and retired tools. F-177 is a `full`-only change. The `DOPL_WRITE_TOOLS` split (FIX F2) is untouched: the workspace-write dopl tools still gate under `auto` and only `bypass` covers them.

**THE `ToolSearch` FINDING, and the config change it forced.** Verified against the bundled runtime (claude 2.1.220 / SDK 0.3.220), not inferred: **every MCP tool is deferred behind `ToolSearch` once tool search is on** (`isDeferredTool`: `if (isMcp) return true`), tool search **defaults to on** (no `ENABLE_TOOL_SEARCH` in the env resolves to mode `tst`; `buildScrubbedEnv` strips only the permission knobs), and the per-server `tools` policy does **not** exempt anything — it is a PERMISSION policy (`{name, permission_policy}`), which is what `prompt-framing`'s FIX F3b comment wrongly assumed it was. The runtime fails SAFE when `ToolSearch` is absent from the offered set ("Tool search disabled: ToolSearchTool is not available (may have been disallowed via disallowedTools)"), which is the **only** reason `mcp__dopl__dopl_channel` was eagerly present before F-177. Releasing `ToolSearch` under `full` would therefore have DEFERRED the session's own delivery path behind a call the prompt forbids by name and the gate stops on a button — the F3 incident verbatim. **`main/sdk-loader.js`'s dopl MCP entry now carries `alwaysLoad: true`**, which makes `ToolSearch`'s presence irrelevant to reaching the dopl tools and additionally guarantees they are present when the turn-1 prompt is built (MCP startup is otherwise non-blocking; `alwaysLoad` blocks it, capped by the CLI at 5s). **Do not remove that field** — `test/prompt-profile-drift.test.mjs` pins it.

**⚠ THE `doplToolsPolicy` HALF OF THAT FINDING WAS UNDERSTATED, AND THE SHARPER FACT IS A SHIPPED OUTAGE (2026-08-08 follow-up).** The line above says the per-server `tools` policy "does not exempt anything", which reads as *inert but harmless*. **It was destructive.** `sdk-loader.js` was assigning `server.tools = doplToolsPolicy` — a bare `string[]` of short dopl names (`['dopl_channel']` for `read_only`). The CLI validates `--mcp-config` **PER ENTRY** with zod, and a failed `safeParse` **does not strip the offending field — it DROPS THE WHOLE SERVER ENTRY** and continues (`Skipped — invalid MCP server config for "dopl"`). Observed end-to-end against the bundled binary, reading the init message's `mcp_servers`:

| `tools` value | resulting `mcp_servers` |
|---|---|
| `['dopl_channel']` (what we shipped) | `[]` — **the dopl entry is GONE** |
| `[{ name: 'dopl_channel' }]` | `[{"name":"dopl",…}]` |
| omitted | `[{"name":"dopl",…}]` |

**So every `read_only` and every `dopl_only` SDK session launched with NO dopl server at all** — no `dopl_channel`, meaning **no delivery path**: the restricted profiles are exactly the ones whose entire purpose is "this agent may talk on the channel and little else", and they shipped unable to talk. It also made the `alwaysLoad` guarantee above moot for the two profiles that carried a policy. `full` passes `null` and was never affected, which is why nothing about the flagship path looked wrong.

**THE FIELD IS NO LONGER SENT AT ALL.** Removed rather than converted: the semantics the line wanted is a VISIBILITY allowlist, and that cannot be expressed by a permission policy — inventing one on a permission surface is the worse error. **Nothing is lost, because SDK `disallowedTools` already carries the identical bound**: its complement over the server's real surface IS the old allowlist (`read_only` denies `DOPL_SAFE_TOOLS` + the admins + the retired tools, leaving `dopl_channel`; `dopl_only` denies the admins + retired, leaving the safe tools + the channel), and everything surviving that still stops at `canUseTool`, which fails closed. `doplToolsPolicy` stays in `buildMcpServers`' SIGNATURE and in the profile table so `session-query`'s call shape is untouched — **it is deliberately unread, a record of intent that is deletion-only, never a live input.** `test/mcp-server-tools-policy.test.mjs` pins that it is never sent AND joins the deny lists to prove the bound is carried; one of its assertions is written to FAIL the day the SDK starts accepting a `string[]`, so a future runtime that makes the old shape legal announces itself instead of silently re-enabling a dead idea.

**WHY IT WENT UNDETECTED IS THE REUSABLE PART.** Nothing asserted the shape the SDK was HANDED — the tests checked what `buildSessionToolConfig` returned, which was correct, and stopped at the boundary. And the CLI's rejection is a warning on a stream nothing reads, so the loudest signal the system produced went nowhere. **A config field is not verified by testing the function that computes it; it is verified by asserting the object that crosses the process boundary, or by reading back what the runtime says it loaded.**

**WHAT THE `ToolSearch` FINDING IS NOT.** It is not evidence that `full` was failing the "your connected apps" promise. It failed SAFE, not closed: with `ToolSearch` denied, deferral was off and EVERY MCP tool arrived in the turn-1 prompt in full. What that cost was CONTEXT and the tool-search feature — not reachability. **Note the SDK lane does NOT set `strictMcpConfig`** (and must not: see C-10's rule for the headless twin), so by the SDK's own definition of that flag the session is not fenced to the dopl server and the operator's other MCP configurations load. Releasing `ToolSearch` under `full` therefore turns deferral back on for those connectors, which is the design working — schemas loaded on demand behind one gated call, instead of every connector's full schema pushed into turn 1. **The dopl server is exempted from that by `alwaysLoad`, deliberately: it is the session's reason for existing.** (Not empirically re-verified: exactly which of the operator's on-disk servers survive `settingSources: []`, which removes the settings-file tier by a different mechanism than `strictMcpConfig`.)

**RESIDUAL, stated so it is not re-derived as handled.** A `Task`/`Agent` call approved by the operator spawns a subagent that does not inherit this session's `canUseTool` bound, so that one click is an approval of everything the subagent then does. That is the H3 argument, and it is now answered by the click rather than by the table — deliberately, because the same session can already run `Bash`.

## 2026-08-15 — Comment-cleanup harvest: rationale relocated from code comments

A repo-wide comment sweep ran on **2026-08-15** across 18 scopes (`src/features/*`, `src/shared/*`, `src/app`, `apps/desktop-ui`, `packages/dopl-client`, `packages/mcp-server`, `dopl-desktop-app`). Its rubric deletes decorative and restating comments outright, and **relocates** the ones that carry a decision, a rejected alternative, or an incident. This section is where the relocated half landed — it is the *why* that used to sit above the code, not new guidance, and nothing here overrides `docs/INVARIANTS.md` or the code (precedence: code > INVARIANTS > ENGINEERING).

**65 entries**, harvested 2026-08-15 from 67 candidates (two pairs told the same story from adjacent scopes and were merged).
